  - Example: `저는 비빔밥을 먹어요.` — *I eat bibimbap.* [저 (I) + 는 (topic) + 비빔밥 (bibimbap) + 을 (object) + 먹어요 (eat)]
  - Example: `민수 씨는 녹차를 마셔요.` — *Minsu drinks green tea.* [민수 씨는 + 녹차 (green tea) + 를 (object) + 마셔요 (drinks)]
- [ ] **Grammar Pattern: `Noun 1 + 하고 / 와 / 과 + Noun 2`** (Noun 1 and Noun 2 (listing items))
  - Formula: `Conversational: Noun 1 + 하고 + Noun 2 | Formal: Consonant + 과, Vowel + 와`
  - Explanation: -하고 is the universal spoken particle for "and" connecting nouns. -와/과 is slightly more formal/literary.
  - Rule: 하고 attaches identically to consonants and vowels: 밥하고 고기, 사과하고 바나나.
  - Rule: 와/과 selects by final sound: 김치(vowel) + 와 -> 김치와; 밥(consonant) + 과 -> 밥과.
  - Example: `불고기하고 냉면을 주문해요.` — *We order bulgogi and cold noodles.* [불고기 + 하고 (and) + 냉면 (cold noodles) + 을 (object) + 주문해요 (order)]
  - Example: `책과 공책이 책상에 있어요.` — *There are books and notebooks on the desk.* [책 (book) + 과 (and) + 공책 (notebook) + 이 (subject) + 책상에 있어요]

#### Module 5.2.4 — 말하기·듣기 (Shadowing & Drills)

- [ ] **Shadowing & Repetition Drill:** Say the orders out loud with confidence using 을/를 and 하고.
  - [ ] Prompt: "Call the server and order 1 bibimbap." → Speak aloud: `여기요! 비빔밥 하나 주세요.` (*Over here! Please give me one bibimbap.*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "Order 2 servings of bulgogi and 1 cola." → Speak aloud: `불고기 이 인분하고 콜라 하나 주세요.` (*Please give us two servings of bulgogi and one cola.*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "State: I eat kimchi stew and rice." → Speak aloud: `저는 김치찌개하고 밥을 먹어요.` (*I eat kimchi stew and rice.*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "Ask: Please give us cold water too." → Speak aloud: `시원한 물도 주세요.` (*Please give us cold water too.*)

#### Module 5.2.5 — 읽기·쓰기 (Reading & Production)

- [ ] **Reading Passage:** "인기 한식당 차림표 (Popular Korean Restaurant Menu)"
  - Text: `행복식당 차림표 (메뉴) - 돌솥비빔밥: 8,000원 - 김치찌개 (공깃밥 포함): 7,500원 - 불고기 백반: 9,000원 - 해물파전: 12,000원 - 음료수 (콜라/사이다): 2,000원 * 물과 추가 반찬은 셀프입니다. 맛있게 드세요!`
  - Translation: *Happiness Restaurant Menu - Hot Stone Bibimbap: 8,000 KRW - Kimchi Stew (steamed rice included): 7,500 KRW - Bulgogi Set Meal: 9,000 KRW - Seafood Scallion Pancake: 12,000 KRW - Soft Drinks (Cola/Cider): 2,000 KRW * Water and extra side dishes are self-service. Bon appetit!*
  - [ ] Check: Is steamed rice included with the Kimchi Stew? → Answer: `Yes (공깃밥 포함)` (*김치찌개 (공깃밥 포함): 7,500원*)
  - [ ] Check: How much does the Seafood Scallion Pancake cost? → Answer: `12,000원` (*해물파전: 12,000원*)
- [ ] **Guided Writing Task:** Write a 6-sentence restaurant interaction where you call the server, order 2 main dishes and a drink, and ask for water.
  - Sample Model Answer: `손님: 저기요! 주문할게요. 직원: 네, 말씀하세요! 손님: 비빔밥 하나하고 된장찌개 하나 주세요. 직원: 마실 것은 필요 없으세요? 손님: 콜라 한 캔도 주세요. 손님: 그리고 시원한 물 좀 주세요. 감사합니다.`


## Unit 6 — Shopping and Prices (쇼핑과 가격)

**Part.** Part B — Handle Everyday Needs (Units 4–8 & Checkpoint B)
**Objective.** Inquire about prices using 얼마예요, understand large Sino-Korean numbers (십, 백, 천, 만), and use appropriate classifiers (개, 병, 권, 벌, 켤레).

### 6.1 — Asking the Price of an Item (얼마예요? 와 가격 묻기)

**Situation & Objective.** Ask prices with 얼마예요 and master Sino-Korean numbers for Korean currency (원).

#### Module 6.1.1 — 대화 (Conversation First)

- [ ] **Dialogue Practice:** Listen to and practice the dialogue between David (데이비드) and Clerk (문구점 사장님) and David (데이비드) and Clerk (문구점 사장님) and David (데이비드).
  - `사장님, 이 한국어 연습장 얼마예요?` (Owner, how much is this Korean practice notebook?) — *Note: 사장님 = Polite title for shopkeepers/business owners.*
  - `그 공책은 삼천오백 원이에요.` (That notebook is 3,500 Won.) — *Note: 3,500 = 삼천 (3,000) + 오백 (500).*
  - `그러면 저 모나미 볼펜은 얼마예요?` (Then how much is that Monami ballpoint pen over there?) — *Note: 저 = "that over there (far from both)".*
  - `볼펜은 천 원이에요. 아주 잘 써져요.` (The ballpoint pen is 1,000 Won. It writes very well.) — *Note: 천 = 1,000 (not 일천; prefix 1 is omitted for thousands).*
  - `공책 두 권하고 볼펜 세 자루 주세요. 여기 만 원 있어요.` (Please give me two notebooks and three pens. Here is 10,000 Won.) — *Note: 권 = counter for books/notebooks; 자루 = counter for pens/writing instruments.*
- [ ] **Cultural Etiquette & Pragmatics:** In Korea, the currency is the Won (원). Prices utilize Sino-Korean numbers: 일(1), 이(2), 삼(3), 사(4), 오(5), 육(6), 칠(7), 팔(8), 구(9), 십(10), 백(100), 천(1,000), 만(10,000). Crucially, Korean numbers group in units of four zeros (만 = 10,000), so 50,000 Won is 오만 원.

#### Module 6.1.2 — 어휘 (Vocabulary in Context)

- [ ] **Core Target Words:** Memorise and pronounce all target words for this lesson:
  - [ ] `얼마` (eolma) [Noun]: how much (price/amount) — e.g., "이 사과 얼마예요?" (*How much is this apple?*)
  - [ ] `원` (won, Hanja: 圓) [Counter]: Korean Won (currency unit) — e.g., "오천 원입니다." (*It is 5,000 Won.*)
  - [ ] `백 / 천 / 만` (baek / cheon / man, Hanja: 百 / 千 / 萬) [Noun]: hundred (100) / thousand (1,000) / ten thousand (10,000) — e.g., "이 티셔츠는 이만오천 원이에요." (*This T-shirt is 25,000 Won.*)
  - [ ] `옷` (ot) [Noun]: clothes / clothing — e.g., "백화점에서 예쁜 옷을 사요." (*I buy pretty clothes at the department store.*)
  - [ ] `비싸다 / 비싸요` (bissada / bissayo) [Adjective]: to be expensive — e.g., "이 가방은 너무 비싸요." (*This bag is too expensive.*)
  - [ ] `싸다 / 싸요` (ssada / ssayo) [Adjective]: to be cheap / inexpensive — e.g., "시장은 물건이 아주 싸요." (*Goods are very cheap at the traditional market.*)

#### Module 6.1.3 — 문법 (Grammar Formulas & Rules)

- [ ] **Grammar Pattern: `Noun + 은/는 얼마예요?`** (How much is [Noun]?)
  - Formula: `[Item Noun] + 은/는 + 얼마예요?`
  - Explanation: The universal formula to ask the price of any product or service.
  - Rule: Attach 은 if the noun ends in a consonant: 이 옷은 얼마예요?
  - Rule: Attach 는 if the noun ends in a vowel: 이 모자는 얼마예요?
  - Example: `이 청바지는 얼마예요?` — *How much are these jeans?* [이 (this) + 청바지 (jeans) + 는 (topic) + 얼마예요? (how much is it?)]
  - Example: `사과 한 상자는 얼마예요?` — *How much is one box of apples?* [사과 (apple) + 한 상자 (one box) + 는 + 얼마예요?]
- [ ] **Grammar Pattern: `Sino-Korean Currency Reading: [Number] + 원이에요/예요`** (It is [X] Won)
  - Formula: `[Sino-Korean Number] + 원 + 이에요 (ends in consonant) / 예요`
  - Explanation: Because 원 ends in the consonant ㄴ, it always takes 이에요: 원이에요 (pronounced 워니에요 through liaison). Notice 1,000 is 천 원 (never 일천 원) and 10,000 is 만 원 (never 일만 원).
  - Rule: 1,500 KRW = 천오백 원이에요
  - Rule: 12,000 KRW = 만 이천 원이에요
  - Rule: 45,000 KRW = 사만 오천 원이에요
  - Example: `모두 합해서 이만 팔천 원이에요.` — *In total it is 28,000 Won.* [모두 합해서 (all together) + 이만 팔천 (28,000) + 원이에요 (it is Won)]

#### Module 6.1.4 — 말하기·듣기 (Shadowing & Drills)

- [ ] **Shadowing & Repetition Drill:** Practice asking prices and reading Korean Won amounts without hesitation.
  - [ ] Prompt: "Ask: How much is this hat?" → Speak aloud: `이 모자는 얼마예요?` (*How much is this hat?*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "Say: It is 15,000 Won." → Speak aloud: `만 오천 원이에요.` (*It is 15,000 Won.*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "Ask: How much is that jacket over there?" → Speak aloud: `저 재킷은 얼마예요?` (*How much is that jacket over there?*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "Say: It is 89,000 Won." → Speak aloud: `팔만 구천 원이에요.` (*It is 89,000 Won.*)

#### Module 6.1.5 — 읽기·쓰기 (Reading & Production)

- [ ] **Reading Passage:** "동대문 패션몰 가격표 (Dongdaemun Price Tags)"
  - Text: `동대문 쇼핑몰 세일 행사! - 흰색 면 티셔츠: 10,000원 (만 원) - 청바지: 35,000원 (삼만 오천 원) - 겨울 털모자: 12,000원 (만 이천 원) - 양말 5켤레 묶음: 5,000원 (오천 원) * 카드 결제 및 현금 결제 모두 가능합니다.`
  - Translation: *Dongdaemun Shopping Mall Sale Event! - White Cotton T-shirt: 10,000 KRW (man won) - Blue Jeans: 35,000 KRW (sam-man o-cheon won) - Winter Beanie Hat: 12,000 KRW (man i-cheon won) - 5-pair bundle of socks: 5,000 KRW (o-cheon won) * Both card and cash payments accepted.*
  - [ ] Check: How much are the blue jeans in Korean? → Answer: `삼만 오천 원 (35,000 KRW)` (*청바지: 35,000원 (삼만 오천 원)*)
  - [ ] Check: How much does the 5-pair bundle of socks cost? → Answer: `5,000원 (오천 원)` (*양말 5켤레 묶음: 5,000원*)
- [ ] **Guided Writing Task:** Create a personal shopping budget list of 4 items you want to buy with item names and prices written out in Korean words.
  - Sample Model Answer: `1. 한국어 책: 만 오천 원 2. 필통: 육천 원 3. 볼펜 두 개: 이천 원 4. 커피 한 잔: 사천오백 원 총합계는 이만 칠천오백 원이에요.`

### 6.2 — Buying Multiple Items and Counters (물건 여러 개 사기와 단위 명사)

**Situation & Objective.** Count diverse items using specific Korean classifiers: 권 (books), 벌 (clothes), 켤레 (shoes/socks), 병 (bottles), and 마리 (animals).

#### Module 6.2.1 — 대화 (Conversation First)

- [ ] **Dialogue Practice:** Listen to and practice the dialogue between Clerk (점원) and Sarah (사라) and Clerk (점원) and Sarah (사라) and Clerk (점원).
  - `손님, 어떤 옷을 찾으세요?` (Customer, what kind of clothes are you looking for?) — *Note: 어떤 = "what kind of".*
  - `이 검은색 바지하고 흰색 셔츠를 보고 싶어요.` (I want to see these black trousers and white shirt.) — *Note: 검은색 = black color; 흰색 = white color.*
  - `여기 사이즈별로 다 있어요. 한번 입어 보세요.` (We have all of them by size here. Please try them on.) — *Note: 입어 보세요 = "Please try it on".*
  - `정말 마음에 들어요! 바지 한 벌하고 셔츠 두 벌 주세요.` (I really like them! Please give me one pair of trousers and two shirts.) — *Note: 벌 = counter for suits/sets of clothing; 마음에 들어요 = "I like it / it suits my heart".*
  - `네! 양말 두 켤레도 서비스로 드릴게요.` (Yes! I will also give you two pairs of socks as a complimentary bonus service.) — *Note: 켤레 = counter for footwear/socks; 서비스 = complimentary store gift.*
- [ ] **Cultural Etiquette & Pragmatics:** Korean uses specialized noun classifiers (단위 명사) matched to the physical nature of the object. While 개 is a universal general counter for inanimate items, polite and natural Korean requires specific counters for clothing (벌), books (권), bottles (병), paper sheets (장), and footwear pairs (켤레). Additionally, Korean merchants frequently offer "서비스" (small complimentary gifts or discounts) for good customers.

#### Module 6.2.2 — 어휘 (Vocabulary in Context)

- [ ] **Core Target Words:** Memorise and pronounce all target words for this lesson:
  - [ ] `권` (gwon, Hanja: 卷) [Counter]: counter for bound volumes (books, magazines, notebooks) — e.g., "소설책 세 권을 빌렸어요." (*I borrowed three novels.*)
  - [ ] `벌` (beol) [Counter]: counter for suits of clothing (shirts, coats, dresses, trousers) — e.g., "정장 한 벌을 샀어요." (*I bought one suit.*)
  - [ ] `켤레` (kyeolle) [Counter]: counter for footwear pairs (shoes, socks, gloves) — e.g., "운동화 한 켤레에 오만 원이에요." (*One pair of sneakers is 50,000 Won.*)
  - [ ] `병` (byeong, Hanja: 甁) [Counter]: counter for bottles — e.g., "생수 세 병을 샀어요." (*I bought three bottles of mineral water.*)
  - [ ] `마리` (mari) [Counter]: counter for animals, fish, birds — e.g., "강아지 한 마리와 고양이 두 마리가 있어요." (*There is one puppy and two cats.*)
  - [ ] `장` (jang, Hanja: 張) [Counter]: counter for flat sheets of paper, tickets, photos — e.g., "영화표 두 장 주세요." (*Please give me two movie tickets.*)

#### Module 6.2.3 — 문법 (Grammar Formulas & Rules)

- [ ] **Grammar Pattern: `Noun + Native Number Modifier + Specific Counter`** (Accurate classifier matching in Korean)
  - Formula: `[Noun] + [한 / 두 / 세 / 네 / 다섯...] + [권 / 벌 / 켤레 / 병 / 장 / 마리]`
  - Explanation: Always remember to use the shortened Native Korean number stems (한, 두, 세, 네, 스무) directly preceding the classifier noun.
  - Rule: Books: 책 한 권, 책 두 권, 책 세 권
  - Rule: Clothes: 옷 한 벌, 셔츠 두 벌
  - Rule: Shoes: 구두 한 켤레, 양말 세 켤레
  - Rule: Bottles: 콜라 한 병, 맥주 두 병
  - Rule: Sheets: 종이 한 장, 티켓 두 장
  - Example: `서점에서 소설책 두 권을 샀어요.` — *I bought two novels at the bookstore.* [서점에서 (at bookstore) + 소설책 (novel) + 두 권 (2 volumes) + 을 + 샀어요]
  - Example: `시장에서 사과 다섯 개와 생수 두 병을 샀어요.` — *I bought five apples and two bottles of water at the market.* [사과 다섯 개 (5 apples) + 와 (and) + 생수 두 병 (2 water bottles)]

#### Module 6.2.4 — 말하기·듣기 (Shadowing & Drills)

- [ ] **Shadowing & Repetition Drill:** Replace the general counter 개 with the exact Korean classifier word.
  - [ ] Prompt: "Buy 2 shirts (clothes counter: 벌)." → Speak aloud: `셔츠 두 벌 주세요.` (*Please give me two shirts.*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "Buy 1 pair of shoes (pair counter: 켤레)." → Speak aloud: `운동화 한 켤레 주세요.` (*Please give me one pair of sneakers.*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "Buy 3 Korean books (book counter: 권)." → Speak aloud: `한국어 책 세 권 주세요.` (*Please give me three Korean books.*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "Buy 2 bottles of water (bottle counter: 병)." → Speak aloud: `생수 두 병 주세요.` (*Please give me two bottles of mineral water.*)

#### Module 6.2.5 — 읽기·쓰기 (Reading & Production)

- [ ] **Reading Passage:** "주말 벼룩시장 쇼핑 일기 (Flea Market Shopping Note)"
  - Text: `토요일에 홍대 벼룩시장에 갔어요. 시장에 사람이 아주 많았어요. 저는 빈티지 모자 하나와 따뜻한 스웨터 한 벌을 샀어요. 가격도 삼만 원으로 아주 쌌어요. 친구는 귀여운 인형 두 개와 재미있는 만화책 세 권을 샀어요. 정말 즐거웠어요.`
  - Translation: *On Saturday, I went to the Hongdae flea market. There were very many people at the market. I bought one vintage hat and one warm sweater. The price was also very cheap at 30,000 Won. My friend bought two cute dolls and three interesting comic books. It was really enjoyable.*
  - [ ] Check: What clothing item did the writer buy? → Answer: `스웨터 한 벌 (One sweater)` (*따뜻한 스웨터 한 벌을 샀어요.*)
  - [ ] Check: How many comic books did the writer's friend buy? → Answer: `세 권 (3 volumes)` (*재미있는 만화책 세 권을 샀어요.*)
- [ ] **Guided Writing Task:** Write a 5-sentence paragraph describing what you purchased at a store or market, specifying at least 3 items with their exact counters.
  - Sample Model Answer: `저는 어제 백화점에서 쇼핑을 했어요. 따뜻한 셔츠 한 벌을 샀어요. 운동화 한 켤레도 샀어요. 그리고 재미있는 한국어 소설책 두 권을 샀어요. 모두 합해서 팔만 오천 원이었어요.`


## Unit 7 — Daily Activities (일상생활과 동사)

**Part.** Part B — Handle Everyday Needs (Units 4–8 & Checkpoint B)
**Objective.** Conjugate basic Korean verbs in the informal polite present tense (아요/어요/해요) and clearly differentiate destination particle 에 from dynamic action particle 에서.

### 7.1 — Talking About Daily Actions (한국어 공부해요: 아요/어요/해요)

**Situation & Objective.** Master present tense verb conjugation based on stem vowel harmony (bright vowels ㅏ/ㅗ vs dark vowels ㅓ/ㅜ/ㅡ/ㅣ and 하다 verbs).

#### Module 7.1.1 — 대화 (Conversation First)

- [ ] **Dialogue Practice:** Listen to and practice the dialogue between Min-su (민수) and Sarah (사라) and Min-su (민수) and Sarah (사라) and Min-su (민수).
  - `사라 씨, 지금 뭐 해요?` (Sarah, what are you doing right now?) — *Note: 뭐 해요? = "What are you doing?"*
  - `방에서 한국어 단어를 공부해요. 민수 씨는요?` (I am studying Korean vocabulary in my room. How about you, Min-su?) — *Note: 공부해요 = 공부하다 conjugated; 민수 씨는요? = "How about Min-su?"*
  - `저는 거실에서 음악을 들어요. 그리고 커피를 마셔요.` (I am listening to music in the living room. And I am drinking coffee.) — *Note: 들어요 = 듣다 irregular verb (ㄷ -> ㄹ); 마셔요 = 마시다 (ㅣ + 어 -> 여).*
  - `오늘 저녁에 같이 밥 먹어요!` (Let's eat dinner together this evening!) — *Note: 밥 먹어요 = "Let's eat / I eat rice/meal".*
  - `좋아요! 여섯 시에 학생식당에서 만나요.` (Sounds great! Let's meet at 6 o'clock at the student cafeteria.) — *Note: 만나요 = 만나다 (ㅏ + 아 -> 아).*
- [ ] **Cultural Etiquette & Pragmatics:** The informal polite ending -아요/어요/해요 (해요체) is the most versatile and widespread speech style in modern Korea. It serves equally as declarative ("I study"), interrogative ("Do you study?"), imperative ("Please study"), and propositive ("Let's study"), distinguished entirely by conversational intonation.

#### Module 7.1.2 — 어휘 (Vocabulary in Context)

- [ ] **Core Target Words:** Memorise and pronounce all target words for this lesson:
  - [ ] `공부하다 / 공부해요` (gongbuhada / gongbuhaeyo, Hanja: 工夫) [Verb]: to study — e.g., "매일 한국어를 세 시간 공부해요." (*I study Korean for three hours every day.*)
  - [ ] `먹다 / 먹어요` (meokda / meogeoyo) [Verb]: to eat — e.g., "아침에 사과를 먹어요." (*I eat an apple in the morning.*)
  - [ ] `마시다 / 마셔요` (masida / masyeoyo) [Verb]: to drink — e.g., "시원한 물을 마셔요." (*I drink cold water.*)
  - [ ] `자다 / 자요` (jada / jayo) [Verb]: to sleep — e.g., "밤 열한 시에 자요." (*I sleep at 11 o'clock at night.*)
  - [ ] `읽다 / 읽어요` (ikda / ilgeoyo) [Verb]: to read — e.g., "도서관에서 책을 읽어요." (*I read a book at the library.*)
  - [ ] `일하다 / 일해요` (ilhada / ilhaeyo) [Verb]: to work — e.g., "회사에서 열심히 일해요." (*I work diligently at the company.*)
  - [ ] `만나다 / 만나요` (mannada / mannayo) [Verb]: to meet — e.g., "주말에 친구를 만나요." (*I meet a friend on the weekend.*)

#### Module 7.1.3 — 문법 (Grammar Formulas & Rules)

- [ ] **Grammar Pattern: `Verb Stem + 아요 / 어요 / 해요 (Present Polite Informal)`** (do / does / am doing)
  - Formula: `Stem vowel ㅏ, ㅗ -> + 아요 | Other vowels (ㅓ, ㅜ, ㅡ, ㅣ) -> + 어요 | -하다 -> 해요`
  - Explanation: To conjugate a Korean verb into the polite informal style (해요체), drop 다 from the dictionary form and inspect the last vowel of the stem.
  - Rule: 1. Bright Vowel (ㅏ, ㅗ): Attach -아요.    - 가다 -> 가 + 아요 = 가요 (contracted)    - 보다 -> 보 + 아요 = 봐요 (contracted)    - 살다 -> 살 + 아요 = 살아요
  - Rule: 2. Dark/Neutral Vowel (ㅓ, ㅜ, ㅡ, ㅣ): Attach -어요.    - 먹다 -> 먹 + 어요 = 먹어요    - 마시다 -> 마시 + 어요 = 마셔요 (contracted)    - 쓰다 -> 쓰 + 어요 = 써요 (ㅡ deleted)
  - Rule: 3. -하다 Verbs: Always become 해요.    - 공부하다 -> 공부해요    - 운동하다 -> 운동해요    - 일하다 -> 일해요
  - Example: `저는 매일 도서관에서 책을 읽어요.` — *I read books at the library every day.* [읽 (stem ending in ㅣ) + 어요 -> 읽어요]
  - Example: `친구와 함께 공원에서 운동해요.` — *I exercise in the park together with a friend.* [운동하 (하다 stem) -> 운동해요]

#### Module 7.1.4 — 말하기·듣기 (Shadowing & Drills)

- [ ] **Shadowing & Repetition Drill:** Conjugate each dictionary form verb into the polite present tense ending.
  - [ ] Prompt: "Conjugate: 보다 (to see/watch) -> [I watch television]." → Speak aloud: `텔레비전을 봐요.` (*I watch television.*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "Conjugate: 마시다 (to drink) -> [I drink water]." → Speak aloud: `물을 마셔요.` (*I drink water.*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "Conjugate: 먹다 (to eat) -> [I eat breakfast]." → Speak aloud: `아침밥을 먹어요.` (*I eat breakfast.*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "Conjugate: 공부하다 (to study) -> [I study Korean]." → Speak aloud: `한국어를 공부해요.` (*I study Korean.*)

#### Module 7.1.5 — 읽기·쓰기 (Reading & Production)

- [ ] **Reading Passage:** "사라의 하루 일과 (Sarah's Daily Routine)"
  - Text: `저는 아침 일곱 시에 일어나요. 먼저 물을 한 잔 마셔요. 그리고 여덟 시에 아침밥을 먹어요. 오전 아홉 시에 대학교에 가요. 교실에서 한국어를 열심히 공부해요. 오후 한 시에 친구와 함께 점심을 먹어요. 저녁에는 방에서 음악을 듣고 숙제를 해요.`
  - Translation: *I wake up at 7 o'clock in the morning. First, I drink a glass of water. And at 8 o'clock, I eat breakfast. At 9 AM, I go to university. In the classroom, I study Korean diligently. At 1 PM, I eat lunch together with a friend. In the evening, I listen to music and do homework in my room.*
  - [ ] Check: What time does Sarah wake up? → Answer: `아침 7시 (7 AM)` (*아침 일곱 시에 일어나요.*)
  - [ ] Check: What does Sarah do in the evening? → Answer: `음악을 듣고 숙제를 해요 (Listens to music and does homework)` (*저녁에는 방에서 음악을 듣고 숙제를 해요.*)
- [ ] **Guided Writing Task:** Write 6 sentences describing your daily routine using present tense verbs (일어나요, 먹어요, 공부해요, 운동해요, 자요).
  - Sample Model Answer: `저는 아침 일곱 시 반에 일어나요. 아침에 커피와 빵을 먹어요. 낮에 도서관에서 한국어를 열심히 공부해요. 오후에 카페에서 친구를 만나요. 저녁에 집에서 영화를 봐요. 밤 열두 시에 자요.`

### 7.2 — Where You Do Things (에 가요 vs 에서 공부해요)

**Situation & Objective.** Distinguish destination particle -에 (movement with 가요/와요) from dynamic action location particle -에서.

#### Module 7.2.1 — 대화 (Conversation First)

- [ ] **Dialogue Practice:** Listen to and practice the dialogue between David (데이비드) and Min-su (민수) and David (데이비드) and Min-su (민수) and David (데이비드).
  - `민수 씨, 지금 어디에 가요?` (Min-su, where are you going right now?) — *Note: 어디에 가요 = Directional movement to a destination.*
  - `저는 중앙도서관에 가요. 사라 씨를 만나요.` (I am going to the Central Library. I am meeting Sarah.) — *Note: 중앙도서관에 가요 = Going TO the library.*
  - `도서관에서 무엇을 해요?` (What are you doing at the library?) — *Note: 도서관에서 = Dynamic action taking place AT the library.*
  - `도서관에서 한국어 시험공부를 해요. 데이비드 씨도 같이 갈래요?` (We are studying for the Korean exam at the library. David, would you like to come along too?) — *Note: 시험공부 = exam study; 같이 갈래요? = "Would you like to go together?"*
  - `좋아요! 저도 도서관에서 책을 빌려요.` (Great! I will borrow a book at the library too.) — *Note: 빌려요 = 빌리다 (to borrow).*
- [ ] **Cultural Etiquette & Pragmatics:** The distinction between "에" and "에서" is one of the most critical structural rules in Korean. Use "에" with directional movement verbs (가다, 오다, 다니다) and static existence (있다, 없다, 살다). Use "에서" whenever an active physical or mental action (먹다, 공부하다, 운동하다, 일하다, 사다) happens at that location.

#### Module 7.2.2 — 어휘 (Vocabulary in Context)

- [ ] **Core Target Words:** Memorise and pronounce all target words for this lesson:
  - [ ] `도서관` (doseogwan, Hanja: 圖書館) [Noun]: library — e.g., "도서관에서 조용히 공부해요." (*I study quietly at the library.*)
  - [ ] `학교` (hakgyo, Hanja: 學校) [Noun]: school — e.g., "아침마다 학교에 가요." (*I go to school every morning.*)
  - [ ] `식당` (sikdang, Hanja: 食堂) [Noun]: restaurant / cafeteria — e.g., "학생 식당에서 점심을 먹어요." (*I eat lunch at the student cafeteria.*)
  - [ ] `회사` (hoesa, Hanja: 會社) [Noun]: company / workplace — e.g., "아버지께서 회사에서 일하세요." (*My father works at a company.*)
  - [ ] `가다 / 가요` (gada / gayo) [Verb]: to go — e.g., "지금 공항에 가요." (*I am going to the airport right now.*)
  - [ ] `오다 / 와요` (oda / wayo) [Verb]: to come — e.g., "친구가 한국에 와요." (*My friend comes to Korea.*)

#### Module 7.2.3 — 문법 (Grammar Formulas & Rules)

- [ ] **Grammar Pattern: `Place + 에 가다/오다 (Destination) vs Place + 에서 Action Verb (Dynamic Location)`** (to go TO a place vs do an action AT a place)
  - Formula: `[Destination Place] + 에 + 가요/와요 | [Action Place] + 에서 + [Action Verb]`
  - Explanation: Never use 에서 with 가다/오다 when stating where you are traveling to; use 에. Conversely, never use 에 when performing a dynamic activity like eating or buying; use 에서.
  - Rule: 학교에 가요 (O) -> Going TO school
  - Rule: 학교에서 가요 (X - unless departing from school)
  - Rule: 식당에서 밥을 먹어요 (O) -> Eating AT the restaurant
  - Rule: 식당에 밥을 먹어요 (X - incorrect particle)
  - Example: `저는 매일 카페에 가요. 그리고 카페에서 공부해요.` — *I go to the cafe every day. And I study at the cafe.* [카페에 가요 (Destination 에) -> 카페에서 공부해요 (Action location 에서)]
  - Example: `백화점에서 옷을 사요.` — *I buy clothes at the department store.* [백화점 (dept store) + 에서 (at) + 옷 (clothes) + 을 + 사요 (buy)]

#### Module 7.2.4 — 말하기·듣기 (Shadowing & Drills)

- [ ] **Shadowing & Repetition Drill:** Choose whether to supply -에 or -에서 in each situational prompt.
  - [ ] Prompt: "I go TO school every day." → Speak aloud: `저는 매일 학교에 가요.` (*I go to school every day.*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "I study Korean AT the library." → Speak aloud: `도서관에서 한국어를 공부해요.` (*I study Korean at the library.*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "I meet my friend AT the cafe." → Speak aloud: `카페에서 친구를 만나요.` (*I meet my friend at the cafe.*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "My parents come TO Korea." → Speak aloud: `부모님이 한국에 와요.` (*My parents come to Korea.*)

#### Module 7.2.5 — 읽기·쓰기 (Reading & Production)

- [ ] **Reading Passage:** "어디에서 무엇을 해요? (Where Do You Do What?)"
  - Text: `사람들은 주말에 여러 장소에 가요. 학생들은 도서관에서 책을 읽고 시험공부를 해요. 직장인들은 공원에서 산책하고 운동해요. 어떤 사람들은 백화점에서 옷과 신발을 사요. 그리고 저녁에는 맛있는 식당에서 가족과 함께 식사해요.`
  - Translation: *People go to various places on weekends. Students read books and study for exams at the library. Office workers take walks and exercise in the park. Some people buy clothes and shoes at the department store. And in the evening, they dine together with their family at delicious restaurants.*
  - [ ] Check: What do students do at the library on weekends? → Answer: `책을 읽고 시험공부를 해요 (Read books and study for exams)` (*학생들은 도서관에서 책을 읽고 시험공부를 해요.*)
  - [ ] Check: Where do people buy clothes and shoes? → Answer: `백화점에서 (At the department store)` (*어떤 사람들은 백화점에서 옷과 신발을 사요.*)
- [ ] **Guided Writing Task:** Write 5 sentences detailing where you go on weekends and what specific action you do at each location using 에 and 에서.
  - Sample Model Answer: `저는 주말에 대형 서점에 가요. 서점에서 한국어 소설을 읽어요. 그리고 자주 가는 카페에도 가요. 카페에서 친구와 함께 시원한 커피를 마셔요. 저녁에는 편안하게 집에 와요.`


## Unit 8 — Family and People (가족과 사람들)

**Part.** Part B — Handle Everyday Needs (Units 4–8 & Checkpoint B)
**Objective.** Introduce family members with accurate familial titles, describe appearance and personality using Korean adjectives, and express negation with 안.

### 8.1 — Introducing Your Family (우리 가족을 소개합니다)

**Situation & Objective.** Name family relationships and introduce family members politely using honorific vocabulary (부모님, 아버지, 어머니) and 우리.

#### Module 8.1.1 — 대화 (Conversation First)

- [ ] **Dialogue Practice:** Listen to and practice the dialogue between Sarah (사라) and Min-su (민수) and Sarah (사라) and Min-su (민수) and Sarah (사라).
  - `민수 씨, 가족사진이에요? 가족이 몇 명이에요?` (Min-su, is this a family photo? How many people are in your family?) — *Note: 가족이 몇 명이에요? = "How many members in family?"; 명 = counter for people.*
  - `우리 가족은 모두 네 명이에요. 아버지, 어머니, 여동생, 그리고 저예요.` (Our family has four people in total. Father, mother, younger sister, and me.) — *Note: 우리 = "our/my" (Koreans say 우리 가족, 우리 집, 우리 학교).*
  - `여동생이 정말 귀여워요! 학생이에요?` (Your younger sister is really cute! Is she a student?) — *Note: 귀여워요 = 귀엽다 (cute, irregular ㅂ -> 워).*
  - `네, 고등학생이에요. 부모님은 부산에 사세요.` (Yes, she is a high school student. My parents live in Busan.) — *Note: 사세요 = 살다 with honorific -(으)시-.*
  - `아, 그러시군요! 아주 화목한 가족이에요.` (Ah, I see! It is a very warm and harmonious family.) — *Note: 그러시군요 = Polite sympathetic reaction.*
- [ ] **Cultural Etiquette & Pragmatics:** In Korean culture, communal solidarity means you rarely say "내 가족" (my family) or "내 집" (my house). Instead, always use "우리 가족" (our family), "우리 집" (our house), and "우리 나라" (our country). Furthermore, when referring to someone else's parents or your own elders respectfully, use "아버지" (father), "어머니" (mother), and "부모님" (parents).

#### Module 8.1.2 — 어휘 (Vocabulary in Context)

- [ ] **Core Target Words:** Memorise and pronounce all target words for this lesson:
  - [ ] `가족` (gajok, Hanja: 家族) [Noun]: family — e.g., "우리 가족은 서울에 살아요." (*Our family lives in Seoul.*)
  - [ ] `부모님` (bumonim, Hanja: 父母님) [Noun]: parents (respectful) — e.g., "부모님께 편지를 써요." (*I write a letter to my parents.*)
  - [ ] `아버지 / 어머니` (abeoji / eomeoni) [Noun]: father / mother — e.g., "아버지는 회사원이고 어머니는 선생님이세요." (*My father is an office worker and my mother is a teacher.*)
  - [ ] `형 / 오빠` (hyeong / oppa) [Noun]: older brother (male speaker: 형 / female speaker: 오빠) — e.g., "우리 형은 키가 커요." (*My older brother is tall.*)
  - [ ] `누나 / 언니` (nuna / eonni) [Noun]: older sister (male speaker: 누나 / female speaker: 언니) — e.g., "우리 언니는 요리를 잘해요." (*My older sister is good at cooking.*)
  - [ ] `남동생 / 여동생` (namdongsaeng / yeodongsaeng) [Noun]: younger brother / younger sister — e.g., "여동생은 지금 중학생이에요." (*My younger sister is currently a middle school student.*)
  - [ ] `명` (myeong, Hanja: 名) [Counter]: counter for people — e.g., "학생 세 명과 선생님 한 분" (*Three students and one teacher (honorific 분)*)

#### Module 8.1.3 — 문법 (Grammar Formulas & Rules)

- [ ] **Grammar Pattern: `우리 + [가족 / 집 / 학교]`** (Our / My (collective possessive pronoun))
  - Formula: `우리 + [Group/Shared Noun]`
  - Explanation: Although literally meaning "we/our", Korean speakers use 우리 where English speakers would naturally say "my", reflecting the collective and relational cultural mindset.
  - Rule: Always use 우리 with family: 우리 아버지, 우리 어머니, 우리 가족.
  - Rule: Use with institutions: 우리 학교 (my school), 우리 회사 (my company).
  - Example: `우리 가족은 모두 네 명이에요.` — *My family has four members in total.* [우리 (our/my) + 가족 (family) + 은 + 모두 (all) + 네 명 (4 people) + 이에요]

#### Module 8.1.4 — 말하기·듣기 (Shadowing & Drills)

- [ ] **Shadowing & Repetition Drill:** State the members and count of your family clearly.
  - [ ] Prompt: "Ask: How many people are in your family?" → Speak aloud: `가족이 몇 명이에요?` (*How many people are in your family?*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "Answer: My family has 3 people (Father, mother, and me)." → Speak aloud: `우리 가족은 세 명이에요. 아버지, 어머니, 그리고 저예요.` (*My family has 3 people. Father, mother, and me.*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "State: I have one older sister." → Speak aloud: `저는 언니가 한 명 있어요.` (*I have one older sister.*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "State: I do not have a younger sibling." → Speak aloud: `동생은 없어요.` (*I do not have a younger sibling.*)

#### Module 8.1.5 — 읽기·쓰기 (Reading & Production)

- [ ] **Reading Passage:** "민수의 가족 소개 (Minsu's Family Introduction)"
  - Text: `우리 가족을 소개합니다. 우리 가족은 아버지, 어머니, 저, 여동생 모두 네 명입니다. 아버지는 은행원이고 어머니는 중학교 교사이십니다. 여동생은 노래를 아주 잘합니다. 우리는 주말마다 함께 맛있는 음식을 먹고 공원을 산책합니다. 저는 우리 가족을 정말 사랑합니다.`
  - Translation: *Let me introduce our family. Our family has four people: father, mother, myself, and my younger sister. My father is a bank employee and my mother is a middle school teacher. My younger sister sings very well. Every weekend, we eat delicious food together and stroll in the park. I really love my family.*
  - [ ] Check: What is Minsu's mother's occupation? → Answer: `중학교 교사 (Middle school teacher)` (*어머니는 중학교 교사이십니다.*)
  - [ ] Check: What does Minsu's sister do well? → Answer: `노래 (Singing)` (*여동생은 노래를 아주 잘합니다.*)
- [ ] **Guided Writing Task:** Write a 6-sentence paragraph introducing your family, stating how many members you have, what their occupations are, and where they live.
  - Sample Model Answer: `우리 가족을 소개해요. 우리 가족은 모두 네 명이에요. 아버지, 어머니, 남동생, 그리고 저예요. 아버지는 회사원이고 어머니는 요리를 아주 잘하세요. 제 남동생은 대학생이에요. 우리 가족은 지금 서울에 살아요.`

### 8.2 — Describing People and Adjectives (사람 묘사와 형용사, 안 부정문)

**Situation & Objective.** Describe physical traits and personality using Korean descriptive adjectives and express simple negation with 안.

#### Module 8.2.1 — 대화 (Conversation First)

- [ ] **Dialogue Practice:** Listen to and practice the dialogue between Sarah (사라) and Min-su (민수) and Sarah (사라) and Min-su (민수) and Sarah (사라).
  - `민수 씨의 친구 지훈 씨는 어떤 사람이에요?` (What kind of person is your friend Ji-hoon, Min-su?) — *Note: 어떤 사람이에요? = "What kind of person is he?"*
  - `지훈이는 키가 아주 커요. 그리고 성격이 정말 친절해요.` (Ji-hoon is very tall. And his personality is really kind.) — *Note: 키가 커요 = tall (키 height + 크다 big); 친절해요 = kind.*
  - `요즘도 많이 바빠요?` (Is he very busy these days too?) — *Note: 바빠요 = 바쁘다 (busy).*
  - `아니요, 이번 주는 시험이 끝났어요. 그래서 안 바빠요.` (No, exams ended this week. So he is not busy.) — *Note: 안 바빠요 = 안 + adjective negation (not busy).*
  - `잘됐네요! 주말에 다 같이 영화를 봐요.` (That's great to hear! Let's all watch a movie together on the weekend.) — *Note: 잘됐네요 = "That turned out well / good to hear".*
- [ ] **Cultural Etiquette & Pragmatics:** In Korean, adjectives function syntactically as "descriptive verbs" (형용사). They do not require a separate linking verb "to be" (like "is" or "am" in English). You conjugate 크다 directly into 커요 ("is tall/big") and 친절하다 directly into 친절해요 ("is kind"). To make simple negatives, simply prefix "안" right before the verb or adjective (e.g. 안 바빠요, 안 먹어요).

#### Module 8.2.2 — 어휘 (Vocabulary in Context)

- [ ] **Core Target Words:** Memorise and pronounce all target words for this lesson:
  - [ ] `크다 / 커요` (keuda / keoyo) [Adjective]: to be big / tall (for height: 키가 크다) — e.g., "그 사람은 키가 아주 커요." (*That person is very tall.*)
  - [ ] `작다 / 작아요` (jakda / jagayo) [Adjective]: to be small / short (for height: 키가 작다) — e.g., "방이 조금 작아요." (*The room is a little small.*)
  - [ ] `친절하다 / 친절해요` (chinjeolhada / chinjeolhaeyo, Hanja: 親切) [Adjective]: to be kind / friendly — e.g., "선생님이 아주 친절해요." (*The teacher is very kind.*)
  - [ ] `바쁘다 / 바빠요` (bappeuda / bappayo) [Adjective]: to be busy — e.g., "오늘 일이 많아서 바빠요." (*I am busy because I have a lot of work today.*)
  - [ ] `재미있다 / 재미있어요` (jaemiitda / jaemiisseoyo) [Adjective]: to be interesting / fun — e.g., "한국어 수업이 정말 재미있어요." (*Korean class is really fun.*)
  - [ ] `안` (an) [Adverb]: not (short-form negation adverb) — e.g., "아침밥을 안 먹었어요." (*I did not eat breakfast.*)

#### Module 8.2.3 — 문법 (Grammar Formulas & Rules)

- [ ] **Grammar Pattern: `안 + Verb / Adjective (Short-form Negation)`** (not / does not / is not)
  - Formula: `안 + [Inflected Verb or Adjective]`
  - Explanation: Place the adverb 안 directly before the predicate verb or adjective. For noun-하다 verbs (e.g. 공부하다, 운동하다), 안 is placed between the noun and 하다 (공부 안 해요, 운동 안 해요). For adjectives ending in -하다 (친절하다), 안 comes in front: 안 친절해요.
  - Rule: Adjective: 안 바빠요 (not busy), 안 커요 (not big)
  - Rule: Action verb: 안 가요 (don't go), 안 먹어요 (don't eat)
  - Rule: Noun-하다 action verb: 일 안 해요 (don't work), 공부 안 해요 (don't study)
  - Example: `저는 매운 음식을 안 먹어요.` — *I do not eat spicy food.* [매운 음식 (spicy food) + 을 + 안 (not) + 먹어요 (eat)]
  - Example: `오늘 날씨가 안 추워요.` — *The weather is not cold today.* [날씨 (weather) + 가 + 안 (not) + 추워요 (cold)]

#### Module 8.2.4 — 말하기·듣기 (Shadowing & Drills)

- [ ] **Shadowing & Repetition Drill:** Practice describing people and negating statements promptly.
  - [ ] Prompt: "Describe: Min-su is kind and tall." → Speak aloud: `민수 씨는 친절하고 키가 커요.` (*Min-su is kind and tall.*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "Negate: I am not busy today." → Speak aloud: `저는 오늘 안 바빠요.` (*I am not busy today.*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "Negate: That computer is not expensive." → Speak aloud: `그 컴퓨터는 안 비싸요.` (*That computer is not expensive.*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "State: Korean study is really interesting." → Speak aloud: `한국어 공부가 정말 재미있어요.` (*Korean study is really interesting.*)

#### Module 8.2.5 — 읽기·쓰기 (Reading & Production)

- [ ] **Reading Passage:** "제장 친한 친구 (My Best Friend)"
  - Text: `제 가장 친한 친구 이름은 김유진입니다. 유진 씨는 키가 작지만 눈이 아주 큽니다. 성격이 매우 밝고 친절합니다. 우리는 주말에 자주 만나서 함께 커피를 마시고 한국어 공부를 합니다. 유진 씨는 노래를 아주 잘합니다. 항상 친구들을 따뜻하게 도와줍니다.`
  - Translation: *My best friend's name is Kim Yu-jin. Yu-jin is short in height, but her eyes are very big. Her personality is very bright and kind. We meet often on weekends, drink coffee together, and study Korean. Yu-jin is very good at singing. She always warmly helps her friends.*
  - [ ] Check: What is Yu-jin's personality like? → Answer: `매우 밝고 친절해요 (Very bright and kind)` (*성격이 매우 밝고 친절합니다.*)
  - [ ] Check: What does the writer do with Yu-jin on weekends? → Answer: `커피를 마시고 한국어 공부를 해요 (Drink coffee and study Korean)` (*함께 커피를 마시고 한국어 공부를 합니다.*)
- [ ] **Guided Writing Task:** Write 5-6 sentences describing your best friend or classmate's appearance, personality, and whether they are busy.
  - Sample Model Answer: `제 친구 이름은 마이클이에요. 마이클은 키가 아주 커요. 성격이 정말 친절하고 재미있어요. 요즘은 아르바이트 때문에 조금 바빠요. 하지만 주말에 같이 축구를 해요. 정말 좋은 친구예요.`


## Checkpoint B — Checkpoint B: Everyday Needs, Shopping & Daily Life

**Prerequisites.** Completion of Unit 4, Unit 5, Unit 6, Unit 7, and Unit 8.
**Target Skills:**
- [ ] Order at cafes and Korean restaurants using native counters (한 잔, 두 개, 일 인분) and 주세요.
- [ ] Ask prices in Korean Won with 얼마예요 and read Sino-Korean sums (만, 천, 백).
- [ ] State object and building locations with 에 있어요 and spatial nouns (앞, 뒤, 옆, 안).
- [ ] Conjugate daily verbs in the present tense (아요/어요/해요) and differentiate 에 vs 에서.
- [ ] Introduce family members and describe people with adjectives and 안 negation.

### Spoken Simulation
- **Scenario:** You enter a traditional Korean restaurant in Insadong and order lunch, ask for water, and pay at the counter.
- **Prompt:** Call the server, order 2 bibimbap and 1 cola, ask for water, and ask the cashier how much the total is.
- [ ] Deliver unprompted: `여기요! 비빔밥 두 개하고 콜라 한 캔 주세요. 시원한 물도 좀 주세요. (식사 후) 사장님, 맛있게 먹었습니다. 모두 얼마예요? 카드로 결제할게요.` (*Over here! Please give us two bibimbap and one can of cola. Please give us cold water too. (After meal) Owner, we ate deliciously. How much is it all together? I will pay by card.*)

### Written Milestone Assignment
- **Prompt:** Write a 7-sentence descriptive journal entry about your room, daily routine, and what you bought this week. (60–80 Korean words)
- [ ] Submit written draft: `제 방에 큰 책상과 침대가 있어요. 책상 위에 노트북이 있어요. 저는 매일 아침 도서관에 가요. 도서관에서 한국어를 세 시간 동안 공부해요. 어제는 백화점에서 따뜻한 셔츠 한 벌을 샀어요. 삼만 오천 원이었어요. 그리고 저녁에 가족과 함께 맛있는 밥을 먹었어요.`

---

## Unit 9 — Time and Daily Routine (시간과 하루 일과)

**Part.** Part C — Talk About Your Life (Units 9–13 & Checkpoint C)
**Objective.** Tell the time using Native Korean numbers for hours and Sino-Korean numbers for minutes, state daily routines with time particle 에, and connect sentences with 그리고.

### 9.1 — Asking When Someone Wakes Up (몇 시에 일어나요?)

**Situation & Objective.** Master telling time using Native numbers for hours (시) and Sino-Korean numbers for minutes (분), and time marker 에.

#### Module 9.1.1 — 대화 (Conversation First)

- [ ] **Dialogue Practice:** Listen to and practice the dialogue between Min-su (민수) and Sarah (사라) and Min-su (민수) and Sarah (사라) and Min-su (민수).
  - `사라 씨, 보통 아침 몇 시에 일어나요?` (Sarah, what time do you usually wake up in the morning?) — *Note: 보통 = usually; 몇 시에 = at what time.*
  - `저는 일곱 시 반에 일어나요. 민수 씨는요?` (I wake up at 7:30. How about you, Min-su?) — *Note: 반 = half (30 minutes); 일곱 시 = Native 7 + 시.*
  - `저는 여덟 시 십 분에 일어나요. 그리고 아홉 시에 수업이 시작돼요.` (I wake up at 8:10. And my class starts at 9:00.) — *Note: 십 분 = Sino-Korean 10 + 분; 시작돼요 = starts.*
  - `수업이 몇 시에 끝나요?` (What time does class finish?) — *Note: 끝나요 = 끝나다 (to finish/end).*
  - `오후 한 시에 끝나요. 그래서 한 시 반에 점심을 먹어요.` (It finishes at 1:00 PM. So I eat lunch at 1:30.) — *Note: 한 시 = 1:00 (하나 -> 한).*
- [ ] **Cultural Etiquette & Pragmatics:** Telling time is one of the most famous dual-number quirks in Korean. Hours (시) use Native Korean numbers (한 시, 두 시, 세 시...), while minutes (분) use Sino-Korean numbers (일 분, 십 분, 삼십 분). For 30 minutes, Koreans frequently substitute the word "반" (half), as in "일곱 시 반" (7:30).

#### Module 9.1.2 — 어휘 (Vocabulary in Context)

- [ ] **Core Target Words:** Memorise and pronounce all target words for this lesson:
  - [ ] `시 / 분 / 초` (si / bun / cho, Hanja: 時 / 分 / 秒) [Counter]: hour (o'clock) / minute / second — e.g., "지금은 오후 세 시 십오 분이에요." (*It is 3:15 PM right now.*)
  - [ ] `반` (ban, Hanja: 半) [Noun]: half / half past (30 minutes) — e.g., "열두 시 반에 만나요." (*Let us meet at 12:30.*)
  - [ ] `오전 / 오후` (ojeon / ohu, Hanja: 午前 / 午後) [Noun]: AM (morning) / PM (afternoon) — e.g., "오전 아홉 시부터 오후 여섯 시까지 일해요." (*I work from 9 AM to 6 PM.*)
  - [ ] `보통` (botong, Hanja: 普通) [Adverb]: usually / normally — e.g., "보통 주말에는 푹 쉬어요." (*I usually rest deeply on weekends.*)
  - [ ] `일어나다 / 일어나요` (ireonada / ireonayo) [Verb]: to wake up / get up — e.g., "아침 일찍 일어나요." (*I get up early in the morning.*)
  - [ ] `시작하다 / 끝나다` (sijakhada / kkeutnada) [Verb]: to start / to finish — e.g., "영화가 여덟 시에 시작하고 열 시에 끝나요." (*The movie starts at 8 and ends at 10.*)

#### Module 9.1.3 — 문법 (Grammar Formulas & Rules)

- [ ] **Grammar Pattern: `Time + 에 (Time Marker)`** (at / on / in (indicating the time of an event))
  - Formula: `[Specific Hour/Minute/Day] + 에`
  - Explanation: Attach -에 to specific clock times, days, and calendar units. Note: -에 is NOT attached to relative time adverbs like 오늘 (today), 어제 (yesterday), 내일 (tomorrow), 지금 (now), or 언제 (when).
  - Rule: Attach to clock times: 일곱 시에 (at 7 o'clock), 세 시 반에 (at 3:30).
  - Rule: DO NOT attach to: 오늘, 내일, 어제, 지금, 매일 (say 오늘 가요, NOT 오늘에 가요).
  - Example: `저는 밤 열한 시에 자요.` — *I sleep at 11 o'clock at night.* [밤 (night) + 열한 시 (11 o'clock) + 에 (at) + 자요 (sleep)]
  - Example: `내일 세 시에 시험이 있어요.` — *There is an exam at 3 o'clock tomorrow.* [내일 (no 에) + 세 시 (3 o'clock) + 에 (at) + 시험이 있어요]

#### Module 9.1.4 — 말하기·듣기 (Shadowing & Drills)

- [ ] **Shadowing & Repetition Drill:** Practice reciting clock times with the correct hybrid numbering system.
  - [ ] Prompt: "Say the time: 6:00 AM." → Speak aloud: `오전 여섯 시예요.` (*It is 6:00 AM.*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "Say the time: 2:30 PM." → Speak aloud: `오후 두 시 반이에요.` (*It is 2:30 PM.*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "Ask: What time do you eat lunch?" → Speak aloud: `몇 시에 점심을 먹어요?` (*What time do you eat lunch?*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "Answer: I eat at 12:45." → Speak aloud: `열두 시 사십오 분에 먹어요.` (*I eat at 12:45.*)

#### Module 9.1.5 — 읽기·쓰기 (Reading & Production)

- [ ] **Reading Passage:** "하루 시간표 (Daily Schedule Table)"
  - Text: `김민수의 하루 시간표: - 07:00 기상 및 아침 운동 - 08:00 아침 식사 - 09:00 - 13:00 대학교 전공 수업 - 13:00 점심 식사 (학생식당) - 14:00 - 17:00 도서관 자습 - 19:00 저녁 식사 및 휴식 - 23:30 취침`
  - Translation: *Kim Minsu's Daily Schedule: - 07:00 Wake-up and morning exercise - 08:00 Breakfast - 09:00 - 13:00 University major classes - 13:00 Lunch (student cafeteria) - 14:00 - 17:00 Library self-study - 19:00 Dinner and relaxation - 23:30 Bedtime*
  - [ ] Check: What time does Minsu eat breakfast? → Answer: `8:00 AM (여덟 시)` (*08:00 아침 식사*)
  - [ ] Check: What does Minsu do at 11:30 PM? → Answer: `취침 (Goes to sleep - 자요)` (*23:30 취침*)
- [ ] **Guided Writing Task:** Write out your exact daily schedule for 5 key events in full Korean sentences specifying the time with Native hours and Sino-Korean minutes.
  - Sample Model Answer: `1. 저는 아침 일곱 시에 일어나요. 2. 여덟 시 십 분에 아침 식사를 해요. 3. 여덟 시 오십 분에 학교에 가요. 4. 오후 열두 시 반에 점심을 먹어요. 5. 밤 열한 시 사십오 분에 잠을 자요.`

### 9.2 — Comparing Daily Schedules and Sentences (하루 일과와 그리고)

**Situation & Objective.** Connect multiple routine actions into cohesive paragraphs using 그리고, frequency adverbs (매일, 자주, 가끔), and time sequence.

#### Module 9.2.1 — 대화 (Conversation First)

- [ ] **Dialogue Practice:** Listen to and practice the dialogue between David (데이비드) and Sarah (사라) and David (데이비드) and Sarah (사라) and David (데이비드).
  - `사라 씨는 주말에 보통 뭐 해요?` (Sarah, what do you usually do on the weekend?) — *Note: 보통 = usually.*
  - `저는 토요일 아침에 빨래를 해요. 그리고 방 청소를 해요.` (I do laundry on Saturday morning. And I clean my room.) — *Note: 빨래를 해요 = do laundry; 청소를 해요 = clean.*
  - `오후에는 운동을 자주 해요?` (Do you often exercise in the afternoon?) — *Note: 자주 = often (frequency adverb).*
  - `네, 한강 공원에서 자전거를 타요. 그리고 저녁에는 친구와 맛있는 음식을 먹어요.` (Yes, I ride a bicycle at Hangang Park. And in the evening, I eat delicious food with a friend.) — *Note: 자전거를 타요 = ride a bicycle.*
  - `정말 알차고 건강한 주말이네요!` (That is truly a productive and healthy weekend!) — *Note: 알차다 = fruitful/productive.*
- [ ] **Cultural Etiquette & Pragmatics:** Hangang Park (한강공원) in Seoul is the centerpiece of weekend recreational life for university students and residents. Renting a bicycle (따릉이), picnicking on the grass, and eating instant ramen cooked on convenience store machines by the river is a classic weekend ritual.

#### Module 9.2.2 — 어휘 (Vocabulary in Context)

- [ ] **Core Target Words:** Memorise and pronounce all target words for this lesson:
  - [ ] `그리고` (geurigo) [Particle]: and / and then (sentence connector) — e.g., "영화를 봤어요. 그리고 밥을 먹었어요." (*I watched a movie. And then I ate a meal.*)
  - [ ] `매일 / 항상` (maeil / hangsang, Hanja: 每日 / 恒常) [Adverb]: every day / always — e.g., "저는 매일 한국어 단어를 외워요." (*I memorize Korean words every day.*)
  - [ ] `자주 / 가끔` (jaju / gakkeum) [Adverb]: often / sometimes — e.g., "카페에 자주 가요. 하지만 술은 가끔 마셔요." (*I often go to cafes. But I drink alcohol only sometimes.*)
  - [ ] `청소하다 / 빨래하다` (cheongsohada / ppallaehada, Hanja: 淸掃 / ──) [Verb]: to clean / to do laundry — e.g., "토요일마다 집을 청소해요." (*I clean the house every Saturday.*)
  - [ ] `자전거를 타다` (jajeon-georeul tada) [Verb]: to ride a bicycle — e.g., "한강에서 자전거를 타요." (*I ride a bicycle at the Han River.*)

#### Module 9.2.3 — 문법 (Grammar Formulas & Rules)

- [ ] **Grammar Pattern: `Sentence 1. 그리고 + Sentence 2.`** (Sentence 1. And / And then Sentence 2.)
  - Formula: `[Full Sentence with ending]. 그리고 [Full Sentence with ending].`
  - Explanation: 그리고 is placed at the start of a new clause or sentence to link two actions chronologically or conceptually.
  - Rule: Always end the first sentence completely with a period: 저는 밥을 먹어요. 그리고 커피를 마셔요.
  - Rule: Do not confuse 그리고 (sentence connector) with 하고/와/과 (noun connectors).
  - Example: `아침에 운동을 해요. 그리고 샤워를 해요.` — *I exercise in the morning. And then I take a shower.* [운동을 해요 (exercise) + . + 그리고 (and then) + 샤워를 해요 (shower)]

#### Module 9.2.4 — 말하기·듣기 (Shadowing & Drills)

- [ ] **Shadowing & Repetition Drill:** Incorporate frequency adverbs and link two actions using 그리고.
  - [ ] Prompt: "Connect: I study Korean. And I listen to music." → Speak aloud: `저는 한국어를 공부해요. 그리고 음악을 들어요.` (*I study Korean. And I listen to music.*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "State: I exercise every day." → Speak aloud: `저는 매일 운동해요.` (*I exercise every day.*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "State: I often drink coffee at the cafe." → Speak aloud: `카페에서 커피를 자주 마셔요.` (*I often drink coffee at the cafe.*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "State: I clean my room on Saturdays. And I do laundry." → Speak aloud: `토요일에 방 청소를 해요. 그리고 빨래를 해요.` (*I clean my room on Saturdays. And I do laundry.*)

#### Module 9.2.5 — 읽기·쓰기 (Reading & Production)

- [ ] **Reading Passage:** "두 친구의 주말 비교 (Comparing Two Friends' Weekends)"
  - Text: `지훈 씨와 마이클 씨의 주말은 서로 달라요. 지훈 씨는 주말에 집에서 푹 쉬어요. 늦게까지 잠을 자요. 그리고 거실에서 넷플릭스 드라마를 봐요. 하지만 마이클 씨는 주말마다 밖으로 나가요. 북한산에 등산을 가요. 그리고 저녁에는 친구들과 축구를 해요. 두 사람 모두 즐거운 주말을 보내요.`
  - Translation: *Ji-hoon and Michael's weekends are different from each other. Ji-hoon rests deeply at home on weekends. He sleeps until late. And he watches Netflix dramas in the living room. However, Michael goes outside every weekend. He goes hiking at Bukhansan Mountain. And in the evening, he plays soccer with his friends. Both people spend enjoyable weekends.*
  - [ ] Check: What does Ji-hoon do on weekends? → Answer: `집에서 푹 쉬고 드라마를 봐요 (Rests at home and watches dramas)` (*집에서 푹 쉬어요. 그리고 거실에서 넷플릭스 드라마를 봐요.*)
  - [ ] Check: Where does Michael go hiking? → Answer: `북한산 (Bukhansan Mountain)` (*북한산에 등산을 가요.*)
- [ ] **Guided Writing Task:** Write a 6-sentence paragraph describing your typical Saturday, using 그리고 twice and at least two frequency adverbs (매일, 자주, 보통).
  - Sample Model Answer: `저는 토요일에 보통 아홉 시에 일어나요. 아침에 가볍게 조깅을 해요. 그리고 시원한 물을 마셔요. 오후에는 도서관에서 친구를 자주 만나요. 서점에서 재미있는 책을 함께 읽어요. 그리고 밤 열한 시에 집으로 돌아와요.`


## Unit 10 — Dates, Days and Appointments (날짜와 약속)

**Part.** Part C — Talk About Your Life (Units 9–13 & Checkpoint C)
**Objective.** Read calendar dates in Sino-Korean numbers, state birthdays, name days of the week, and schedule appointments using 언제 and time particle 에.

### 10.1 — Asking About Birthdays and Dates (생일과 날짜 묻기)

**Situation & Objective.** Read full calendar dates using Sino-Korean numbers with 년 (year), 월 (month), and 일 (day), including irregular month pronunciations.

#### Module 10.1.1 — 대화 (Conversation First)

- [ ] **Dialogue Practice:** Listen to and practice the dialogue between Min-su (민수) and Sarah (사라) and Min-su (민수) and Sarah (사라) and Min-su (민수).
  - `사라 씨, 생일이 언제예요?` (Sarah, when is your birthday?) — *Note: 생일이 언제예요? = "When is your birthday?"*
  - `제 생일은 10월 24일이에요. 민수 씨 생일은요?` (My birthday is October 24th. What about yours, Min-su?) — *Note: 10월 is pronounced "시월" (not 십월); 24일 = 이십사일.*
  - `제 생일은 6월 15일이에요. 다음 달이네요!` (My birthday is June 15th. That's next month!) — *Note: 6월 is pronounced "유월" (not 육월).*
  - `오늘이 5월 며칠이에요?` (What day of May is it today?) — *Note: 며칠 = "what date / how many days".*
  - `오늘은 5월 18일이에요. 날씨가 참 좋아요.` (Today is May 18th. The weather is truly nice.) — *Note: 5월 18일 = 오월 십팔일.*
- [ ] **Cultural Etiquette & Pragmatics:** Crucial calendar phonetic rule: While months are numbered 1 to 12 with Sino-Korean digits, month 6 and month 10 lose their final 받침 consonants to ensure smooth pronunciation: 6월 is pronounced and spelled "유월" (never 육월), and 10월 is pronounced and spelled "시월" (never 십월). In contrast, days of the month (일) keep normal Sino-Korean forms: 1일(일일), 2일(이일)... 31일(삼십일일).

#### Module 10.1.2 — 어휘 (Vocabulary in Context)

- [ ] **Core Target Words:** Memorise and pronounce all target words for this lesson:
  - [ ] `년 / 월 / 일` (nyeon / wol / il, Hanja: 年 / 月 / 日) [Counter]: year / month / day — e.g., "2026년 10월 24일" (*October 24, 2026 (이천이십육년 시월 이십사일)*)
  - [ ] `유월 / 시월` (yuwol / siwol) [Noun]: June (6월) / October (10월) [irregular pronunciation] — e.g., "유월과 시월에 축제가 열려요." (*Festivals are held in June and October.*)
  - [ ] `생일` (saeng-il, Hanja: 生日) [Noun]: birthday (honorific: 생신) — e.g., "생일 축하합니다!" (*Happy Birthday!*)
  - [ ] `언제` (eonje) [Noun]: when (interrogative pronoun) — e.g., "한국에 언제 왔어요?" (*When did you come to Korea?*)
  - [ ] `며칠` (myeochil) [Noun]: what date / how many days — e.g., "오늘이 몇 월 며칠이에요?" (*What month and date is it today?*)

#### Module 10.1.3 — 문법 (Grammar Formulas & Rules)

- [ ] **Grammar Pattern: `Sino-Korean Year + 년, Month + 월, Day + 일`** (Korean Date Format (Year-Month-Day order))
  - Formula: `[Year]년 + [Month]월 + [Day]일 + 이에요/예요`
  - Explanation: Korean dates always progress hierarchically from the largest unit to the smallest: Year -> Month -> Day.
  - Rule: Year: 2026년 (이천이십육 년)
  - Rule: Month: 1월 (일월), 2월 (이월), 3월 (삼월), 4월 (사월), 5월 (오월), 6월 (유월 *), 7월 (칠월), 8월 (팔월), 9월 (구월), 10월 (시월 *), 11월 (십일월), 12월 (십이월)
  - Rule: Day: 며칠 (what day), 15일 (십오 일)
  - Example: `제 생일은 삼월 십사일이에요.` — *My birthday is March 14th.* [삼월 (March) + 십사일 (14th) + 이에요]

#### Module 10.1.4 — 말하기·듣기 (Shadowing & Drills)

- [ ] **Shadowing & Repetition Drill:** Read each calendar date aloud in smooth Korean without pausing.
  - [ ] Prompt: "Read date: June 15th." → Speak aloud: `유월 십오일이에요.` (*It is June 15th.*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "Read date: October 24th." → Speak aloud: `시월 이십사일이에요.` (*It is October 24th.*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "Ask: What month and date is it today?" → Speak aloud: `오늘이 몇 월 며칠이에요?` (*What month and date is it today?*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "Answer: Today is August 15th (National Liberation Day)." → Speak aloud: `오늘은 팔월 십오일 광복절이에요.` (*Today is August 15th, National Liberation Day.*)

#### Module 10.1.5 — 읽기·쓰기 (Reading & Production)

- [ ] **Reading Passage:** "한국의 주요 기념일 (Major Korean National Holidays)"
  - Text: `한국에는 의미 있는 기념일이 많습니다. - 삼일절: 3월 1일 (삼월 일일) - 어린이날: 5월 5일 (오월 오일) - 현충일: 6월 6일 (유월 육일) - 광복절: 8월 15일 (팔월 십오일) - 한글날: 10월 9일 (시월 구일) * 한글날은 세종대왕이 한글을 반포한 날을 기념하는 날입니다.`
  - Translation: *Korea has many meaningful memorial holidays. - March 1st Movement Day: March 1st - Children's Day: May 5th - Memorial Day: June 6th - National Liberation Day: August 15th - Hangul Day: October 9th * Hangul Day is a day commemorating the promulgation of Hangul by King Sejong.*
  - [ ] Check: When is Hangul Day celebrated in Korea? → Answer: `10월 9일 (시월 구일)` (*한글날: 10월 9일 (시월 구일)*)
  - [ ] Check: What is June 6th in Korea? → Answer: `현충일 (Memorial Day)` (*현충일: 6월 6일 (유월 육일)*)
- [ ] **Guided Writing Task:** Write 4 sentences detailing your birthday, the current year and date, and the birthday of one family member or friend in Korean.
  - Sample Model Answer: `오늘은 2026년 10월 24일이에요. 제 생일은 4월 12일이에요. 제 친구 민수의 생일은 6월 15일이에요. 우리는 생일에 맛있는 케이크를 먹어요.`

### 10.2 — Planning a Meeting and Days of the Week (요일과 약속 잡기)

**Situation & Objective.** Master days of the week (월, 화, 수, 목, 금, 토, 일) and negotiate appointment times and places.

#### Module 10.2.1 — 대화 (Conversation First)

- [ ] **Dialogue Practice:** Listen to and practice the dialogue between Sarah (사라) and Min-su (민수) and Sarah (사라) and Min-su (민수) and Sarah (사라).
  - `민수 씨, 이번 주 금요일에 시간 있어요?` (Min-su, do you have time this Friday?) — *Note: 이번 주 = this week; 시간 있어요? = "Do you have time?"*
  - `금요일에는 아르바이트가 있어요. 토요일은 어때요?` (On Friday I have a part-time job. How about Saturday?) — *Note: 아르바이트 = part-time job (from German Arbeit); 토요일은 어때요? = "How about Saturday?"*
  - `토요일 좋아요! 언제 만날까요?` (Saturday is good! When shall we meet?) — *Note: 언제 만날까요? = "When shall we meet?"*
  - `오후 두 시에 홍대입구역 9번 출구에서 만나요.` (Let's meet at 2:00 PM at Hongik University Station Exit 9.) — *Note: 홍대입구역 9번 출구 = Seoul's most famous meeting spot.*
  - `네, 좋아요! 토요일 두 시에 봐요.` (Yes, sounds great! See you at two on Saturday.) — *Note: 두 시에 봐요 = "See you at two".*
- [ ] **Cultural Etiquette & Pragmatics:** Days of the week in Korean are named after the celestial elements of East Asian philosophy: Moon (월요일 - Monday), Fire/Mars (화요일 - Tuesday), Water/Mercury (수요일 - Wednesday), Wood/Jupiter (목요일 - Thursday), Gold/Venus (금요일 - Friday), Earth/Saturn (토요일 - Saturday), and Sun (일요일 - Sunday). Meeting someone at "홍대 9번 출구" or "강남역 10번 출구" is standard Seoul youth culture.

#### Module 10.2.2 — 어휘 (Vocabulary in Context)

- [ ] **Core Target Words:** Memorise and pronounce all target words for this lesson:
  - [ ] `월요일 / 화요일 / 수요일` (woryoil / hwayoil / suyoil, Hanja: 月 / 火 / 水) [Noun]: Monday / Tuesday / Wednesday — e.g., "월요일부터 수요일까지 수업이 있어요." (*I have classes from Monday to Wednesday.*)
  - [ ] `목요일 / 금요일` (mogyoil / geumyoil, Hanja: 木 / 金) [Noun]: Thursday / Friday — e.g., "불타는 금요일 (불금)!" (*Burning Friday (TGIF)!*)
  - [ ] `토요일 / 일요일 / 주말` (toyoil / iryoil / jumal, Hanja: 土 / 日 / 週末) [Noun]: Saturday / Sunday / weekend — e.g., "주말에 푹 쉬세요." (*Have a good rest over the weekend.*)
  - [ ] `약속` (yaksok, Hanja: 約束) [Noun]: appointment / plan / promise — e.g., "오늘 저녁에 중요한 약속이 있어요." (*I have an important appointment this evening.*)
  - [ ] `어때요?` (eottaeyo) [Expression]: How about...? / How is it? — e.g., "내일 점심 어때요?" (*How about lunch tomorrow?*)

#### Module 10.2.3 — 문법 (Grammar Formulas & Rules)

- [ ] **Grammar Pattern: `Day + 요일에 (On [Day])`** (on Monday, on Sunday, etc.)
  - Formula: `[Day of Week] + 에`
  - Explanation: Attach the time particle -에 to days of the week to state when an activity occurs.
  - Rule: 월요일에 만나요 (Let's meet on Monday)
  - Rule: 주말에 쉬어요 (I rest on the weekend)
  - Example: `우리는 수요일 오후에 한국어를 공부해요.` — *We study Korean on Wednesday afternoons.* [수요일 (Wednesday) + 오후 (afternoon) + 에 (on) + 공부해요]

#### Module 10.2.4 — 말하기·듣기 (Shadowing & Drills)

- [ ] **Shadowing & Repetition Drill:** Propose and accept meeting times using days of the week and 어때요.
  - [ ] Prompt: "Ask: Do you have time on Thursday?" → Speak aloud: `목요일에 시간 있어요?` (*Do you have time on Thursday?*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "Propose: How about Saturday at 3 PM?" → Speak aloud: `토요일 오후 세 시 어때요?` (*How about Saturday at 3 PM?*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "Accept: Sounds great! Let's meet in front of the station." → Speak aloud: `좋아요! 역 앞에서 만나요.` (*Sounds great! Let's meet in front of the station.*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "State: I have an appointment on Friday evening." → Speak aloud: `금요일 저녁에 약속이 있어요.` (*I have an appointment on Friday evening.*)

#### Module 10.2.5 — 읽기·쓰기 (Reading & Production)

- [ ] **Reading Passage:** "카카오톡 약속 메시지 (KakaoTalk Appointment Chat)"
  - Text: `사라: 민수 씨, 이번 주말에 약속 있어요? 민수: 토요일에는 동아리 모임이 있어요. 하지만 일요일에는 시간 있어요. 사라: 잘됐네요! 일요일 점심에 같이 비빔밥 먹을까요? 민수: 좋아요! 일요일 열두 시에 신촌역 3번 출구에서 만나요. 사라: 네, 일요일에 봐요!`
  - Translation: *Sarah: Minsu, do you have plans this weekend? Minsu: On Saturday, I have a university club meeting. But on Sunday, I have time. Sarah: That's great! Shall we eat bibimbap together for lunch on Sunday? Minsu: Great! Let's meet at 12 o'clock on Sunday at Sinchon Station Exit 3. Sarah: Yes, see you on Sunday!*
  - [ ] Check: Why can't Minsu meet on Saturday? → Answer: `동아리 모임이 있어요 (He has a club meeting)` (*토요일에는 동아리 모임이 있어요.*)
  - [ ] Check: Where and when are they meeting? → Answer: `일요일 12시 신촌역 3번 출구 (Sunday 12:00 at Sinchon Station Exit 3)` (*일요일 열두 시에 신촌역 3번 출구에서 만나요.*)
- [ ] **Guided Writing Task:** Write a realistic 5-line KakaoTalk chat message inviting a friend to hang out on a specific day of the week, setting a time and landmark.
  - Sample Model Answer: `사라: 지훈 씨, 이번 주 토요일에 시간 있어요? 지훈: 네, 토요일에는 약속이 없어요. 왜요? 사라: 우리 같이 코엑스에서 영화 봐요. 지훈: 좋아요! 몇 시에 어디에서 만날까요? 사라: 오후 두 시에 삼성역 개찰구 앞에서 만나요.`


## Unit 11 — Hobbies and Preferences (취미와 선호)

**Part.** Part C — Talk About Your Life (Units 9–13 & Checkpoint C)
**Objective.** Discuss personal hobbies, express preferences with 좋아하다/싫어하다 and direct objects, formulate desires using -고 싶어요, and contrast ideas using 하지만.

### 11.1 — Asking About Hobbies and Preferences (취미와 좋아하다/싫어하다)

**Situation & Objective.** Express likes and dislikes with 좋아해요 and 싫어해요 paired with the object particle 을/를.

#### Module 11.1.1 — 대화 (Conversation First)

- [ ] **Dialogue Practice:** Listen to and practice the dialogue between David (데이비드) and Sarah (사라) and David (데이비드) and Sarah (사라) and David (데이비드).
  - `사라 씨는 취미가 뭐예요?` (Sarah, what is your hobby?) — *Note: 취미 = hobby.*
  - `제 취미는 음악 감상과 여행이에요. 데이비드 씨는요?` (My hobbies are listening to music and traveling. What about you, David?) — *Note: 음악 감상 = music appreciation; 여행 = travel.*
  - `저는 운동을 정말 좋아해요. 주말마다 축구를 해요.` (I really like sports. I play soccer every weekend.) — *Note: 운동을 좋아해요 = Object particle 을 + 좋아해요.*
  - `매운 음식도 좋아해요? 떡볶이 어때요?` (Do you also like spicy food? How about tteokbokki?) — *Note: 떡볶이 = spicy stir-fried rice cakes.*
  - `아, 저는 매운 음식을 조금 싫어해요. 하지만 불고기는 아주 좋아해요.` (Ah, I dislike spicy food a little. But I like bulgogi very much.) — *Note: 싫어해요 = dislike; 하지만 = "however / but".*
- [ ] **Cultural Etiquette & Pragmatics:** Be careful of the critical distinction in Korean between the adjective 좋다 (to be good) and the transitive verb 좋아하다 (to like). When expressing what you like, use the object particle: [Noun]을/를 좋아해요. If you use 좋다, you must use the subject particle: [Noun]이/가 좋아요 (literally "Noun is good to me").

#### Module 11.1.2 — 어휘 (Vocabulary in Context)

- [ ] **Core Target Words:** Memorise and pronounce all target words for this lesson:
  - [ ] `취미` (chwimi, Hanja: 趣味) [Noun]: hobby — e.g., "취미가 뭐예요?" (*What is your hobby?*)
  - [ ] `좋아하다 / 좋아해요` (joahada / joahaeyo) [Verb]: to like (takes 을/를) — e.g., "저는 케이팝 음악을 좋아해요." (*I like K-pop music.*)
  - [ ] `싫어하다 / 싫어해요` (sireohada / sireohaeyo) [Verb]: to dislike / hate (takes 을/를) — e.g., "비 오는 날을 싫어해요." (*I dislike rainy days.*)
  - [ ] `음악 / 영화 / 독서` (eum-ak / yeonghwa / dokseo, Hanja: 音樂 / 映畵 / 讀書) [Noun]: music / movies / reading books — e.g., "주말에 영화를 봐요." (*I watch movies on the weekend.*)
  - [ ] `여행 / 운동 / 등산` (yeohaeng / undong / deungsan, Hanja: 旅行 / 運動 / 登山) [Noun]: travel / exercise (sports) / mountain hiking — e.g., "가을에 등산을 가요." (*I go hiking in autumn.*)

#### Module 11.1.3 — 문법 (Grammar Formulas & Rules)

- [ ] **Grammar Pattern: `Noun + 을/를 좋아하다 / 싫어하다`** (to like / to dislike [Noun])
  - Formula: `[Noun] + 을/를 + 좋아해요 / 싫어해요`
  - Explanation: Always pair 좋아하다 and 싫어하다 with the object particles 을/를 because they are active transitive verbs.
  - Rule: 커피를 좋아해요 (I like coffee)
  - Rule: 야채를 싫어해요 (I dislike vegetables)
  - Example: `저는 한국 영화를 정말 좋아해요.` — *I really like Korean movies.* [한국 영화 (Korean movies) + 를 (object) + 정말 (really) + 좋아해요]

#### Module 11.1.4 — 말하기·듣기 (Shadowing & Drills)

- [ ] **Shadowing & Repetition Drill:** State what hobbies and foods you like and dislike using 을/를 좋아해요/싫어해요.
  - [ ] Prompt: "State: I like listening to music." → Speak aloud: `저는 음악을 좋아해요.` (*I like music.*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "State: I dislike cold winter." → Speak aloud: `저는 추운 겨울을 싫어해요.` (*I dislike cold winter.*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "State: My hobby is reading books." → Speak aloud: `제 취미는 독서예요.` (*My hobby is reading.*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "Ask: What sports do you like?" → Speak aloud: `어떤 운동을 좋아해요?` (*What sports do you like?*)

#### Module 11.1.5 — 읽기·쓰기 (Reading & Production)

- [ ] **Reading Passage:** "어학당 학생들의 다양한 취미 (Varied Hobbies of Language Students)"
  - Text: `서울대 한국어 어학당 학생들은 취미가 다양합니다. 사라 씨는 한국 드라마 보기를 좋아합니다. 그래서 한국어를 빨리 배웁니다. 데이비드 씨는 사진 찍기를 좋아해서 주말마다 서울의 고궁을 방문합니다. 타쿠야 씨는 요리를 좋아해서 불고기와 김치전을 직접 만듭니다. 취미 활동은 한국 생활을 더욱 즐겁게 만듭니다.`
  - Translation: *SNU Korean language students have diverse hobbies. Sarah likes watching Korean dramas. Therefore, she learns Korean quickly. David likes taking photographs, so every weekend he visits Seoul's ancient palaces. Takuya likes cooking, so he makes bulgogi and kimchi pancakes himself. Hobby activities make life in Korea even more enjoyable.*
  - [ ] Check: What does David like doing on weekends? → Answer: `사진 찍기 / 고궁 방문 (Taking photos / visiting ancient palaces)` (*데이비드 씨는 사진 찍기를 좋아해서 주말마다 서울의 고궁을 방문합니다.*)
  - [ ] Check: What does Takuya make himself? → Answer: `불고기와 김치전 (Bulgogi and kimchi pancakes)` (*불고기와 김치전을 직접 만듭니다.*)
- [ ] **Guided Writing Task:** Write 5 sentences detailing your hobbies, what music or food you like, and what you dislike.
  - Sample Model Answer: `제 취미는 영화 감상과 요리예요. 저는 한국 가요를 정말 좋아해요. 주말에 자주 새로운 요리를 만들어요. 하지만 시끄러운 장소는 싫어해요. 취미 활동이 아주 재미있어요.`

### 11.2 — Expressing Wants and Weekend Desires (-고 싶어요와 하지만)

**Situation & Objective.** Formulate personal wishes and goals using Verb Stem + -고 싶어요 and contrast clauses with 하지만.

#### Module 11.2.1 — 대화 (Conversation First)

- [ ] **Dialogue Practice:** Listen to and practice the dialogue between Min-su (민수) and Sarah (사라) and Min-su (민수) and Sarah (사라) and Min-su (민수).
  - `사라 씨, 이번 방학에 뭐 하고 싶어요?` (Sarah, what do you want to do during this vacation?) — *Note: 뭐 하고 싶어요? = "What do you want to do?"*
  - `저는 제주도에 여행 가고 싶어요. 한라산도 보고 싶어요.` (I want to travel to Jeju Island. I also want to see Hallasan Mountain.) — *Note: 가고 싶어요 = 가다 + 고 싶어요; 보고 싶어요 = 보다 + 고 싶어요.*
  - `제주도는 정말 아름다워요! 맛있는 흑돼지도 꼭 드셔 보세요.` (Jeju Island is truly beautiful! Be sure to try delicious black pork too.) — *Note: 흑돼지 = Jeju black pork.*
  - `네, 먹고 싶어요! 하지만 비행기 표가 조금 비싸요.` (Yes, I want to eat it! However, airplane tickets are a little expensive.) — *Note: 하지만 = However / But.*
  - `미리 예약하면 할인이 돼요. 제가 도와줄게요.` (If you reserve in advance, you get a discount. I will help you.) — *Note: 예약 = reservation; 도와줄게요 = I will help.*
- [ ] **Cultural Etiquette & Pragmatics:** Jeju Island (제주도) is Korea's premier holiday island, renowned for Hallasan Volcano, mandarin orange groves (감귤), emerald beaches, and fresh seafood. When expressing your own desires in Korean, attach "-고 싶어요" directly to the verb stem (가고 싶어요). However, when asking someone else what they want, use the honorific question "-고 싶으세요?".

#### Module 11.2.2 — 어휘 (Vocabulary in Context)

- [ ] **Core Target Words:** Memorise and pronounce all target words for this lesson:
  - [ ] `-고 싶다 / -고 싶어요` (-go sipda / -go sipeoyo) [Expression]: want to [verb] (first-person desire) — e.g., "한국 친구를 많이 사귀고 싶어요." (*I want to make many Korean friends.*)
  - [ ] `하지만` (hajiman) [Particle]: however / but (contrasting sentence connector) — e.g., "피곤해요. 하지만 공부해야 해요." (*I am tired. But I must study.*)
  - [ ] `방학 / 휴가` (banghak / hyuga, Hanja: 放學 / 休暇) [Noun]: school vacation / workplace leave (vacation) — e.g., "여름 방학에 고향에 가요." (*I go to my hometown during summer vacation.*)
  - [ ] `여행하다 / 여행 가다` (yeohaenghada / yeohaeng gada, Hanja: 旅行) [Verb]: to travel / to go on a trip — e.g., "부산으로 기차 여행을 가고 싶어요." (*I want to go on a train trip to Busan.*)
  - [ ] `쉬다 / 쉬어요` (swida / swieoyo) [Verb]: to rest — e.g., "집에서 편안하게 쉬고 싶어요." (*I want to rest comfortably at home.*)

#### Module 11.2.3 — 문법 (Grammar Formulas & Rules)

- [ ] **Grammar Pattern: `Verb Stem + -고 싶어요`** (I want to [do verb])
  - Formula: `[Verb Stem (drop 다)] + 고 싶어요`
  - Explanation: Attach -고 싶어요 directly to any verb stem, regardless of whether it ends in a vowel or consonant. There are zero stem irregularities with this ending.
  - Rule: 가다 -> 가 + 고 싶어요 = 가고 싶어요 (I want to go)
  - Rule: 먹다 -> 먹 + 고 싶어요 = 먹고 싶어요 (I want to eat)
  - Rule: 배우다 -> 배우 + 고 싶어요 = 배우고 싶어요 (I want to learn)
  - Rule: 쉬다 -> 쉬 + 고 싶어요 = 쉬고 싶어요 (I want to rest)
  - Example: `한국어를 유창하게 말하고 싶어요.` — *I want to speak Korean fluently.* [말하 (speak) + 고 싶어요 (want to)]
  - Example: `오늘은 일찍 자고 싶어요.` — *I want to sleep early today.* [자 (sleep) + 고 싶어요]

#### Module 11.2.4 — 말하기·듣기 (Shadowing & Drills)

- [ ] **Shadowing & Repetition Drill:** Express what you want to do and connect a counter-fact with 하지만.
  - [ ] Prompt: "State: I want to eat Korean food." → Speak aloud: `한국 음식을 먹고 싶어요.` (*I want to eat Korean food.*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "State: I want to go to Busan." → Speak aloud: `부산에 가고 싶어요.` (*I want to go to Busan.*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "Connect: I want to buy shoes. But I don't have money." → Speak aloud: `신발을 사고 싶어요. 하지만 돈이 없어요.` (*I want to buy shoes. But I have no money.*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "State: I want to meet my family." → Speak aloud: `가족을 만나고 싶어요.` (*I want to meet my family.*)

#### Module 11.2.5 — 읽기·쓰기 (Reading & Production)

- [ ] **Reading Passage:** "나의 소원 버킷리스트 (My Wish Bucket List)"
  - Text: `저는 한국에 머무는 동안 하고 싶은 일이 세 가지 있습니다. 첫째, 제주도에서 일출을 보고 싶습니다. 둘째, 서울 광장시장에서 맛있는 빈대떡을 먹고 싶습니다. 셋째, K-POP 콘서트에 직접 가서 응원하고 싶습니다. 시험이 끝나면 이 꿈들을 모두 이루고 싶습니다.`
  - Translation: *While staying in Korea, there are three things I want to do. First, I want to watch the sunrise in Jeju Island. Second, I want to eat delicious mung bean pancakes at Gwangjang Market in Seoul. Third, I want to go to a K-POP concert in person and cheer. When exams end, I want to fulfill all of these dreams.*
  - [ ] Check: What does the writer want to do at Gwangjang Market? → Answer: `맛있는 빈대떡 먹기 (Eat delicious mung bean pancakes)` (*서울 광장시장에서 맛있는 빈대떡을 먹고 싶습니다.*)
  - [ ] Check: Where does the writer want to see the sunrise? → Answer: `제주도 (Jeju Island)` (*제주도에서 일출을 보고 싶습니다.*)
- [ ] **Guided Writing Task:** Write a 6-sentence paragraph describing 3 things you want to do in Korea or during your next vacation using -고 싶어요 and 하지만.
  - Sample Model Answer: `저는 다음 여름 방학에 한국 여행을 가고 싶어요. 그리고 서울 남산타워에 올라가고 싶어요. 그곳에서 맛있는 한정식을 꼭 먹고 싶어요. 하지만 비행기 표가 조금 비싸요. 그래도 돈을 모아서 꼭 가고 싶어요. 정말 기대돼요.`


## Unit 12 — Past Events and Experiences (과거 사건과 경험)

**Part.** Part C — Talk About Your Life (Units 9–13 & Checkpoint C)
**Objective.** Formulate accurate past tense sentences using 았어요/었어요/했어요, discuss yesterday's activities and weekend outings, and sequence events logically.

### 12.1 — Talking About Yesterday (어제 뭐 했어요?: 았/었/했어요)

**Situation & Objective.** Master past tense conjugation for Korean verbs and adjectives using 았어요, 었어요, and 했어요 with past time words (어제, 지난주).

#### Module 12.1.1 — 대화 (Conversation First)

- [ ] **Dialogue Practice:** Listen to and practice the dialogue between Min-su (민수) and Sarah (사라) and Min-su (민수) and Sarah (사라) and Min-su (민수).
  - `사라 씨, 어제 뭐 했어요? 연락이 안 됐어요.` (Sarah, what did you do yesterday? I couldn't get in touch.) — *Note: 어제 뭐 했어요? = "What did you do yesterday?"*
  - `어제 감기에 걸렸어요. 그래서 하루 종일 집에서 쉬었어요.` (I caught a cold yesterday. So I rested at home all day long.) — *Note: 걸렸어요 = past of 걸리다; 쉬었어요 = past of 쉬다.*
  - `아이구, 정말요? 병원에 갔어요? 약은 먹었어요?` (Oh dear, really? Did you go to the hospital? Did you take medicine?) — *Note: 갔어요 = past of 가다; 먹었어요 = past of 먹다.*
  - `네, 약국에서 약을 샀어요. 그리고 따뜻한 꿀차를 마셨어요. 지금은 많이 좋아졌어요.` (Yes, I bought medicine at the pharmacy. And I drank warm honey tea. I am much better now.) — *Note: 샀어요 = past of 사다; 마셨어요 = past of 마시다.*
  - `정말 다행이에요! 무리하지 마세요.` (That is such a relief! Please don't overwork yourself.) — *Note: 다행이에요 = "It is a relief".*
- [ ] **Cultural Etiquette & Pragmatics:** In Korea, asking about past physical well-being ("밥 먹었어요?", "약 먹었어요?", "어제 잘 쉬었어요?") is an expression of genuine warmth and social bonding. When conjugating the past tense, Korean stems follow the same vowel harmony as the present tense: bright vowels (ㅏ, ㅗ) take -았어요, all other vowels take -었어요, and -하다 verbs become -했어요.

#### Module 12.1.2 — 어휘 (Vocabulary in Context)

- [ ] **Core Target Words:** Memorise and pronounce all target words for this lesson:
  - [ ] `어제 / 그저께` (eoje / geujeokke) [Noun]: yesterday / the day before yesterday — e.g., "어제 친구를 만났어요." (*I met a friend yesterday.*)
  - [ ] `지난주 / 지난달 / 작년` (jinanju / jinandal / jangnyeon, Hanja: ── / ── / 昨年) [Noun]: last week / last month / last year — e.g., "작년에 한국에 왔어요." (*I came to Korea last year.*)
  - [ ] `사다 / 샀어요` (sada / sasseoyo) [Verb]: to buy -> bought — e.g., "백화점에서 신발을 샀어요." (*I bought shoes at the department store.*)
  - [ ] `가다 / 갔어요` (gada / gasseoyo) [Verb]: to go -> went — e.g., "어제 도서관에 갔어요." (*I went to the library yesterday.*)
  - [ ] `보다 / 봤어요` (boda / bwasseoyo) [Verb]: to see/watch -> saw/watched — e.g., "극장에서 한국 영화를 봤어요." (*I watched a Korean movie at the theater.*)

#### Module 12.1.3 — 문법 (Grammar Formulas & Rules)

- [ ] **Grammar Pattern: `Verb/Adj Stem + -았어요 / -었어요 / -했어요 (Past Polite Tense)`** (did / was / were)
  - Formula: `Stem vowel ㅏ, ㅗ -> + 았어요 | Other vowels -> + 었어요 | 하다 -> 했어요`
  - Explanation: Past tense adds the infix -았/었- before the polite sentence ending -어요.
  - Rule: 1. Bright Vowel (ㅏ, ㅗ): + 았어요    - 가다 -> 가 + 았어요 = 갔어요    - 보다 -> 보 + 았어요 = 봤어요    - 살다 -> 살 + 았어요 = 살았어요
  - Rule: 2. Dark/Neutral Vowels: + 었어요    - 먹다 -> 먹 + 었어요 = 먹었어요    - 마시다 -> 마시 + 었어요 = 마셨어요    - 읽다 -> 읽 + 었어요 = 읽었어요
  - Rule: 3. -하다 -> 했어요    - 공부하다 -> 공부했어요    - 운동하다 -> 운동했어요    - 쇼핑하다 -> 쇼핑했어요
  - Example: `어제 한국어 숙제를 다 했어요.` — *I did all my Korean homework yesterday.* [하 (do) + 였어요/했어요 (did)]
  - Example: `점심에 비빔밥을 맛있게 먹었어요.` — *I ate bibimbap deliciously for lunch.* [먹 (eat) + 었어요 (ate)]

#### Module 12.1.4 — 말하기·듣기 (Shadowing & Drills)

- [ ] **Shadowing & Repetition Drill:** Conjugate each verb into the past tense and state the action.
  - [ ] Prompt: "Conjugate: 먹다 (to eat) -> [I ate pizza yesterday]." → Speak aloud: `어제 피자를 먹었어요.` (*I ate pizza yesterday.*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "Conjugate: 가다 (to go) -> [I went to school last week]." → Speak aloud: `지난주에 학교에 갔어요.` (*I went to school last week.*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "Conjugate: 공부하다 (to study) -> [I studied Korean for 2 hours]." → Speak aloud: `한국어를 두 시간 공부했어요.` (*I studied Korean for two hours.*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "Ask: Did you watch the movie yesterday?" → Speak aloud: `어제 영화 봤어요?` (*Did you watch the movie yesterday?*)

#### Module 12.1.5 — 읽기·쓰기 (Reading & Production)

- [ ] **Reading Passage:** "어제 나의 하루 (Yesterday in My Life)"
  - Text: `어제는 참 바쁜 하루였습니다. 아침 여덟 시에 일어났습니다. 커피를 한 잔 마시고 바로 대학교에 갔습니다. 오전에는 전공 강의를 들었습니다. 점심에는 학생식당에서 친구들과 라면을 먹었습니다. 오후에는 도서관에서 세 시간 동안 한국어 시험공부를 했습니다. 저녁 여덟 시에 집에 돌아와서 따뜻한 물로 샤워를 하고 푹 잤습니다.`
  - Translation: *Yesterday was a truly busy day. I woke up at 8 AM. I drank a cup of coffee and went straight to university. In the morning, I listened to my major lectures. At lunch, I ate ramen with my friends at the student cafeteria. In the afternoon, I studied for my Korean exam for three hours at the library. At 8 PM, I returned home, took a warm shower, and slept soundly.*
  - [ ] Check: What did the writer eat for lunch yesterday? → Answer: `라면 (Ramen)` (*학생식당에서 친구들과 라면을 먹었습니다.*)
  - [ ] Check: How long did the writer study at the library? → Answer: `3시간 (Three hours)` (*도서관에서 세 시간 동안 한국어 시험공부를 했습니다.*)
- [ ] **Guided Writing Task:** Write 6 past-tense sentences recounting your activities yesterday from morning to evening.
  - Sample Model Answer: `어제 저는 아침 일곱 시 반에 일어났어요. 아침에 사과와 토스트를 먹었어요. 낮에 한국어 어학당에 갔어요. 교실에서 선생님과 말하기 연습을 했어요. 저녁에 친구와 함께 삼겹살을 먹었어요. 밤 열한 시에 집에 와서 일찍 잤어요.`

### 12.2 — Describing a Weekend Outing (주말 나들이와 과거 회상)

**Situation & Objective.** Narrate a weekend trip or excursion sequencing places visited, activities done, and impressions using past tense adjectives (좋았어요, 재미있었어요, 맛있었어요).

#### Module 12.2.1 — 대화 (Conversation First)

- [ ] **Dialogue Practice:** Listen to and practice the dialogue between David (데이비드) and Min-su (민수) and David (데이비드) and Min-su (민수) and David (데이비드) and Min-su (민수).
  - `민수 씨, 지난 주말에 뭐 했어요?` (Min-su, what did you do last weekend?) — *Note: 지난 주말에 = last weekend.*
  - `친구들과 함께 경복궁에 갔어요. 한복도 입었어요.` (I went to Gyeongbokgung Palace with my friends. We wore Hanbok too.) — *Note: 입었어요 = past of 입다 (to wear); 한복 = traditional Korean clothes.*
  - `와, 정말 멋있었겠네요! 사진도 많이 찍었어요?` (Wow, that must have been so cool! Did you take many photos too?) — *Note: 찍었어요 = past of 찍다 (to take a photo).*
  - `네, 궁궐 앞에서 멋진 사진을 많이 찍었어요. 그리고 인사동에서 전통차를 마셨어요.` (Yes, we took lots of great photos in front of the palace. And we drank traditional tea in Insadong.) — *Note: 인사동 = Insadong cultural district; 전통차 = traditional tea.*
  - `음식은 어땠어요?` (How was the food?) — *Note: 어땠어요? = Past of 어때요? ("How was it?").*
  - `비빔밥과 해물파전을 먹었는데 아주 맛있었어요!` (We ate bibimbap and seafood scallion pancake, and it was extremely delicious!) — *Note: 맛있었어요 = past of 맛있다.*
- [ ] **Cultural Etiquette & Pragmatics:** Gyeongbokgung Palace (경복궁) in northern Seoul is the primary royal palace of the Joseon Dynasty. Visitors who wear Hanbok (한복) receive completely free admission to the palace grounds. Directly neighboring Gyeongbokgung is Insadong (인사동), famous for traditional teahouses, art galleries, and handcrafted souvenirs.

#### Module 12.2.2 — 어휘 (Vocabulary in Context)

- [ ] **Core Target Words:** Memorise and pronounce all target words for this lesson:
  - [ ] `경복궁 / 인사동` (gyeongbokgung / insadong) [Noun]: Gyeongbokgung Palace / Insadong Cultural District — e.g., "경복궁에서 한복을 입었어요." (*I wore Hanbok at Gyeongbokgung.*)
  - [ ] `사진을 찍다 / 찍었어요` (sajineul jjikda / jjigeosseoyo) [Verb]: to take a photo -> took a photo — e.g., "친구들과 추억 사진을 찍었어요." (*I took memory photos with my friends.*)
  - [ ] `입다 / 입었어요` (ipda / ibeosseoyo) [Verb]: to wear (clothes) -> wore — e.g., "예쁜 옷을 입었어요." (*I wore pretty clothes.*)
  - [ ] `재미있었다 / 재미있었어요` (jaemiisseotda / jaemiisseosseoyo) [Adjective]: was interesting / was fun — e.g., "어제 본 연극이 정말 재미있었어요." (*The play I watched yesterday was really fun.*)
  - [ ] `맛있었다 / 맛있었어요` (masisseotda / masisseosseoyo) [Adjective]: was delicious — e.g., "어제 저녁 불고기가 아주 맛있었어요." (*The bulgogi last night was very delicious.*)

#### Module 12.2.3 — 문법 (Grammar Formulas & Rules)

- [ ] **Grammar Pattern: `Adjective Past: [좋았다 -> 좋았어요 / 맛있었다 -> 맛있었어요]`** (was good / was delicious / was fun)
  - Formula: `[Adjective Stem] + 았/었어요`
  - Explanation: Just like action verbs, Korean descriptive adjectives conjugate into the past tense to describe past states or feelings.
  - Rule: 좋다 -> 좋 + 았어요 = 좋았어요 (It was good)
  - Rule: 맛있다 -> 맛있 + 었어요 = 맛있었어요 (It was delicious)
  - Rule: 바쁘다 -> 바빴어요 (I was busy)
  - Rule: 어렵다 -> 어려웠어요 (It was difficult - ㅂ irregular)
  - Example: `어제 날씨가 정말 좋았어요.` — *The weather was really nice yesterday.* [날씨 (weather) + 가 + 좋았어요 (was good)]

#### Module 12.2.4 — 말하기·듣기 (Shadowing & Drills)

- [ ] **Shadowing & Repetition Drill:** Narrate what you did on your outing and describe how it was.
  - [ ] Prompt: "State: I went to Gyeongbokgung Palace last Saturday." → Speak aloud: `지난 토요일에 경복궁에 갔어요.` (*I went to Gyeongbokgung Palace last Saturday.*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "State: I took lots of photos with my friend." → Speak aloud: `친구와 함께 사진을 많이 찍었어요.` (*I took many photos with my friend.*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "Describe: The food was really delicious." → Speak aloud: `음식이 정말 맛있었어요.` (*The food was really delicious.*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "State: The weekend outing was truly fun." → Speak aloud: `주말 나들이가 참 재미있었어요.` (*The weekend outing was truly fun.*)

#### Module 12.2.5 — 읽기·쓰기 (Reading & Production)

- [ ] **Reading Passage:** "부산 1박 2일 여행 일기 (Busan 2-Day Trip Diary)"
  - Text: `지난 주말에 친구들과 KTX 기차를 타고 부산에 다녀왔습니다. 부산역에 도착해서 먼저 유명한 돼지국밥을 먹었습니다. 국물이 정말 진하고 맛있었습니다. 오후에는 해운대 해수욕장에 갔습니다. 푸른 바다가 아주 아름다웠습니다. 저녁에는 자갈치 시장에서 싱싱한 생선회를 먹었습니다. 밤에는 광안리 해변에서 화려한 드론 쇼를 보았습니다. 정말 잊을 수 없는 행복한 여행이었습니다.`
  - Translation: *Last weekend, I visited Busan by KTX train with my friends. Upon arriving at Busan Station, we first ate famous pork soup with rice (Dwaeji Gukbap). The broth was really rich and delicious. In the afternoon, we went to Haeundae Beach. The blue sea was very beautiful. In the evening, we ate fresh raw fish at Jagalchi Market. At night, we watched a spectacular drone show at Gwangalli Beach. It was truly an unforgettable, happy trip.*
  - [ ] Check: What did they eat upon arriving at Busan Station? → Answer: `돼지국밥 (Pork soup with rice)` (*유명한 돼지국밥을 먹었습니다.*)
  - [ ] Check: What did they watch at Gwangalli Beach at night? → Answer: `화려한 드론 쇼 (Spectacular drone show)` (*광안리 해변에서 화려한 드론 쇼를 보았습니다.*)
- [ ] **Guided Writing Task:** Write a 6-sentence diary entry about a memorable weekend outing, describing where you went, who you were with, what you ate, and how the experience was.
  - Sample Model Answer: `지난 주말에 친구와 함께 한강공원에 갔어요. 날씨가 아주 따뜻하고 좋았어요. 그곳에서 자전거를 타고 산책을 했어요. 그리고 잔디밭에서 한강 라면과 치킨을 맛있게 먹었어요. 강변 경치가 정말 아름다웠어요. 참 행복한 주말이었어요.`


## Unit 13 — Weather and Clothing (날씨와 옷차림)

**Part.** Part C — Talk About Your Life (Units 9–13 & Checkpoint C)
**Objective.** Describe daily weather and seasons using descriptive adjectives (덥다, 춥다, 맑다, 비가 오다), explain clothing choices, and give simple reasons.

### 13.1 — Discussing Today's Weather and Seasons (오늘 날씨와 사계절)

**Situation & Objective.** Describe weather conditions using 덥다 (hot), 춥다 (cold), 맑다 (clear), and 비가 와요 (it rains), and identify Korea's four seasons.

#### Module 13.1.1 — 대화 (Conversation First)

- [ ] **Dialogue Practice:** Listen to and practice the dialogue between Sarah (사라) and Min-su (민수) and Sarah (사라) and Min-su (민수) and Sarah (사라).
  - `민수 씨, 오늘 날씨가 어때요?` (Min-su, how is the weather today?) — *Note: 날씨가 어때요? = "How is the weather?"*
  - `오늘은 하늘이 아주 맑고 따뜻해요.` (Today the sky is very clear and warm.) — *Note: 맑고 = clear and (맑다 + 고); 따뜻해요 = warm.*
  - `내일도 날씨가 좋아요?` (Is the weather good tomorrow too?) — *Note: 내일도 = tomorrow too.*
  - `일기예보에서 내일은 비가 온다고 했어요. 조금 쌀쌀할 거예요.` (The weather forecast said it will rain tomorrow. It will be a bit chilly.) — *Note: 비가 와요 / 비가 온다 = rain falls; 쌀쌀해요 = chilly.*
  - `그러면 내일 우산을 꼭 챙겨야겠네요.` (In that case, I will certainly have to take an umbrella tomorrow.) — *Note: 우산 = umbrella; 챙겨야겠네요 = "must pack/bring".*
- [ ] **Cultural Etiquette & Pragmatics:** Korea has four distinct, dramatic seasons: 봄 (Spring - cherry blossoms & mild breezes), 여름 (Summer - monsoon 장마 and tropical heat), 가을 (Autumn - crisp clear skies and vibrant foliage 단풍), and 겨울 (Winter - sub-zero dry cold with snow). Weather check greetings ("오늘 날씨가 참 좋지요?", "오늘 꽤 춥죠?") are classic conversation starters.

#### Module 13.1.2 — 어휘 (Vocabulary in Context)

- [ ] **Core Target Words:** Memorise and pronounce all target words for this lesson:
  - [ ] `날씨` (nalssi) [Noun]: weather — e.g., "오늘 날씨가 정말 화창해요." (*Today's weather is really sunny.*)
  - [ ] `봄 / 여름 / 가을 / 겨울` (bom / yeoreum / gaeul / gyeoul) [Noun]: spring / summer / autumn / winter — e.g., "저는 시원한 가을을 제일 좋아해요." (*I like cool autumn the best.*)
  - [ ] `더워요 / 추워요` (deowoyo / chuwoyo) [Adjective]: hot (덥다) / cold (춥다) [ㅂ irregular] — e.g., "여름에는 덥고 겨울에는 추워요." (*It is hot in summer and cold in winter.*)
  - [ ] `따뜻해요 / 시원해요` (ttatteuthaeyo / siwonhaeyo) [Adjective]: warm (따뜻하다) / cool & refreshing (시원하다) — e.g., "봄바람이 참 따뜻해요." (*The spring breeze is truly warm.*)
  - [ ] `비가 오다 / 눈이 오다` (biga oda / nuni oda) [Verb]: to rain (rain comes) / to snow (snow comes) — e.g., "지금 밖에는 하얀 눈이 와요." (*White snow is falling outside right now.*)

#### Module 13.1.3 — 문법 (Grammar Formulas & Rules)

- [ ] **Grammar Pattern: `ㅂ-Irregular Adjectives (덥다 -> 더워요, 춥다 -> 추워요)`** (Conjugating ㅂ-drop adjectives)
  - Formula: `Stem ending in ㅂ + vowel ending -> ㅂ changes to 우 + 어요 = 워요`
  - Explanation: Most descriptive adjectives ending in the 받침 ㅂ change ㅂ to 우 when meeting a vowel-initial ending like -아요/어요.
  - Rule: 덥다: 덥 -> 더 + 우 + 어요 = 더워요 (It is hot)
  - Rule: 춥다: 춥 -> 추 + 우 + 어요 = 추워요 (It is cold)
  - Rule: 어렵다: 어렵 -> 어려 + 우 + 어요 = 어려워요 (It is difficult)
  - Rule: 쉬운: 쉽다 -> 쉬워요 (It is easy)
  - Example: `한국의 겨울은 바람이 많이 불어서 아주 추워요.` — *Korea's winter is very cold because the wind blows heavily.* [춥 (cold) -> 추워요]

#### Module 13.1.4 — 말하기·듣기 (Shadowing & Drills)

- [ ] **Shadowing & Repetition Drill:** Answer questions about the weather using ㅂ-irregular forms and weather expressions.
  - [ ] Prompt: "State: Today is very hot." → Speak aloud: `오늘은 너무 더워요.` (*Today is too hot.*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "State: Today is cold and windy." → Speak aloud: `오늘은 춥고 바람이 불어요.` (*Today is cold and windy.*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "State: Rain is falling right now." → Speak aloud: `지금 비가 와요.` (*It is raining right now.*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "State: In spring the weather is warm." → Speak aloud: `봄에는 날씨가 따뜻해요.` (*In spring the weather is warm.*)

#### Module 13.1.5 — 읽기·쓰기 (Reading & Production)

- [ ] **Reading Passage:** "한국의 사계절 매력 (The Charm of Korea's Four Seasons)"
  - Text: `한국은 사계절이 뚜렷합니다. 3월부터 5월까지는 봄입니다. 봄에는 예쁜 벚꽃이 피고 날씨가 따뜻합니다. 6월부터 8월까지는 여름입니다. 여름은 덥고 비가 자주 옵니다. 9월부터 11월까지는 가을입니다. 하늘이 높고 푸르며 단풍이 아름답습니다. 12월부터 2월까지는 겨울입니다. 겨울은 매우 춥고 하얀 눈이 내립니다. 계절마다 색다른 매력이 있습니다.`
  - Translation: *Korea has four distinct seasons. From March to May is spring. In spring, pretty cherry blossoms bloom and the weather is warm. From June to August is summer. Summer is hot and it rains often. From September to November is autumn. The sky is high and blue, and the autumn leaves are beautiful. From December to February is winter. Winter is very cold and white snow falls. Each season has a unique charm.*
  - [ ] Check: Which months are autumn in Korea? → Answer: `9월부터 11월 (September to November)` (*9월부터 11월까지는 가을입니다.*)
