  - [ ] Check: What is the summer weather like in Korea? → Answer: `덥고 비가 자주 와요 (Hot and rains often)` (*여름은 덥고 비가 자주 옵니다.*)
- [ ] **Guided Writing Task:** Write 5 sentences describing what the weather is like in your hometown across different seasons and state your favorite season.
  - Sample Model Answer: `제 고향은 밴쿠버예요. 여름에는 날씨가 아주 맑고 시원해요. 겨울에는 눈과 비가 자주 와요. 제가 제일 좋아하는 계절은 가을이에요. 단풍이 예쁘고 산책하기에 날씨가 참 좋아요.`

### 13.2 — Choosing Clothes and Colors (옷차림과 색깔)

**Situation & Objective.** Identify garments (코트, 바지, 치마, 모자), match colors (흰색, 검은색, 빨간색, 파란색), and justify clothing choices based on weather.

#### Module 13.2.1 — 대화 (Conversation First)

- [ ] **Dialogue Practice:** Listen to and practice the dialogue between Sarah (사라) and Min-su (민수) and Sarah (사라) and Min-su (민수) and Sarah (사라).
  - `민수 씨, 오늘 뭐 입을 거예요?` (Min-su, what are you going to wear today?) — *Note: 뭐 입을 거예요? = "What are you going to wear?"*
  - `오늘 날씨가 꽤 쌀쌀해요. 그래서 두꺼운 코트를 입을 거예요.` (Today the weather is quite chilly. So I am going to wear a thick coat.) — *Note: 두꺼운 = thick; 코트 = coat; 입을 거예요 = future intention.*
  - `저도 따뜻한 파란색 니트와 목도리를 할 거예요.` (I will wear a warm blue knit sweater and scarf too.) — *Note: 목도리를 하다 = wear/do a scarf (uses verb 하다).*
  - `파란색이 사라 씨한테 아주 잘 어울려요.` (Blue suits you very well, Sarah.) — *Note: 잘 어울려요 = "suits / matches well".*
  - `고마워요! 감기 조심하세요.` (Thank you! Be careful not to catch a cold.) — *Note: 감기 조심하세요 = Standard warm winter parting wish.*
- [ ] **Cultural Etiquette & Pragmatics:** In Korean, the verb for "to wear" changes depending on the item and where it is worn on the body: Clothes (shirts, pants, coats) take "입다"; Footwear (shoes, socks) takes "신다"; Headgear (hats, caps) takes "쓰다"; Accessories like scarves or neckties take "하다" or "매다"; Gloves take "끼다"; Watches take "차다".

#### Module 13.2.2 — 어휘 (Vocabulary in Context)

- [ ] **Core Target Words:** Memorise and pronounce all target words for this lesson:
  - [ ] `코트 / 패딩 / 니트` (koteu / paeding / niteu) [Noun]: coat / padded down puffer jacket (padding) / knit sweater — e.g., "겨울에는 롱패딩을 입어요." (*In winter, I wear a long padded puffer jacket.*)
  - [ ] `바지 / 치마 / 셔츠` (baji / chima / syeocheu) [Noun]: trousers / skirt / shirt — e.g., "편한 청바지를 입어요." (*I wear comfortable jeans.*)
  - [ ] `신다 / 쓰다 / 입다` (sinda / sseuda / ipda) [Verb]: wear (shoes: 신다 / hat, glasses: 쓰다 / clothes: 입다) — e.g., "운동화를 신어요. 모자를 써요. 옷을 입어요." (*Put on sneakers. Put on a hat. Put on clothes.*)
  - [ ] `흰색 / 검은색 / 파란색 / 빨간색` (heinsaek / geomeunsaek / paransaek / ppalgansaek) [Noun]: white / black / blue / red — e.g., "저는 검은색 옷을 자주 입어요." (*I frequently wear black clothes.*)
  - [ ] `목도리 / 장갑` (mokdori / jang-gap) [Noun]: muffler (scarf) / gloves — e.g., "추워서 목도리를 했어요." (*It was cold so I put on a muffler.*)

#### Module 13.2.3 — 문법 (Grammar Formulas & Rules)

- [ ] **Grammar Pattern: `Body-part specific wearing verbs (입다, 신다, 쓰다, 하다)`** (to wear / put on)
  - Formula: `Clothes + 입어요 | Shoes/Socks + 신어요 | Hat/Glasses + 써요 | Scarf + 해요`
  - Explanation: Match the Korean verb to the clothing classification.
  - Rule: 상의/하의 (Tops & bottoms): 티셔츠를 입어요, 바지를 입어요.
  - Rule: 신발 (Footwear): 운동화를 신어요, 구두를 신어요.
  - Rule: 머리/얼굴 (Head & face): 모자를 써요, 안경을 써요.
  - Rule: 소품 (Accessories): 목도리를 해요, 넥타이를 매요.
  - Example: `비가 와서 장화를 신었어요.` — *It was raining so I put on rain boots.* [장화 (rain boots) + 를 + 신었어요 (wore footwear)]

#### Module 13.2.4 — 말하기·듣기 (Shadowing & Drills)

- [ ] **Shadowing & Repetition Drill:** Select the precise verb for each piece of clothing.
  - [ ] Prompt: "Say: I wear a warm coat. (use 입다)" → Speak aloud: `따뜻한 코트를 입어요.` (*I wear a warm coat.*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "Say: I put on sneakers. (use 신다)" → Speak aloud: `운동화를 신어요.` (*I put on sneakers.*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "Say: I put on a black hat. (use 쓰다)" → Speak aloud: `검은색 모자를 써요.` (*I put on a black hat.*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "Say: I put on a warm muffler. (use 하다)" → Speak aloud: `따뜻한 목도리를 해요.` (*I put on a warm muffler.*)

#### Module 13.2.5 — 읽기·쓰기 (Reading & Production)

- [ ] **Reading Passage:** "오늘의 패션 스타일 (Today's Fashion Style)"
  - Text: `오늘 서울의 아침 기온은 3도로 아주 쌀쌀합니다. 그래서 사람들의 옷차림이 두꺼워졌습니다. 저는 오늘 포근한 아이보리색 니트를 입었습니다. 그리고 편안한 검은색 슬랙스 바지를 입었습니다. 밖으로 나갈 때는 긴 베이지색 트렌치코트를 입고 갈색 가죽 구두를 신었습니다. 목에는 체크무늬 목도리를 둘렀습니다. 따뜻하면서도 아주 세련된 옷차림입니다.`
  - Translation: *Today Seoul's morning temperature is 3 degrees, which is very chilly. Therefore, people's clothing has become thicker. Today I wore a cozy ivory knit sweater. And I wore comfortable black slacks. When going outside, I put on a long beige trench coat and wore brown leather dress shoes. Around my neck, I wrapped a checkered scarf. It is a warm yet very stylish outfit.*
  - [ ] Check: What kind of shoes did the writer wear? → Answer: `갈색 가죽 구두 (Brown leather dress shoes)` (*갈색 가죽 구두를 신었습니다.*)
  - [ ] Check: What color knit sweater did the writer wear? → Answer: `아이보리색 (Ivory color)` (*포근한 아이보리색 니트를 입었습니다.*)
- [ ] **Guided Writing Task:** Write 5-6 sentences describing what you are wearing today based on the weather, specifying the item, color, and corresponding wearing verb.
  - Sample Model Answer: `오늘 날씨는 조금 쌀쌀해요. 그래서 저는 흰색 셔츠와 파란색 스웨터를 입었어요. 그리고 편안한 검은색 바지를 입었어요. 신발은 하얀 운동화를 신었어요. 햇빛이 강해서 선글라스도 썼어요. 아주 편안한 옷차림이에요.`


## Checkpoint C — Checkpoint C: Schedules, Hobbies, Past Outings & Weather

**Prerequisites.** Completion of Unit 9, Unit 10, Unit 11, Unit 12, and Unit 13.
**Target Skills:**
- [ ] Tell clock time accurately (Native hours + Sino-Korean minutes) and state schedules with time particle 에.
- [ ] Read calendar dates with 년, 월, 일 and arrange meetings on specific days of the week (요일).
- [ ] Express personal hobbies with 을/를 좋아하다/싫어하다 and desires with -고 싶어요.
- [ ] Recount past events and trips using past tense verb/adjective forms (았어요/었어요/했어요).
- [ ] Describe weather conditions (덥다, 춥다, 비가 오다) and explain clothing choices.

### Spoken Simulation
- **Scenario:** Your classmate asks what you did last weekend, what the weather was like, and what you want to do next weekend.
- **Prompt:** Explain where you went on Saturday, what you ate, how the weather was, and express your desire for next weekend.
- [ ] Deliver unprompted: `지난 토요일에 친구와 함께 한강공원에 갔어요. 날씨가 아주 맑고 따뜻했어요. 공원에서 자전거를 타고 치킨을 맛있게 먹었어요. 정말 재미있었어요! 다음 주말에는 제주도에 여행 가고 싶어요.` (*Last Saturday, I went to Hangang Park with a friend. The weather was very clear and warm. We rode bicycles in the park and ate chicken deliciously. It was really fun! Next weekend, I want to travel to Jeju Island.*)

### Written Milestone Assignment
- **Prompt:** Write an 8-sentence diary entry about a memorable day in Korea, including the date, weather, past activities, food eaten, and evening feelings. (80–100 Korean words)
- [ ] Submit written draft: `2026년 10월 24일 토요일. 오늘 날씨는 하늘이 푸르고 아주 화창했습니다. 저는 아침 아홉 시 반에 일어났습니다. 친구와 함께 경복궁에 갔습니다. 경복궁에서 예쁜 한복을 입고 사진을 많이 찍었습니다. 점심에는 인사동에서 따뜻한 비빔밥을 맛있게 먹었습니다. 음식도 맛있었고 궁궐 경치도 정말 아름다웠습니다. 오늘 하루는 참 행복하고 잊을 수 없는 날이었습니다.`

## Unit 14 — Transport and Going Out (교통과 길 찾기)

**Part.** Part D — Connect Your Korean (Units 14–16 & Checkpoint D)
**Objective.** Navigate Korean public transit, ask how to get to landmarks using 어떻게 가요, and use instrument/direction marker -(으)로 with transit verbs (타다, 내리다, 갈아타다).

### 14.1 — Asking How to Get Somewhere (어떻게 가요? 와 대중교통)

**Situation & Objective.** Inquire about transit routes with 어떻게 and specify modes of transport (지하철, 버스, 택시) using -(으)로.

#### Module 14.1.1 — 대화 (Conversation First)

- [ ] **Dialogue Practice:** Listen to and practice the dialogue between Sarah (사라) and Seoul Resident (시민) and Sarah (사라) and Seoul Resident (시민) and Sarah (사라).
  - `실례합니다, 서울역까지 어떻게 가요?` (Excuse me, how do I get to Seoul Station?) — *Note: 어떻게 가요? = "How do I get there?"; 까지 = "until / up to".*
  - `여기서 지하철 4호선으로 가세요. 가장 빨라요.` (Go by subway Line 4 from here. It is the fastest.) — *Note: 4호선으로 = by Line 4 (consonant + 으로); 가장 빨라요 = is the fastest.*
  - `시간이 얼마나 걸려요?` (How long does it take?) — *Note: 얼마나 걸려요? = "How long does it take?" (걸리다 = to take time).*
  - `이십 분 정도 걸려요. 환승 안 해도 돼요.` (It takes about twenty minutes. You don't even need to transfer.) — *Note: 정도 = "about / approximately"; 환승 = transit transfer.*
  - `친절하게 알려 주셔서 정말 감사합니다!` (Thank you very much for kindly letting me know!) — *Note: 알려 주셔서 감사합니다 = Polite gratitude for directions.*
- [ ] **Cultural Etiquette & Pragmatics:** Seoul possesses one of the world's most punctual and comprehensive metropolitan transit systems (수도권 전철). Lines are designated by numbers and distinct colors (Line 1: Dark Blue, Line 2: Green Circle Line, Line 4: Sky Blue, etc.). Transferring (환승) between subway lines or buses within 30 minutes using a T-money card (교통카드) is free or heavily discounted.

#### Module 14.1.2 — 어휘 (Vocabulary in Context)

- [ ] **Core Target Words:** Memorise and pronounce all target words for this lesson:
  - [ ] `지하철 / 전철` (jihacheol / jeoncheol, Hanja: 地下鐵 / 電鐵) [Noun]: subway / metro train — e.g., "지하철 2호선을 타요." (*I take subway Line 2.*)
  - [ ] `시내버스 / 고속버스` (sinaebeoseu / gosokbeoseu) [Noun]: city bus / express highway bus — e.g., "정류장에서 시내버스를 기다려요." (*I wait for the city bus at the bus stop.*)
  - [ ] `택시 / 기차 (KTX)` (taeksi / gicha) [Noun]: taxi / bullet train (KTX) — e.g., "짐이 많아서 택시를 탔어요." (*I had lots of luggage, so I took a taxi.*)
  - [ ] `어떻게` (eotteoke) [Noun]: how (method / way) — e.g., "명동에 어떻게 가요?" (*How do I get to Myeongdong?*)
  - [ ] `얼마나 걸리다` (eolmana geollida) [Verb]: how long does it take (time) — e.g., "집에서 학교까지 삼십 분 걸려요." (*It takes thirty minutes from home to school.*)
  - [ ] `타다 / 내리다 / 갈아타다` (tada / naerida / garatada) [Verb]: to ride/take / to get off / to transfer — e.g., "시청역에서 1호선으로 갈아타세요." (*Please transfer to Line 1 at City Hall Station.*)

#### Module 14.1.3 — 문법 (Grammar Formulas & Rules)

- [ ] **Grammar Pattern: `Noun (Transport Method) + (으)로`** (by means of / by transport)
  - Formula: `Consonant (except ㄹ) + 으로 | Vowel or ㄹ + 로`
  - Explanation: Attach -(으)로 to vehicles and instruments to express the method of traveling.
  - Rule: Ends in consonant: 지하철 + 으로 = 지하철로 (note: ㄹ ends take 로 -> 지하철로)
  - Rule: Ends in vowel: 버스 + 로 = 버스로, 택시 + 로 = 택시로, 비행기 + 로 = 비행기로
  - Rule: By foot: 걸어서 가요 (special expression)
  - Example: `저는 매일 아침 버스로 통학해요.` — *I commute to school by bus every morning.* [버스 (bus) + 로 (by) + 통학해요 (commute to school)]
  - Example: `부산까지 KTX 기차로 두 시간 반 걸려요.` — *It takes two and a half hours to Busan by KTX train.* [KTX 기차 (train) + 로 (by) + 걸려요]

#### Module 14.1.4 — 말하기·듣기 (Shadowing & Drills)

- [ ] **Shadowing & Repetition Drill:** Formulate travel instructions specifying the exact transit vehicle and -(으)로.
  - [ ] Prompt: "Ask: How do I get to Incheon Airport?" → Speak aloud: `인천공항까지 어떻게 가요?` (*How do I get to Incheon Airport?*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "Answer: Go by airport railroad train." → Speak aloud: `공항철도로 가세요.` (*Go by airport railroad train.*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "Ask: How long does it take by taxi?" → Speak aloud: `택시로 시간이 얼마나 걸려요?` (*How long does it take by taxi?*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "Answer: It takes about 40 minutes." → Speak aloud: `사십 분 정도 걸려요.` (*It takes about 40 minutes.*)

#### Module 14.1.5 — 읽기·쓰기 (Reading & Production)

- [ ] **Reading Passage:** "서울 대중교통 이용 안내 (Seoul Transit Guide)"
  - Text: `서울에서는 지하철과 버스를 이용하면 아주 편리합니다. 교통카드를 태그하고 타면 요금이 할인되고, 다른 노선으로 갈아탈 때 무료 환승 혜택도 받을 수 있습니다. 강남역에서 명동역까지 갈 때는 지하철 2호선을 타고 사당역에서 4호선으로 갈아타면 약 35분이 걸립니다. 출퇴근 시간에는 지하철이 버스보다 훨씬 빠릅니다.`
  - Translation: *Using the subway and bus in Seoul is very convenient. If you tap and ride with a transit card, fares are discounted, and you can also receive free transfer benefits when changing to different lines. When traveling from Gangnam Station to Myeongdong Station, if you ride Line 2 and transfer to Line 4 at Sadang Station, it takes approximately 35 minutes. During rush hours, the subway is far faster than the bus.*
  - [ ] Check: At which station do you transfer when going from Gangnam to Myeongdong? → Answer: `사당역 (Sadang Station)` (*사당역에서 4호선으로 갈아타면*)
  - [ ] Check: Why is the subway better during rush hours? → Answer: `버스보다 훨씬 빨라요 (It is much faster than the bus)` (*출퇴근 시간에는 지하철이 버스보다 훨씬 빠릅니다.*)
- [ ] **Guided Writing Task:** Write 5 sentences explaining how to get from your home to your school or workplace using transport modes, -(으)로, and duration expressions.
  - Sample Model Answer: `제 집에서 서울대학교까지 지하철로 가요. 먼저 합정역에서 지하철 2호선을 타요. 그리고 서울대입구역에서 내려요. 그곳에서 버스로 갈아타요. 전체 시간은 약 40분 정도 걸려요.`

### 14.2 — Finding Places in Town and Directions (길 찾기와 방향 안내)

**Situation & Objective.** Guide pedestrians using directional particles -(으)로 and directional verbs (쭉 가세요, 오른쪽으로 도세요, 건너세요).

#### Module 14.2.1 — 대화 (Conversation First)

- [ ] **Dialogue Practice:** Listen to and practice the dialogue between Tourist (관광객) and Local Guide (안내원) and Tourist (관광객) and Local Guide (안내원) and Tourist (관광객).
  - `실례합니다, 인사동 쌈지길이 어디예요?` (Excuse me, where is Insadong Ssamziegil?) — *Note: 쌈지길 = famous spiral handicraft mall in Insadong.*
  - `이 길로 똑바로 100미터쯤 쭉 가세요.` (Go straight along this street for about 100 meters.) — *Note: 이 길로 = along this road; 똑바로 / 쭉 = straight.*
  - `100미터 직진한 후에 어디로 가요?` (After going straight 100 meters, where do I go?) — *Note: 어디로 = toward where; 직진 = moving straight ahead.*
  - `사거리에서 오른쪽으로 도세요. 횡단보도를 건너면 바로 보여요.` (Turn right at the 4-way intersection. If you cross the pedestrian crosswalk, you will see it right away.) — *Note: 오른쪽으로 = to the right; 횡단보도 = crosswalk; 건너면 = if you cross.*
  - `자세히 설명해 주셔서 정말 감사합니다!` (Thank you so much for explaining in detail!) — *Note: 자세히 = in detail.*
- [ ] **Cultural Etiquette & Pragmatics:** Giving directions in Korean uses clear spatial and directional commands ending in the polite imperative -(으)세요: "쭉 가세요" (go straight), "오른쪽으로 가세요" (go to the right), "왼쪽으로 도세요" (turn left), and "횡단보도를 건너세요" (cross the crosswalk).

#### Module 14.2.2 — 어휘 (Vocabulary in Context)

- [ ] **Core Target Words:** Memorise and pronounce all target words for this lesson:
  - [ ] `오른쪽 / 왼쪽 / 똑바로 (직진)` (oreunjjok / oenjjok / ttokbaro) [Noun]: right side / left side / straight — e.g., "사거리에서 왼쪽으로 가세요." (*Go to the left at the four-way intersection.*)
  - [ ] `사거리 / 삼거리` (sageori / samgeori, Hanja: 四거리 / 三거리) [Noun]: four-way intersection / three-way T-junction — e.g., "신호등 있는 사거리에서 멈추세요." (*Stop at the intersection with the traffic light.*)
  - [ ] `횡단보도 / 신호등` (hoengdanbodo / sinhodeung, Hanja: 橫斷步道 / 信號燈) [Noun]: pedestrian crosswalk / traffic light — e.g., "초록불에 횡단보도를 건너요." (*Cross the crosswalk on the green light.*)
  - [ ] `건너다 / 건너세요` (geonneoda / geonneoseyo) [Verb]: to cross -> please cross — e.g., "길을 조심히 건너세요." (*Please cross the street carefully.*)
  - [ ] `돌다 / 도세요` (dolda / doseyo) [Verb]: to turn -> please turn — e.g., "약국 모퉁이에서 우회전하세요 (오른쪽으로 도세요)." (*Turn right at the pharmacy corner.*)

#### Module 14.2.3 — 문법 (Grammar Formulas & Rules)

- [ ] **Grammar Pattern: `Direction Noun + (으)로 (Directional Marker: Towards)`** (towards / in the direction of)
  - Formula: `Consonant + 으로 | Vowel or ㄹ + 로`
  - Explanation: When attached to spatial words (오른쪽, 왼쪽, 위, 아래, 앞, 뒤), -(으)로 marks orientation or movement path.
  - Rule: 오른쪽 (consonant) + 으로 = 오른쪽으로 (to the right)
  - Rule: 왼쪽 (consonant) + 으로 = 왼쪽으로 (to the left)
  - Rule: 위 (vowel) + 로 = 위로 (upward)
  - Example: `다음 골목에서 왼쪽으로 도세요.` — *Please turn left at the next alley.* [다음 골목 (next alley) + 에서 + 왼쪽 (left) + 으로 (toward) + 도세요 (turn)]

#### Module 14.2.4 — 말하기·듣기 (Shadowing & Drills)

- [ ] **Shadowing & Repetition Drill:** Give clear walking directions using directional adverbs and -(으)로.
  - [ ] Prompt: "Instruct: Go straight ahead." → Speak aloud: `앞으로 똑바로 가세요.` (*Go straight ahead.*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "Instruct: Turn to the right at the bank." → Speak aloud: `은행에서 오른쪽으로 도세요.` (*Turn right at the bank.*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "Instruct: Turn to the left at the pharmacy." → Speak aloud: `약국에서 왼쪽으로 도세요.` (*Turn left at the pharmacy.*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "Instruct: Cross the crosswalk over there." → Speak aloud: `저기 횡단보도를 건너세요.` (*Cross the crosswalk over there.*)

#### Module 14.2.5 — 읽기·쓰기 (Reading & Production)

- [ ] **Reading Passage:** "우리 집 찾아오는 길 (Directions to My House)"
  - Text: `친구들을 제 생일 파티에 초대합니다. 우리 집 찾아오는 방법을 알려 드릴게요. 지하철 3호선 안국역 2번 출구로 나오세요. 나온 후에 편의점 쪽으로 50미터 직진하세요. 베이커리 빵집이 나오면 오른쪽 골목으로 도세요. 골목 안으로 30미터쯤 들어오면 1층에 꽃집이 있는 흰색 빌라 건물 302호입니다.`
  - Translation: *I invite my friends to my birthday party. Let me tell you how to find my house. Come out of Exit 2 of Anguk Station on Subway Line 3. After coming out, go straight for 50 meters toward the convenience store. When the bakery appears, turn into the right alley. If you walk in about 30 meters into the alley, it is Apartment 302 of the white villa building with a flower shop on the first floor.*
  - [ ] Check: Which subway station exit should guests use? → Answer: `안국역 2번 출구 (Anguk Station Exit 2)` (*지하철 3호선 안국역 2번 출구로 나오세요.*)
  - [ ] Check: What store is located on the first floor of the building? → Answer: `꽃집 (Flower shop)` (*1층에 꽃집이 있는 흰색 빌라 건물*)
- [ ] **Guided Writing Task:** Write 5 sentences providing clear walking directions from a local subway station or bus stop to your home, favorite cafe, or classroom.
  - Sample Model Answer: `홍대입구역 9번 출구로 나오세요. 출구 앞에서 앞으로 100미터 똑바로 직진하세요. 올리브영 화장품 가게가 보이면 왼쪽으로 도세요. 횡단보도를 하나 건너세요. 건너편에 우리가 만날 카페가 바로 있어요.`


## Unit 15 — Plans and Invitations (계획과 초대)

**Part.** Part D — Connect Your Korean (Units 14–16 & Checkpoint D)
**Objective.** Express future intentions using -(으)ㄹ 거예요, suggest shared plans using -(으)ㄹ까요?, and accept or politely decline invitations.

### 15.1 — Inviting a Friend and Future Plansㄹ 거예요) (친구 초대와 -(으)

**Situation & Objective.** Formulate future commitments, intentions, and vacation plans using Verb Stem + -(으)ㄹ 거예요.

#### Module 15.1.1 — 대화 (Conversation First)

- [ ] **Dialogue Practice:** Listen to and practice the dialogue between Min-su (민수) and Sarah (사라) and Min-su (민수) and Sarah (사라) and Min-su (민수).
  - `사라 씨, 이번 주말에 특별한 계획이 있어요?` (Sarah, do you have special plans this weekend?) — *Note: 특별한 계획 = special plans.*
  - `토요일에 한국인 튜터와 어학원에서 공부할 거예요. 일요일에는 계획 없어요.` (On Saturday I am going to study at the language academy with my Korean tutor. On Sunday I have no plans.) — *Note: 공부할 거예요 = 공부하다 + ㄹ 거예요 (future intention).*
  - `그러면 일요일에 같이 야구장에 갈래요? 재미있는 경기가 있을 거예요.` (In that case, would you like to go to the baseball stadium together on Sunday? There will be an interesting match.) — *Note: 야구장 = baseball stadium; 있을 거예요 = there will be.*
  - `와, 정말 신나요! 한국 야구 응원을 꼭 보고 싶었어요.` (Wow, that sounds so exciting! I really wanted to see Korean baseball cheering.) — *Note: 신나요 = exciting; 응원 = cheering/fan songs.*
  - `제가 티켓을 예매할게요. 잠실야구장에서 만나요!` (I will reserve the tickets. Let's meet at Jamsil Baseball Stadium!) — *Note: 예매할게요 = I will book/reserve.*
- [ ] **Cultural Etiquette & Pragmatics:** Korean professional baseball (KBO) at venues like Jamsil Baseball Stadium (잠실야구장) is legendary for its participatory fan culture. Fans sing individual cheer songs for every batter, eat fried chicken and beer (치맥), and cheer rhythmically with thundersticks.

#### Module 15.1.2 — 어휘 (Vocabulary in Context)

- [ ] **Core Target Words:** Memorise and pronounce all target words for this lesson:
  - [ ] `계획` (gyehoek, Hanja: 計劃) [Noun]: plan / project — e.g., "미래 계획을 세워요." (*I make future plans.*)
  - [ ] `야구장 / 경기장` (yagujang / gyeonggijang, Hanja: 野球場 / 競技場) [Noun]: baseball stadium / sports stadium — e.g., "야구장에서 신나게 응원해요." (*We cheer excitedly at the baseball stadium.*)
  - [ ] `예매하다 / 예약하다` (yemaehada / yeyakhada, Hanja: 豫買 / 豫約) [Verb]: to purchase tickets in advance / to reserve — e.g., "영화표를 미리 예매했어요." (*I reserved the movie tickets in advance.*)
  - [ ] `내일 / 다음 주 / 내년` (naeil / daeumju / naenyeon, Hanja: 來日 / ── / 來年) [Noun]: tomorrow / next week / next year — e.g., "다음 주에 고향 친구가 와요." (*My hometown friend is coming next week.*)

#### Module 15.1.3 — 문법 (Grammar Formulas & Rules)

- [ ] **Grammar Pattern: `Verb Stem + -(으)ㄹ 거예요 (Future / Probable Tense)`** (will / am going to [do verb])
  - Formula: `Consonant ends (받침 O) + 을 거예요 | Vowel ends (받침 X) or ㄹ ends + ㄹ 거예요`
  - Explanation: Expresses future intention, planned action, or objective probability.
  - Rule: Stem ends in vowel: 가다 -> 가 + ㄹ 거예요 = 갈 거예요
  - Rule: Stem ends in consonant: 먹다 -> 먹 + 을 거예요 = 먹을 거예요
  - Rule: Stem ends in ㄹ: 살다 -> 살 거예요, 만들다 -> 만들 거예요
  - Rule: 하다 verbs: 하다 -> 할 거예요, 공부하다 -> 공부할 거예요
  - Example: `저는 주말에 친구들과 삼겹살을 먹을 거예요.` — *I am going to eat pork belly with friends on the weekend.* [먹 (eat) + 을 거예요 (will eat)]
  - Example: `내일은 날씨가 아주 맑을 거예요.` — *Tomorrow the weather will be very clear.* [맑 (clear) + 을 거예요 (will be)]

#### Module 15.1.4 — 말하기·듣기 (Shadowing & Drills)

- [ ] **Shadowing & Repetition Drill:** Conjugate each verb into the future tense -(으)ㄹ 거예요.
  - [ ] Prompt: "Conjugate: 만나다 (to meet) -> [I will meet Sarah tomorrow]." → Speak aloud: `내일 사라 씨를 만날 거예요.` (*I will meet Sarah tomorrow.*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "Conjugate: 읽다 (to read) -> [I will read a book tonight]." → Speak aloud: `오늘 밤에 책을 읽을 거예요.` (*I will read a book tonight.*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "Conjugate: 여행하다 (to travel) -> [I will travel to Busan next month]." → Speak aloud: `다음 달에 부산을 여행할 거예요.` (*I will travel to Busan next month.*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "Conjugate: 만들다 (to make) -> [I will make Korean food]." → Speak aloud: `한국 음식을 만들 거예요.` (*I will make Korean food.*)

#### Module 15.1.5 — 읽기·쓰기 (Reading & Production)

- [ ] **Reading Passage:** "사라의 여름방학 계획서 (Sarah's Summer Vacation Plan)"
  - Text: `이번 여름방학은 두 달 동안 아주 알차게 보낼 거예요. 첫 번째 달에는 서울대학교 어학당에서 2급 한국어 과정을 열심히 공부할 거예요. 한국어 말하기를 매일 연습할 거예요. 두 번째 달에는 친구들과 제주도와 경주로 일주일 동안 여행을 갈 거예요. 한국의 전통 유적지를 둘러보고 맛있는 지역 음식도 많이 먹을 거예요. 방학이 정말 기다려져요.`
  - Translation: *This summer vacation, I am going to spend two months very productively. In the first month, I will study the Level 2 Korean course diligently at the SNU Language Education Center. I will practice Korean speaking every day. In the second month, I will go on a one-week trip to Jeju Island and Gyeongju with friends. I will tour Korea's traditional historical sites and eat lots of delicious regional food. I am really looking forward to vacation.*
  - [ ] Check: What course will Sarah study in the first month? → Answer: `2급 한국어 과정 (Level 2 Korean course)` (*2급 한국어 과정을 열심히 공부할 거예요.*)
  - [ ] Check: Which cities will Sarah visit in the second month? → Answer: `제주도와 경주 (Jeju Island and Gyeongju)` (*친구들과 제주도와 경주로 일주일 동안 여행을 갈 거예요.*)
- [ ] **Guided Writing Task:** Write a 6-sentence paragraph describing your goals and plans for next week or your next holiday using the future tense -(으)ㄹ 거예요.
  - Sample Model Answer: `저는 다음 주말에 많은 계획이 있어요. 먼저 토요일 아침에 밀린 청소와 빨래를 할 거예요. 오후에는 강남 서점에 갈 거예요. 서점에서 재미있는 한국어 소설을 살 거예요. 저녁에는 친구를 만나서 불고기를 먹을 거예요. 아주 보람찬 주말을 보낼 거예요.`

### 15.2 — Accepting and Declining Invitationsㄹ까요?) (초대 수락과 거절: -(으)

**Situation & Objective.** Propose joint activities using -(으)ㄹ까요?, accept warmly, or politely decline with considerate excuses.

#### Module 15.2.1 — 대화 (Conversation First)

- [ ] **Dialogue Practice:** Listen to and practice the dialogue between David (데이비드) and Yujin (유진) and David (데이비드) and Yujin (유진) and David (데이비드).
  - `유진 씨, 오늘 수업 끝나고 같이 커피 한잔할까요?` (Yu-jin, shall we have a cup of coffee together after class today?) — *Note: 커피 한잔할까요? = "Shall we have a cup of coffee together?"*
  - `정말 미안해요, 데이비드 씨. 오늘은 치과 예약이 있어요.` (I am really sorry, David. Today I have a dentist appointment.) — *Note: 치과 = dentist; 예약 = appointment.*
  - `아, 그렇군요! 그러면 내일은 어때요?` (Ah, I see! Then how about tomorrow?) — *Note: 내일은 어때요? = Polite alternative proposal.*
  - `내일은 좋아요! 내일 점심 같이 먹을까요?` (Tomorrow is good! Shall we eat lunch together tomorrow?) — *Note: 점심 같이 먹을까요? = "Shall we eat lunch together?"*
  - `네, 좋아요! 내일 열두 시에 학생회관 앞에서 봐요.` (Yes, sounds great! See you tomorrow at 12:00 in front of the Student Hall.) — *Note: 학생회관 = Student Center/Hall.*
- [ ] **Cultural Etiquette & Pragmatics:** In Korean etiquette, declining an invitation directly with a flat "아니요" (No) can sound blunt or abrasive. Koreans soften refusals by opening with "미안해요" (I'm sorry) or "아쉽지만..." (It's regrettable, but...) followed by a clear reason ("선약이 있어요" - I have a prior engagement, or "시험공부를 해야 해요" - I must study for exams).

#### Module 15.2.2 — 어휘 (Vocabulary in Context)

- [ ] **Core Target Words:** Memorise and pronounce all target words for this lesson:
  - [ ] `미안해요 / 죄송해요` (mianhaeyo / joesonghaeyo, Hanja: 未安 / 罪悚) [Expression]: I am sorry (polite informal / formal polite) — e.g., "늦어서 정말 죄송합니다." (*I am truly sorry for being late.*)
  - [ ] `선약` (seonyak, Hanja: 先約) [Noun]: prior engagement / previous appointment — e.g., "그날은 선약이 있어서 곤란해요." (*I have a prior engagement that day, so it is difficult.*)
  - [ ] `좋아요 / 괜찮아요` (joayo / gwaenchanayo) [Expression]: Sounds good / That is fine — e.g., "네, 그 시간이면 괜찮아요." (*Yes, if it is that time, it is fine.*)
  - [ ] `다음에 / 나중에` (daeume / najung-e) [Noun]: next time / later on — e.g., "다음에 꼭 같이 가요." (*Let us definitely go together next time.*)

#### Module 15.2.3 — 문법 (Grammar Formulas & Rules)

- [ ] **Grammar Pattern: `Verb Stem + -(으)ㄹ까요? (Shall we...? / Shall I...?)`** (Shall we [do verb] together?)
  - Formula: `Consonant ends + 을까요? | Vowel ends + ㄹ까요?`
  - Explanation: Used to ask the listener's opinion or propose a shared activity.
  - Rule: Vowel ends: 가다 -> 갈까요? (Shall we go?), 보다 -> 볼까요? (Shall we watch?)
  - Rule: Consonant ends: 먹다 -> 먹을까요? (Shall we eat?), 앉다 -> 앉을까요? (Shall we sit?)
  - Rule: Accepting: 네, 좋아요! 같이 [아요/어요].
  - Rule: Declining: 미안해요, [이유]이/가 있어서 못 가요.
  - Example: `우리 오늘 저녁에 한식 먹을까요?` — *Shall we eat Korean food this evening?* [우리 (we) + 먹 (eat) + 을까요? (shall we eat?)]

#### Module 15.2.4 — 말하기·듣기 (Shadowing & Drills)

- [ ] **Shadowing & Repetition Drill:** Propose suggestions with -(으)ㄹ까요? and respond politely.
  - [ ] Prompt: "Suggest: Shall we drink coffee together?" → Speak aloud: `우리 같이 커피 마실까요?` (*Shall we drink coffee together?*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "Accept: Yes, sounds great! Let's go together." → Speak aloud: `네, 좋아요! 같이 가요.` (*Yes, sounds great! Let's go together.*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "Decline: I am sorry. I have an exam today." → Speak aloud: `미안해요. 오늘은 시험이 있어요.` (*I am sorry. Today I have an exam.*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "Suggest alternative: Then let's meet on Friday." → Speak aloud: `그러면 금요일에 만나요.` (*Then let's meet on Friday.*)

#### Module 15.2.5 — 읽기·쓰기 (Reading & Production)

- [ ] **Reading Passage:** "동아리 회식 초대장 (Club Dinner Invitation)"
  - Text: `한국어 회화 동아리 회원 여러분, 안녕하세요? 이번 주 금요일 저녁 6시에 신촌 고깃집에서 중간 모임을 가질 예정입니다. 맛있는 삼겹살을 먹으면서 한국어 연습도 하고 친목을 다질 예정입니다. 참석이 가능하신 분은 목요일까지 회장에게 카톡으로 알려 주세요. 혹시 선약이 있으신 분은 다음에 꼭 함께해요!`
  - Translation: *Hello members of the Korean Conversation Club! This Friday at 6 PM, we plan to hold a midterm gathering at a Sinchon barbecue restaurant. While eating delicious pork belly, we will practice Korean and build camaraderie. Those who can attend, please notify the president via KakaoTalk by Thursday. If anyone has prior plans, please be sure to join us next time!*
  - [ ] Check: When and where is the club gathering held? → Answer: `금요일 저녁 6시 신촌 고깃집 (Friday 6 PM at Sinchon BBQ)` (*이번 주 금요일 저녁 6시에 신촌 고깃집에서*)
  - [ ] Check: By when must members notify their attendance? → Answer: `목요일까지 (By Thursday)` (*참석이 가능하신 분은 목요일까지*)
- [ ] **Guided Writing Task:** Write a realistic 5-line message declining an invitation from a classmate due to prior study commitments, and proposing an alternative time.
  - Sample Model Answer: `민수 씨, 저녁 식사에 초대해 줘서 고마워요. 하지만 금요일에는 한국어 말하기 시험공부가 있어서 갈 수 없어요. 정말 아쉽고 미안해요. 대신 시험이 끝나는 토요일 점심에 같이 먹을까요? 그때 제가 맛있는 커피 살게요!`


## Unit 16 — Everyday Communication and Integration (종합 의사소통과 정리)

**Part.** Part D — Connect Your Korean (Units 14–16 & Checkpoint D)
**Objective.** Handle everyday communicative hurdles using repair phrases (다시 말해 주세요, 천천히 말해 주세요, 모르겠어요), and synthesize all Level 1 grammar and vocabulary into comprehensive narratives.

### 16.1 — Explaining a Problem and Conversation Repair (대화 수리와 문제 설명)

**Situation & Objective.** Keep conversations going when confused by asking native speakers to repeat, slow down, or explain words.

#### Module 16.1.1 — 대화 (Conversation First)

- [ ] **Dialogue Practice:** Listen to and practice the dialogue between Sarah (사라) and Teacher (선생님) and Sarah (사라) and Teacher (선생님) and Sarah (사라).
  - `선생님, 죄송하지만 방금 하신 말씀을 다시 한번 말씀해 주시겠어요?` (Teacher, I am sorry, but could you please say what you just said once more?) — *Note: 다시 한번 = once more; 말씀해 주시겠어요 = respectful request.*
  - `네, 그럼요. 조금 더 천천히 말해 줄게요.` (Yes, of course. I will speak a little more slowly for you.) — *Note: 천천히 = slowly.*
  - `감사합니다. 그런데 그 단어의 뜻을 잘 모르겠어요. 설명해 주세요.` (Thank you. But I don't know the meaning of that word well. Please explain it.) — *Note: 모르겠어요 = I don't know; 뜻 = meaning; 설명해 주세요 = please explain.*
  - `이 단어는 쉬운 말로 "약속"이라는 뜻이에요.` (This word means "appointment / promise" in simple words.) — *Note: 쉬운 말로 = in simple words.*
  - `아, 이제 이해했어요! 정말 감사합니다.` (Ah, I understand now! Thank you so much.) — *Note: 이제 이해했어요 = "Now I understood".*
- [ ] **Cultural Etiquette & Pragmatics:** Never remain silent when you don't understand something in Korea! Native speakers deeply appreciate proactive communication repair. Essential conversational survival phrases include: "천천히 말해 주세요" (Please speak slowly), "다시 한번 말해 주세요" (Please say it once again), and "한국어로 어떻게 말해요?" (How do you say this in Korean?).

#### Module 16.1.2 — 어휘 (Vocabulary in Context)

- [ ] **Core Target Words:** Memorise and pronounce all target words for this lesson:
  - [ ] `다시 / 또` (dasi / tto) [Adverb]: again / once more — e.g., "다시 말씀해 주세요." (*Please speak once again.*)
  - [ ] `천천히` (cheoncheonhi) [Adverb]: slowly — e.g., "조금만 천천히 말씀해 주시겠어요?" (*Could you please speak just a little more slowly?*)
  - [ ] `모르다 / 모르겠어요` (moreuda / moreugesseoyo) [Verb]: to not know -> I don't know — e.g., "이 단어 뜻을 잘 모르겠어요." (*I don't know the meaning of this word well.*)
  - [ ] `알다 / 알겠어요 / 이해했어요` (alda / algesseoyo / ihaehaesseoyo) [Verb]: to know -> Understood / I got it — e.g., "네, 무슨 뜻인지 잘 알겠어요." (*Yes, I understand well what it means.*)
  - [ ] `도와주세요` (dowajuseyo) [Expression]: please help me — e.g., "길을 잃었어요. 좀 도와주세요." (*I lost my way. Please help me a little.*)

#### Module 16.1.3 — 문법 (Grammar Formulas & Rules)

- [ ] **Grammar Pattern: `다시 말해 주세요 / 천천히 말해 주세요 / 써 주세요`** (Please repeat / Please speak slowly / Please write it down)
  - Formula: `[Verb Stem + 아/어] + 주세요`
  - Explanation: Using the polite request form -아/어 주세요 with conversational repair verbs.
  - Rule: 말하다 -> 말해 주세요 (Please speak)
  - Rule: 천천히 말해 주세요 (Please speak slowly)
  - Rule: 다시 한번 말해 주세요 (Please say it once again)
  - Rule: 종이에 써 주세요 (Please write it down on paper)
  - Example: `죄송하지만 한국어로 다시 말씀해 주세요.` — *I am sorry, but please say it again in Korean.* [한국어 (Korean language) + 로 (in) + 다시 (again) + 말씀해 주세요]

#### Module 16.1.4 — 말하기·듣기 (Shadowing & Drills)

- [ ] **Shadowing & Repetition Drill:** Practice delivering emergency comprehension phrases clearly and politely.
  - [ ] Prompt: "Request: Please speak slowly." → Speak aloud: `천천히 말해 주세요.` (*Please speak slowly.*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "Request: Please say it once more." → Speak aloud: `다시 한번 말해 주세요.` (*Please say it once more.*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "State: I don't know well. Please help me." → Speak aloud: `잘 모르겠어요. 도와주세요.` (*I don't know well. Please help me.*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "Ask: How do you say this in Korean?" → Speak aloud: `이거 한국어로 어떻게 말해요?` (*How do you say this in Korean?*)

#### Module 16.1.5 — 읽기·쓰기 (Reading & Production)

- [ ] **Reading Passage:** "외국인 유학생을 위한 생생 한국어 팁 (Live Korean Tips for Foreign Students)"
  - Text: `한국어를 배울 때 모르는 단어가 나오거나 상대방의 말이 너무 빠르면 당황하지 마세요. 한국 사람들은 외국인이 한국어로 노력하는 모습을 매우 좋아합니다. "천천히 말씀해 주세요" 또는 "다시 한번 말씀해 주시겠어요?"라고 정중하게 부탁하면 누구나 친절하게 다시 설명해 줍니다. 모르는 것을 두려워하지 말고 적극적으로 질문하세요!`
  - Translation: *When learning Korean, if unfamiliar words appear or the other person speaks too fast, do not panic. Korean people love seeing foreigners make an effort in Korean. If you politely request "Please speak slowly" or "Could you please say it once more?", anyone will kindly explain again. Do not fear not knowing, and ask questions actively!*
  - [ ] Check: What should you say when someone speaks too fast in Korean? → Answer: `천천히 말씀해 주세요 (Please speak slowly)` (*"천천히 말씀해 주세요"라고 정중하게 부탁하면*)
  - [ ] Check: How do Korean people feel when foreigners try speaking Korean? → Answer: `매우 좋아해요 (They like it very much)` (*한국 사람들은 외국인이 한국어로 노력하는 모습을 매우 좋아합니다.*)
- [ ] **Guided Writing Task:** Write 4 sentences explaining what you do when you cannot understand Korean words in class or on the street.
  - Sample Model Answer: `저는 한국어 수업에서 모르는 단어가 있으면 손을 들어요. 선생님께 "선생님, 다시 한번 천천히 말씀해 주세요"라고 부탁해요. 그리고 한국어 사전을 찾아서 뜻을 확인해요. 새로운 단어를 단어장에 꼭 적고 매일 복습해요.`

### 16.2 — Putting Your Day Together: Capstone Narrative (나의 하루 종합 이야기)

**Situation & Objective.** Synthesize Level 1 vocabulary, particles (은/는, 이/가, 을/를, 에, 에서, 의, (으)로), present, past, and future into a connected life story.

#### Module 16.2.1 — 대화 (Conversation First)

- [ ] **Dialogue Practice:** Listen to and practice the dialogue between Interviewer (면접관) and Sarah (사라) and Interviewer (면접관) and Sarah (사라) and Interviewer (면접관).
  - `사라 씨, 1급 과정을 마친 소감이 어때요? 자신에 대해 소개해 보세요.` (Sarah, how do you feel finishing the Level 1 course? Please introduce yourself.) — *Note: 소감 = feelings/impressions; 소개해 보세요 = please introduce.*
  - `처음에는 한글도 몰랐지만 지금은 한국어로 인사하고 식당에서 주문할 수 있어요.` (At first I didn't even know Hangul, but now I can greet in Korean and order food at restaurants.) — *Note: 주문할 수 있어요 = can order.*
  - `한국 생활은 어때요? 어제와 주말에는 무엇을 했어요?` (How is life in Korea? What did you do yesterday and over the weekend?) — *Note: Comprehensive interview question testing past tense narrative.*
  - `어제는 도서관에서 시험공부를 했고, 지난 주말에는 친구들과 경복궁에 가서 사진을 찍었어요. 내일은 동아리 친구들과 삼겹살을 먹을 거예요!` (Yesterday I studied for exams at the library, and last weekend I went to Gyeongbokgung with friends and took photos. Tomorrow I will eat pork belly with club friends!) — *Note: Integrated use of past, present, and future.*
  - `정말 훌륭합니다! 1급 과정을 완벽하게 수료하셨습니다. 축하합니다!` (Truly outstanding! You have completely passed the Level 1 course. Congratulations!) — *Note: 수료하셨습니다 = You have graduated/completed the course.*
- [ ] **Cultural Etiquette & Pragmatics:** Graduating Level 1 signifies full survival competence (A1 proficiency): you can navigate Seoul independently, shop, order food, commute across lines, discuss past events, make appointments, and form authentic personal friendships.

#### Module 16.2.2 — 어휘 (Vocabulary in Context)

- [ ] **Core Target Words:** Memorise and pronounce all target words for this lesson:
  - [ ] `수료 / 졸업` (suryo / joreop, Hanja: 修了 / 卒業) [Noun]: course completion / graduation — e.g., "한국어 1급 과정을 성공적으로 수료했어요." (*I successfully completed the Level 1 Korean course.*)
  - [ ] `자신감` (jasin-gam, Hanja: 自信感) [Noun]: confidence — e.g., "한국어 말하기에 자신감이 생겼어요." (*I developed confidence in Korean speaking.*)
  - [ ] `축하합니다 / 축하해요` (chukahamnida / chukahaeyo, Hanja: 祝賀) [Expression]: congratulations! — e.g., "1급 완주를 진심으로 축하합니다!" (*Warmest congratulations on finishing Level 1!*)

#### Module 16.2.3 — 문법 (Grammar Formulas & Rules)

- [ ] **Grammar Pattern: `Integrated Level 1 Particle & Tense Matrix`** (Complete mastery of Present, Past, and Future with all 8 core particles)
  - Formula: `Present (아요/어요) + Past (았/었어요) + Future ((으)ㄹ 거예요)`
  - Explanation: The ultimate synthesis of beginner Korean communication.
  - Rule: Topic/Focus: 은/는
  - Rule: Subject identifier: 이/가
  - Rule: Direct object: 을/를
  - Rule: Static location / Time / Destination: 에
  - Rule: Dynamic action place: 에서
  - Rule: Possession: 의
  - Rule: Method/Direction: (으)로
  - Rule: Connector: 하고 / 와 / 과 / 그리고 / 하지만
  - Example: `저는 어제 도서관에서 책을 읽었어요. 그리고 내일은 공원에 갈 거예요.` — *I read books at the library yesterday. And tomorrow I am going to go to the park.* [Integrates past, connectors, location markers, and future intention.]

#### Module 16.2.4 — 말하기·듣기 (Shadowing & Drills)

- [ ] **Shadowing & Repetition Drill:** Deliver full connected sentences transitioning effortlessly across present, past, and future.
  - [ ] Prompt: "Introduce yourself: I am Sarah, American, and studying Korean." → Speak aloud: `저는 사라예요. 미국 사람이고 한국어를 열심히 공부해요.` (*I am Sarah. I am American and studying Korean diligently.*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "State past: Yesterday I went to Hongdae and bought clothes." → Speak aloud: `어제 홍대에 가서 옷을 샀어요.` (*Yesterday I went to Hongdae and bought clothes.*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "State future: Tomorrow I will go to Busan by KTX train." → Speak aloud: `내일은 KTX 기차로 부산에 갈 거예요.` (*Tomorrow I will go to Busan by KTX train.*)
  - [ ] Prompt: "Express wish: I want to speak Korean very fluently." → Speak aloud: `한국어를 정말 유창하게 잘하고 싶어요.` (*I want to speak Korean very fluently.*)

#### Module 16.2.5 — 읽기·쓰기 (Reading & Production)

- [ ] **Reading Passage:** "나의 한국어 성장 일기: 1급을 마치며 (My Korean Growth Diary: Finishing Level 1)"
  - Text: `처음 서울에 왔을 때 저는 한국어를 한마디도 할 수 없었습니다. 하지만 1급 16개 단원을 매일 꾸준히 공부하면서 모든 것이 달라졌습니다. 이제는 혼자서 카페에 가서 아메리카노를 주문하고, 식당에서 좋아하는 찌개를 시킵니다. 지하철 노선도를 보고 원하는 역으로 쉽게 갈 수 있습니다. 어제는 한국인 친구에게 카카오톡 메시지를 한글로 직접 보냈습니다. 다음 2급 과정에서도 포기하지 않고 더욱 열심히 공부할 것입니다!`
  - Translation: *When I first came to Seoul, I could not speak a single word of Korean. However, as I studied the 16 units of Level 1 diligently every day, everything changed. Now, I go to cafes alone and order Americanos, and order my favorite stews at restaurants. Looking at the subway map, I can easily go to my desired station. Yesterday, I directly sent a KakaoTalk message in Hangul to a Korean friend. In the upcoming Level 2 course, I will not give up and will study even harder!*
  - [ ] Check: What can the writer do alone now at cafes? → Answer: `아메리카노를 주문해요 (Order an Americano)` (*혼자서 카페에 가서 아메리카노를 주문하고*)
  - [ ] Check: What did the writer do yesterday with a Korean friend? → Answer: `한글로 카카오톡 메시지를 보냈어요 (Sent a KakaoTalk message in Hangul)` (*어제는 한국인 친구에게 카카오톡 메시지를 한글로 직접 보냈습니다.*)
- [ ] **Guided Writing Task:** Write a comprehensive 10-sentence self-introduction and life essay combining your name, nationality, routine, what you did last weekend, and what you will do next week in Korean.
  - Sample Model Answer: `안녕하세요? 제 이름은 데이비드입니다. 저는 캐나다 사람이고 서울대학교 어학당 학생입니다. 저는 매일 아침 일곱 시 반에 일어나요. 낮에는 어학당 교실에서 한국어를 열심히 공부해요. 제 취미는 음악 감상과 축구예요. 어제는 홍대에서 친구와 함께 쇼핑을 했어요. 따뜻한 김치찌개도 아주 맛있게 먹었어요. 다음 주말에는 KTX 기차를 타고 부산에 갈 거예요. 그곳에서 푸른 바다를 보고 맛있는 해산물을 먹고 싶어요. 만나서 정말 반갑습니다!`


## Checkpoint D — Checkpoint D: Integrated Final Assessment (Level 1 Comprehensive Exit Exam)

**Prerequisites.** Completion of all 16 units (Units 0 through 16).
**Target Skills:**
- [ ] Navigate city transit with -(으)로, ask how to get to landmarks, and understand directional guidance.
- [ ] Formulate future commitments with -(으)ㄹ 거예요 and invite people politely with -(으)ㄹ까요?.
- [ ] Politely accept or decline invitations with reasoned excuses.
- [ ] Perform conversational repair (다시 말해 주세요, 천천히 말해 주세요, 모르겠어요).
- [ ] Synthesize present, past, future, and all beginner particles into an authentic multi-paragraph spoken and written narrative.

### Spoken Simulation
- **Scenario:** Official SNU Level 1 Oral Proficiency Interview with a native examiner.
- **Prompt:** Deliver a comprehensive 2-minute spoken introduction covering: name & background, daily schedule, yesterday's outing, directions to your favorite place, and future plans.
- [ ] Deliver unprompted: `안녕하세요? 저는 캐나다에서 온 데이비드라고 합니다. 서울대학교 어학당에서 한국어를 공부하고 있습니다. 저는 보통 아침 일곱 시 반에 일어나서 학교에 갑니다. 어제는 수업이 끝난 후에 명동에 가서 쇼핑을 했습니다. 지하철 4호선으로 갔는데 약 이십 분 걸렸습니다. 옷 한 벌과 신발 한 켤레를 샀습니다. 내일은 동아리 친구들과 잠실야구장에 갈 거예요. 한국어로 대화하는 것이 정말 즐겁고, 앞으로 2급에서도 열심히 공부할 것입니다. 감사합니다!` (*Hello? I am called David from Canada. I am studying Korean at the SNU Language Education Center. I usually wake up at 7:30 AM and go to school. Yesterday after class finished, I went to Myeongdong and shopped. I went by subway Line 4 and it took about twenty minutes. I bought one outfit and one pair of shoes. Tomorrow I am going to go to Jamsil Baseball Stadium with club friends. Conversing in Korean is truly enjoyable, and I will continue studying hard in Level 2. Thank you!*)

### Written Milestone Assignment
- **Prompt:** Write a comprehensive 12-sentence final graduation essay summarizing your life in Korea, routine, past memories, favorite places, and future ambitions. (100–140 Korean words)
- [ ] Submit written draft: `안녕하세요? 제 이름은 사라입니다. 저는 미국에서 왔고 서울에 살고 있습니다. 한국에 처음 왔을 때는 한글을 읽을 수 없었습니다. 하지만 지금은 한국어로 인사하고 식당에서 맛있는 음식을 주문할 수 있습니다. 저는 아침마다 학교에 가고 도서관에서 친구들과 열심히 공부합니다. 지난 주말에는 부산 해운대에 다녀왔습니다. KTX 기차로 두 시간 반 걸렸습니다. 바다가 정말 아름다웠고 해산물도 맛있게 먹었습니다. 다음 방학에는 제주도에 꼭 여행 가고 싶습니다. 한국어 공부는 아주 흥미롭고 재미있습니다. 1급을 무사히 마치게 되어 매우 기쁩니다.`

## 1.17 — Level 1 Capstone: `korean-level1-mastery`

**Objective.** Consolidate all 16 units of Level 1 into an end-to-end spoken simulation portfolio and a 12-sentence connected Korean life essay.

### Acceptance criteria

- [ ] **Checkpoint A Passed:** Read Hangul accurately, introduce name/country/job, and ask about objects.
- [ ] **Checkpoint B Passed:** Order drinks and meals, ask prices with Sino-Korean Won numbers, and describe room/family.
- [ ] **Checkpoint C Passed:** Recount past events with 았/었어요, schedule appointments with time/day particles, and state hobbies.
- [ ] **Checkpoint D Passed:** Conduct full exit interview with directions, future plans -(으)ㄹ 거예요, and conversation repair.

## Level 1 question bank

### Concept
- Q: What is the fundamental difference between the topic particle 은/는 and the subject particle 이/가?
  - A: 은/는 sets the topic or contrastive focus of the sentence, whereas 이/가 identifies the specific grammatical subject.
- Q: When do you use Native Korean numbers vs Sino-Korean numbers?
  - A: Native numbers are used for counting items/people and clock hours (시). Sino-Korean numbers are used for money (원), minutes (분), dates (년/월/일), and phone numbers.

### Scenario
- Q: You enter a cafe in Seoul and want two iced Americanos and one slice of cheesecake to go. What do you say?
  - A: "아이스 아메리카노 두 잔하고 치즈케이크 하나 포장해 주세요."

## Level 1 assessment

**Knowledge** — All 16 units of grammar formulas, vocabulary, and phonetic liaison rules.
**Practical** — Spoken simulation completed for Checkpoints A, B, C, and D with zero English fallback.
**Project** — `korean-level1-mastery` verified.

---

# LEVEL 2 — THE GRAMMAR ENGINE I

> **Goal.** Stop memorising sentences and start generating them. By the end you can conjugate any regular verb into any of three tenses, negate four ways, and place every core particle correctly.

## Level dashboard

| Item | Details |
|---|---|
| **Level** | 2 — The Grammar Engine I |
| **Difficulty** | Beginner → elementary |
| **Suggested duration** | 3–4 weeks |
| **Core tools** | Anki, recorder, tutor weekly, *Korean Grammar in Use – Beginning* |
| **Main project** | **`conjugation-engine`** — a verified conjugation system |
| **Final outcome** | 200 verbs conjugated correctly across tense, negation, and modality, with no reference. |

**Prerequisites.** Level 1 complete.

---

## 2.1 — The verb: dictionary form, stem, and classes

**Objective.** See the system behind conjugation.

### Module 2.1.1 — Anatomy

- [ ] Dictionary form ends in **-다**: 가다, 먹다, 하다.
- [ ] The **stem** is the dictionary form minus 다: 가, 먹, 하.
- [ ] All endings attach to the stem. The dictionary form is a citation form, never used in speech except in writing and in the plain-style declarative.

### Module 2.1.2 — Verb classes

| Class | Ending | Behaviour |
|---|---|---|
| Action verbs (동사) | 먹다, 가다 | take tenses, -고 있다, imperatives |
| Descriptive verbs (형용사) | 좋다, 크다, 예쁘다 | conjugate like verbs but do not take imperatives or progressives |
| 이다 | copula | irregular in form, not in meaning |
| 있다/없다 | existence | behave like verbs, but 있다 as "have" takes 이/가 |

> **The trap:** Korean adjectives are verbs. `예뻐요` is a complete sentence — *she is pretty*. There is no "to be" inserted. English speakers add one for months.

> **Module completion gate (2.1).** You classify 30 given verbs into action/descriptive, and write the stem of each.

## 2.2 — The present tense

**Objective.** Conjugate any regular verb, correctly, in under two seconds.

### Module 2.2.1 — The rule

| Stem's last vowel | Ending | Example |
|---|---|---|
| ㅏ or ㅗ | **-아요** | 가다 → 가요 |
| anything else | **-어요** | 먹다 → 먹어요 |
| 하다 verbs | **-해요** | 공부하다 → 공부해요 |

### Module 2.2.2 — Contractions

| Dictionary | Path | Result |
|---|---|---|
| 오다 | 오 + 아요 | 와요 |
| 보다 | 보 + 아요 | 봐요 |
| 마시다 | 마시 + 어요 | 마셔요 |
| 기다리다 | 기다리 + 어요 | 기다려요 |
| 하다 | 하 + 여요 | 해요 |
| 좋아하다 | — | 좋아해요 |

### Module 2.2.3 — The ㅡ and ㄷ families (handled fully in 2.8)

- [ ] 쓰다 → 써요, 크다 → 커요, 바쁘다 → 바빠요.
- [ ] 듣다 → 들어요, 걷다 → 걸어요.

> **Module completion gate (2.2).** Twenty-five verbs conjugated to the polite present in under three minutes, out loud, with 90% accuracy.

## 2.3 — The past tense

**Objective.** One rule, applied to everything.

### Module 2.3.1 — The rule

`stem + 았/었 + 어요`, with the same vowel test as the present.

| Present | Past | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| 가요 | 갔어요 | went |
| 와요 | 왔어요 | came |
| 먹어요 | 먹었어요 | ate |
| 마셔요 | 마셨어요 | drank |
| 해요 | 했어요 | did |
| 공부해요 | 공부했어요 | studied |

### Module 2.3.2 — Past of descriptive verbs

- [ ] `추워요` (it is cold) → `추웠어요` (it was cold).
- [ ] `맛있어요` → `맛있었어요`.
- [ ] Past adjectives are how you describe a completed experience: `정말 재미있었어요` — *it was really fun*.

> **Module completion gate (2.3).** Narrate yesterday in ten connected past-tense sentences, spoken, with no more than two errors.

## 2.4 — Future and intention

**Objective.** Distinguish three futures that English collapses into "will".

### Module 2.4.1 — -(으)ㄹ 거예요 (plan)

- [ ] Consonant stem → -을 거예요; vowel stem → -ㄹ 거예요.
- [ ] 먹다 → 먹을 거예요 · 가다 → 갈 거예요 · 하다 → 할 거예요.
- [ ] Past form: -ㄹ 거였어요 = *was going to*.

### Module 2.4.2 — -겠- (intention, presumption, politeness)

| Use | Example | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| Determination | 제가 하겠습니다 | I will do it |
| Polite offer | 도와드리겠습니다 | let me help |
| Presumption | 비가 오겠어요 | it looks like rain |
| Set phrases | 잘 먹겠습니다 / 잘 먹었습니다 | before / after a meal |

### Module 2.4.3 — -(으)ㄹ게요 (promise to the listener)

- [ ] `제가 할게요` — *I'll do it* (because you asked, or as a promise).
- [ ] It only appears in the first person, and it is about the listener's expectation.

> **The distinction in one line:** `할 거예요` = my plan; `하겠습니다` = my determination, formally; `할게요` = my promise to you.

> **Module completion gate (2.4).** Describe next weekend in eight sentences, using all three futures at least once each, without slipping into the present.

## 2.5 — Negation, four ways

**Objective.** Say no precisely, and distinguish choosing not to from being unable to.

### Module 2.5.1 — The four forms

| Form | Meaning | Example |
|---|---|---|
| 안 + verb | don't (short) | 안 가요 |
| -지 않다 | don't (long) | 가지 않아요 |
| 못 + verb | can't (short) | 못 가요 |
| -지 못하다 | can't (long) | 가지 못해요 |

### Module 2.5.2 — 안 vs 못

| Korean | Meaning |
|---|---|
| 안 먹어요 | I'm not eating (by choice) |
| 못 먹어요 | I can't eat (allergy, no time, unavailable) |

- [ ] This distinction is not optional. Using 안 when you mean 못 sounds like stubbornness.

### Module 2.5.3 — Placement rules

- [ ] 안 and 못 go immediately before the verb, never before the object: `밥을 안 먹어요` ✓, `안 밥을 먹어요` ✗.
- [ ] With 하다 verbs, the 안 goes before 하다: `공부 안 해요`.
- [ ] Past negation is formed on the tense: 안 갔어요, 못 갔어요, 가지 않았어요, 가지 못했어요.

> **Module completion gate (2.5).** Twenty sentences negated both ways, with 안/못 chosen correctly and the placement never wrong.

## 2.6 — Particles I: the core three

**Objective.** 은/는, 이/가, 을/를 — the trio that carries most of the grammar and most of the confusion.

### Module 2.6.1 — 을/를 (object)

- [ ] After a consonant 을, after a vowel 를.
- [ ] Only transitive verbs take it.
- [ ] Memorise verbs with their frame: `밥을 먹다`, not `먹다`.

### Module 2.6.2 — 은/는 (topic)

- [ ] Marks what the sentence is *about*, and often contrasts.
- [ ] `저는 학생이에요` — *as for me, I'm a student*.
- [ ] It can replace 이/가 or 을/를 in the same slot: `밥은 먹어요` — *as for rice, I do eat it*.

### Module 2.6.3 — 이/가 (subject, new information)

- [ ] Marks the doer or the bearer of a state, and signals *this is the answer to the question*.
- [ ] `누가 왔어요?` → `민수 씨가 왔어요.` (not 는)
- [ ] `비가 와요` — new information, no contrast.

### Module 2.6.4 — The honest simplification

| Situation | Use |
|---|---|
| introducing a new topic | 은/는 |
| answering "who/what did X?" | 이/가 |
| contrast or comparison | 은/는 |
| neutral description with no contrast | 이/가 |
| after 있어요/없어요 | 이/가 |

> **Do not try to master this at L2.** Learn the five situations above, and let the rest come from exposure between L2 and L7. Fluency in 은/는 vs 이/가 is a late-stage skill, and no amount of rule-reading replaces hearing 1,000 examples.

> **Module completion gate (2.6).** You choose the correct particle in 20 out of 25 given sentences and can explain the choice in the five-situation table.

## 2.7 — Particles II: place, direction, person

**Objective.** The particles that answer *where*, *to whom*, and *by what means*.

### Module 2.7.1 — The table

| Particle | Function | Example |
|---|---|---|
| 에 | time, destination, existence | 세 시에 · 학교에 가요 · 집에 있어요 |
| 에서 | location of an action, source | 학교에서 공부해요 · 인도에서 왔어요 |
| 에게 / 한테 | to a person | 친구에게 말했어요 |
| 에게서 / 한테서 | from a person | 친구에게서 받았어요 |
| 께 | to/from a person (honorific) | 선생님께 드렸어요 |
| (으)로 | direction, means | 집으로 가요 · 버스로 가요 |
| 와/과, 하고, (이)랑 | and, with | 친구와 같이 · 친구하고 |
| 도 | also | 저도 가요 |
| 만 | only | 물만 마셔요 |
| 밖에 + negative | only | 물밖에 없어요 |
| 부터 / 까지 | from / until | 아침부터 밤까지 |
| 마다 | every | 주말마다 |
| 보다 | than | 나보다 커요 |
| 처럼 | like | 친구처럼 |

### Module 2.7.2 — The three that get confused

- [ ] **에 vs 에서.** Movement toward or existence at a place → 에. An action performed there → 에서.
- [ ] **만 vs 밖에.** Both mean "only", but 밖에 always requires a negative verb: `물밖에 없어요`.
- [ ] **하고 vs 와/과.** Same meaning; 하고 is spoken and can be used with any noun, 와/과 is written and more formal.

> **Module completion gate (2.7).** 25 sentences with particles blanked out, 22+ correct, with the reason stated for each.

## 2.8 — Irregular verbs: the six families

**Objective.** Own the irregularities. They are rule-governed, there are only six, and avoiding them caps your accuracy permanently.

### Module 2.8.1 — ㅂ irregular

| Dictionary | Conjugated | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| 춥다 | 추워요 | cold |
| 덥다 | 더워요 | hot |
| 쉽다 | 쉬워요 | easy |
| 어렵다 | 어려워요 | difficult |
| 가깝다 | 가까워요 | close |
| 맵다 | 매워요 | spicy |
| 아름답다 | 아름다워요 | beautiful |
| 무겁다 | 무거워요 | heavy |

- [ ] **Exceptions (regular):** 입다 → 입어요, 잡다 → 잡아요, 좁다 → 좁아요, 씹다 → 씹어요.

### Module 2.8.2 — ㄷ irregular

| Dictionary | Conjugated | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| 듣다 | 들어요 | listen |
| 걷다 | 걸어요 | walk |
| 묻다 | 물어요 | ask |
| 싣다 | 실어요 | load |

- [ ] **Exceptions:** 받다 → 받아요, 닫다 → 닫아요, 믿다 → 믿어요.

### Module 2.8.3 — ㅅ irregular

| Dictionary | Conjugated | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| 낫다 | 나아요 | better |
| 붓다 | 부어요 | swell, pour |
| 잇다 | 이어요 | connect |
| 젓다 | 저어요 | stir |

- [ ] **Exceptions:** 웃다 → 웃어요, 벗다 → 벗어요.

### Module 2.8.4 — 르 irregular

| Dictionary | Conjugated | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| 모르다 | 몰라요 | not know |
| 빠르다 | 빨라요 | fast |
| 부르다 | 불러요 | call, sing |
| 다르다 | 달라요 | different |
| 고르다 | 골라요 | choose |
| 흐르다 | 흘러요 | flow |

- [ ] **Exception:** 따르다 → 따라요.

### Module 2.8.5 — 으 irregular

| Dictionary | Conjugated | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| 쓰다 | 써요 | write, use |
| 크다 | 커요 | big |
| 바쁘다 | 바빠요 | busy |
| 예쁘다 | 예뻐요 | pretty |
| 슬프다 | 슬퍼요 | sad |
| 기쁘다 | 기뻐요 | happy |
| 아프다 | 아파요 | sick |

- [ ] Rule: if the preceding syllable has ㅏ/ㅗ, take -아요; otherwise -어요.

### Module 2.8.6 — ㅎ irregular

| Dictionary | Conjugated | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| 그렇다 | 그래요 | to be so |
| 이렇다 | 이래요 | to be like this |
| 저렇다 | 저래요 | to be like that |
| 어떻다 | 어때요 | how is it |
| 빨갛다 | 빨개요 | red |
| 파랗다 | 파래요 | blue |

### Module 2.8.7 — The ㄹ special case

- [ ] ㄹ drops before ㄴ, ㅂ, ㅅ endings: 살다 → 사세요 (not 살으세요), 알다 → 아는 (not 알는), 만들다 → 만드세요.

> **Module completion gate (2.8).** Thirty irregular verbs conjugated across present, past, and future at 90% accuracy, with the ㅂ and ㄷ exceptions named from memory.

## 2.9 — Modality: can, must, may

**Objective.** The four-way grid that makes you sound capable.

### Module 2.9.1 — Ability

- [ ] `-(으)ㄹ 수 있다` — there is a way: 할 수 있어요.
- [ ] `-(으)ㄹ 수 없다` — there is no way.
- [ ] `못` — I personally cannot, right now.

### Module 2.9.2 — Obligation and permission

| Meaning | Form | Example |
|---|---|---|
| must | -아/어야 하다 / 되다 | 가야 해요 |
| must not | -(으)면 안 되다 | 가면 안 돼요 |
| don't have to | -지 않아도 되다 | 안 가도 돼요 |
| may | -아/어도 되다 | 가도 돼요 |

- [ ] Produce all four from any verb without hesitation. This grid is one of the highest-frequency things an intermediate speaker does.

> **Module completion gate (2.9).** The four-way grid produced for ten verbs, spoken, in under three minutes.

## 2.10 — Honorifics, first pass

**Objective.** Recognise and use the honorific system at the level daily life requires.

### Module 2.10.1 — Subject honorific -시-

- [ ] Add -시- to the stem when the *subject* is respected: 가다 → 가세요, 하다 → 하세요, 먹다 → 드세요.
- [ ] `선생님이 오세요` — the teacher comes. `친구가 와요` — a friend comes.

### Module 2.10.2 — Honorific vocabulary

| Plain | Honorific | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| 먹다 | 드시다 | eat |
| 있다 | 계시다 | exist, be present |
| 자다 | 주무시다 | sleep |
| 말하다 | 말씀하시다 | speak |
| 주다 | 드리다 | give (to a superior) |
| 묻다 | 여쭙다 | ask (to a superior) |
| 보다 | 뵙다 | meet (a superior) |
| 이름 | 성함 | name |
| 나이 | 연세 | age |
| 집 | 댁 | house |

### Module 2.10.3 — The particle changes

- [ ] 이/가 → **께서**: `선생님께서 오세요`.
- [ ] 에게 → **께**: `선생님께 드렸어요`.

> **Module completion gate (2.10).** You convert ten plain sentences into honorific ones, changing the verb, the particle, and the vocabulary where required.

## 2.11 — Level 2 Capstone: `conjugation-engine`

**Objective.** A verified system, not a list.

### A. Functional requirements

1. **The verb bank.** 200 verbs, each with stem, class, and present/past/future forms.
2. **The irregular register.** All six families, plus the exception lists, with 6 examples each.
3. **The particle map.** 14 particles with two examples each, written and recorded.
4. **The modality grid.** 10 verbs × 4 forms (must / must not / don't have to / may).
5. **The conjugation test.** 60 random verbs × 3 tenses, written, timed, scored.
6. **The production log.** 100 original sentences using the level's patterns, each recorded.
7. **The error ledger.** Every conjugation error, with the family it belongs to.

### B. Suggested architecture

```
conjugation-engine/
├── verbs/
│   └── bank.csv             ← 200 rows: verb | stem | class | present | past | future
├── irregulars/
│   ├── ㅂ.md  ㄷ.md  ㅅ.md  르.md  으.md  ㅎ.md
│   └── exceptions.md
├── particles/
│   ├── map.md               ← 14 particles, 2 examples each
│   └── recording.mp3
├── modality/
│   └── grid.md              ← 10 verbs × 4 forms
├── tests/
│   ├── random_60.md         ← the test, timed
│   └── scores.md            ← date | score | error families
├── production/
│   └── 100_sentences.md     ← Korean | gloss | the pattern used
└── errors/
    └── ledger.md
```

### C. Acceptance targets

| Requirement | Target |
|---|---|
| Verb bank | 200 verbs, all forms correct |
| Conjugation test | 85%+ on 60 random verbs × 3 tenses, under 20 minutes |
| Irregulars | 90%+ across all six families |
| Modality grid | 10/10 complete and spoken fluently |
| Particles | 22/25 on a blanked-out test |
| Production | 100 sentences, ≤ 10 requiring correction |
| Error taxonomy | every error attributed to a family, not just "wrong" |

### D. Stretch goals

- Add a column for honorific forms.
- Add negative forms to the bank.
- Beat 95% on the conjugation test.

### E. Acceptance criteria table

| Row | Criterion | Weight | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| Bank complete | 200 verbs | required | `verbs/bank.csv` |
| Conjugation accuracy | ≥ 85% | required | `tests/scores.md` |
| Irregular accuracy | ≥ 90% | required | `tests/scores.md` |
| Modality spoken | 10/10 in 3 min | required | recording |
| Production quality | ≤ 10 corrections in 100 | required | `production/` |
| Error attribution | 100% of errors classified | required | `errors/ledger.md` |

**Rule:** an error you cannot classify is an error you will make again. The ledger's taxonomy is the point of the capstone.

---

## Level 2 question bank

### Group A — Conjugation

1. Conjugate 먹다, 가다, 하다, 살다, 듣다 across present, past, future.
2. Why does 오다 become 와요 and not 오아요?
3. What are the ㅂ-irregular exceptions, and why are they regular?
4. Conjugate 예쁘다 and explain why the vowel choice differs from 크다.

### Group B — Particles

5. Choose: 저__ 학생이에요 (는/가).
6. Choose: 누가 왔어요? 민수 씨__ 왔어요 (는/가).
7. Choose: 학교__ 공부해요 (에/에서).
8. Choose: 물__ 없어요 (만/밖에).
9. Explain why 밖에 requires a negative.

### Group C — Negation and modality

10. Say "I don't eat meat" and "I can't eat meat" — and explain the difference.
11. Where does 안 go in a 하다 verb?
12. Produce all four of: must / must not / don't have to / may, for 가다.

### Group D — Honorifics

13. Convert: 선생님이 밥을 먹어요 → honorific.
14. What changes in the particle when the subject is honoured?
15. Give three honorific vocabulary pairs.

### Group E — Production

16. Describe your yesterday in ten sentences.
17. Describe next weekend in eight.
18. Say five things you have to do this week, and five you don't.
19. Ask permission for three things, and refuse two of them politely.
20. Say something you can do that you could not do a year ago.

### Readiness standard

| Group | Requirement |
|---|---|
| A | 4/4 with the rule stated |
| B | 4/5 with the reason |
| C | all three correct, 안/못 explained |
| D | all three correct |
| E | 5/5 spoken, ≤ 3 errors total |

---

## Level 2 assessment

**Knowledge** — conjugation system, six irregular families, particle functions, modality grid, honorific basics.
**Practical** — 85% conjugation test, 100 sentences produced, 10/10 modality spoken.
**Project** — `conjugation-engine` complete with the error taxonomy.

> **Level 2 completion rule.** You must be able to conjugate a *new* verb you have never seen, correctly, from first principles. If you pass the test by memory of the 200 verbs in the bank, you have learned a list, not a system.

---

# LEVEL 3 — THE GRAMMAR ENGINE II

> **Goal.** Connect clauses into discourse, handle all the irregulars fluently, use honorifics in the full system, and tell a three-minute story about your own life that a native speaker would call fluent.

## Level dashboard

| Item | Details |
|---|---|
| **Level** | 3 — The Grammar Engine II |
| **Difficulty** | Elementary → lower intermediate |
| **Suggested duration** | 3–4 weeks |
| **Core tools** | Anki, recorder, weekly tutor, graded readers from L3 onward |
| **Main project** | **`story-engine`** — a three-minute spoken narrative |
| **Final outcome** | Clause linking, all six irregulars automatic, honorifics in full use, and a spoken narrative that holds together. |

**Prerequisites.** Level 2 complete.

---

## 3.1 — Clause linking I: and, but, so

**Objective.** Stop producing sentences one at a time.

### Module 3.1.1 — -고 (and, and then)

- [ ] Attaches to the stem with no tense: 먹**고**, 가**고**, 하**고**.
- [ ] Tense appears only on the final verb: `밥을 먹고 학교에 갔어요`.
- [ ] It sequences, it does not cause. `비가 오고 추워요` — it rains and (also) it's cold.

### Module 3.1.2 — -지만 (but)

- [ ] `비싸지만 좋아요` — it's expensive but I like it.
- [ ] Can attach to past stems: `갔지만`.

### Module 3.1.3 — -는데 (background, contrast, softening)

- [ ] The most versatile connector in the language, and the last one learners acquire.
- [ ] Background: `지금 바쁜데요…` — *I'm busy right now (so…)*
- [ ] Contrast: `한국어는 어려운데 재미있어요`.
- [ ] Softening a request: `죄송한데요, 다시 말씀해 주시겠어요?`
- [ ] Conjugation: verb stem + 는데; descriptive stem + -(으)ㄴ데; past + -았/었는데.

> **Module completion gate (3.1).** Twenty linked sentences using -고, -지만, -는데 correctly, with the tense only on the final verb.

## 3.2 — Reason and cause: the three-way distinction

**Objective.** The distinction that separates intermediate speakers from phrasebook speakers.

### Module 3.2.1 — The comparison

| Pattern | Nuance | Restriction |
|---|---|---|
| -아/어서 | neutral reason, sequential cause | **cannot** be followed by an imperative or a proposal |
| -(으)니까 | reason the listener should accept; discovery | can be followed by imperatives and suggestions |
| -기 때문에 | formal, emphatic reason | any ending, but stiff |

### Module 3.2.2 — The rule people get wrong

- [ ] `비가 와서 못 갔어요` ✓ (statement)
- [ ] `비가 오니까 우산을 가져가세요` ✓ (imperative — only 니까 works)
- [ ] `비가 와서 우산을 가져가세요` ✗ (grammatically wrong)

- [ ] Second use of -니까: discovery. `가 보니까 사람이 없었어요` — *I went and found nobody there.*

> **Module completion gate (3.2).** You choose correctly between 서 and 니까 in 15 sentences, and you can state the imperative restriction without notes.

## 3.3 — Purpose, intention, and attempt

**Objective.** Say why you went somewhere and what you intend to do.

### Module 3.3.1 — The purpose patterns

| Pattern | Meaning | Example |
|---|---|---|
| -(으)러 가다/오다 | go/come in order to | 밥을 먹으러 가요 |
| -(으)려고 하다 | intend to | 내일 가려고 해요 |
| -(으)려고요 | casual intention | 지금 가려고요 |
| -기 위해(서) | in order to (formal, written) | 성공하기 위해서 노력해요 |

### Module 3.3.2 — Attempt and completion

- [ ] -아/어 보다 — try: 먹어 봤어요 (I've tried it).
- [ ] -고 싶다 — want: 가고 싶어요.
- [ ] -고 싶어하다 — third person: 그 사람은 가고 싶어해요. You cannot use 싶다 about someone else's inner state.

> **Module completion gate (3.3).** Ten purpose sentences, ten intention sentences, and the third-person rule produced correctly.

## 3.4 — Time and condition

**Objective.** Build the time clauses that make a narrative possible.

### Module 3.4.1 — The table

| Pattern | Meaning | Example |
|---|---|---|
| -(으)면 | if, when (conditional) | 비가 오면 안 가요 |
| -(으)ㄹ 때 | when (at the time of) | 어릴 때 |
| -았/었을 때 | when (past) | 학생이었을 때 |
| -자마자 | as soon as | 도착하자마자 전화했어요 |
| -는 동안 | while, during | 공부하는 동안 |
| -기 전에 | before | 가기 전에 |
| -(으)ㄴ 후에 | after | 먹은 후에 |
| -(으)ㄹ 때까지 | until | 끝날 때까지 |

### Module 3.4.2 — The tense trap

- [ ] Time clauses take tense only where the time reference differs from the main clause: `비가 왔을 때 집에 있었어요` (it rained, I was home — both past).
- [ ] `-면` takes no tense at all: 비가 오면, never 비가 왔으면 (that would be counterfactual, a different pattern at L10).

> **Module completion gate (3.4).** A six-sentence narrative using at least five different time patterns, with no tense error in the subordinate clause.

## 3.5 — Nominalisation

**Objective.** Turn verbs into nouns so they can be subjects, objects, and topics. This unlocks a large fraction of intermediate grammar.

### Module 3.5.1 — The three forms

| Form | Use | Example |
|---|---|---|
| -는 것 | the general, spoken form | 공부하는 것이 재미있어요 |
| -기 | often with certain verbs (시작하다, 좋아하다, 어렵다) | 공부하기 어려워요 |
| -(으)ㅁ | formal, written, and in some fixed patterns | 사실임을 알았어요 |

### Module 3.5.2 — The fixed patterns this unlocks

- [ ] -는 것이 좋겠어요 — you'd better
- [ ] -기로 하다 — decide to
- [ ] -기 시작하다 — begin to
- [ ] -기 때문에 — because (formal)
- [ ] -(으)ㅁ에도 불구하고 — despite

> **Module completion gate (3.5).** You convert ten sentences into nominalised forms and use three fixed patterns correctly.

## 3.6 — Noun modifiers: the adjectival engine

**Objective.** Put a whole clause in front of a noun. This is where Korean becomes compact and where English speakers slow down.

### Module 3.6.1 — The four modifiers

| Tense | Ending | Example |
|---|---|---|
| Present (verb) | -는 | 먹는 사람 |
| Present (adjective) | -(으)ㄴ | 예쁜 사람 |
| Past | -(으)ㄴ | 먹은 사람 |
| Future / supposition | -(으)ㄹ | 먹을 사람 |

- [ ] Note the collision: `-(으)ㄴ` is past for verbs and present for adjectives.
- [ ] `-던` = used to, or was in the middle of: `내가 살던 집` — the house I used to live in.

### Module 3.6.2 — The long modifier

- [ ] `어제 만난 사람이 선생님이에요` — the person I met yesterday is a teacher.
- [ ] Korean puts the whole relative clause before the noun, with no "who/which". Long modifiers are the main reason reading comprehension slows at intermediate level.

> **Module completion gate (3.6).** Fifteen noun phrases built from clauses, then used in full sentences.

## 3.7 — Honorifics in full

**Objective.** Operate the whole system, not just the -시- suffix.

### Module 3.7.1 — The three axes

| Axis | Mechanism | Example |
|---|---|---|
| Subject honour | -시-, 께서, honorific vocabulary | 선생님께서 드세요 |
| Object honour | special verbs (드리다, 뵙다, 여쭙다) | 선생님께 여쭀어요 |
| Hearer honour | the register (합니다 / 해요 / 해) | — |

- [ ] All three operate at once. A sentence can be simultaneously subject-honorific, object-honorific, and hearer-polite.
- [ ] The classic error: over-honorifying yourself. `저께서` is wrong — you never honour your own actions with 께서 in the same way.

### Module 3.7.2 — The humble verbs

| Plain | Humble | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| 주다 | 드리다 | give |
| 보다 | 뵙다 | meet |
| 묻다 | 여쭙다 | ask |
| 말하다 | 말씀드리다 | say (to a superior) |
| 데려가다 | 모시고 가다 | take someone along |

> **Module completion gate (3.7).** Ten sentences converted into full honorific form, using all three axes, with no over-honorification of the self.

## 3.8 — Comparison

**Objective.** Express degree, preference, and similarity.

### Module 3.8.1 — The comparison set

| Pattern | Meaning | Example |
|---|---|---|
| A가 B보다 | A is more than B | 한국어가 영어보다 어려워요 |
| 더 / 덜 | more / less | 더 커요 |
| 가장 / 제일 | most | 제일 좋아요 |
| 만큼 | as much as | 생각만큼 어렵지 않아요 |
| 처럼 / 같이 | like | 친구처럼 |
| -보다 나은 | better than | — |
| 별로 | not particularly | 별로 안 좋아요 |

> **Module completion gate (3.8).** Ten comparisons using five different patterns, spoken, with no English word order bleeding in.

## 3.9 — Discourse: making it hold together

**Objective.** The difference between correct sentences and a coherent answer.

### Module 3.9.1 — The connectors of speech

| Marker | Function |
|---|---|
| 근데 / 그런데 | but, by the way — the most common turn in speech |
| 그래서 | so |
| 그러니까 | so, I mean |
| 그리고 | and |
| 근데요 | softening opener |
| 사실은 | actually |
| 어쨌든 | anyway |
| 아무튼 | in any case |
| 즉 | that is |
| 예를 들어 | for example |

### Module 3.9.2 — Fillers that buy time

- [ ] 저기… · 음… · 뭐라고 할까… · 그러니까… · 약간…
- [ ] Using fillers deliberately is a fluency marker, not a weakness. Silent pauses read as failure; filled pauses read as thinking.

> **Module completion gate (3.9).** A 90-second monologue using at least six discourse markers naturally, with no dead air longer than 2 seconds.

## 3.10 — Level 3 Capstone: `story-engine`

**Objective.** A three-minute spoken narrative that a native speaker would call fluent.

### A. Functional requirements

1. **The story.** A true story from your life, 3 minutes, told without notes, recorded on video.
2. **The transcript.** Written out in Hangeul, with a gloss and a grammar annotation for every clause connector.
3. **The structure.** A clear beginning, three developments, and an ending — marked explicitly in the transcript.
4. **The connector inventory.** Every linking pattern used, with a count, targeting at least ten distinct patterns.
5. **The tense audit.** All three tenses used correctly; the narrative must have a past main line and a present reflection.
6. **The revision log.** Three recorded takes with the improvements listed between them.
7. **The comprehension check.** A native speaker or advanced learner listens once and retells the story — the retelling must be accurate.

### B. Suggested architecture

```
story-engine/
├── story/
│   ├── transcript.md        ← Hangeul | gloss | connector annotation
│   ├── structure.md         ← opening / development 1-3 / ending
│   └── take_1.mp4  take_2.mp4  take_final.mp4
├── connectors/
│   └── inventory.md         ← pattern | count | example from the story
├── tense_audit.md
├── revision_log.md          ← take N | what changed | why
└── comprehension/
    └── listener_retelling.md
```

### C. Acceptance targets

| Dimension | Target |
|---|---|
| Length | 2:30–3:30 |
| Connectors | ≥ 10 distinct patterns, used correctly |
| Tense accuracy | ≥ 95% |
| Honorific accuracy | no errors in the framed opening and closing |
| Fluency | ≤ 5 hesitations over 1 second; no dead air over 2 seconds |
| Comprehension | listener retells the story accurately |
| Structure | beginning, development, end all clearly marked |

### D. Stretch goals

- Tell the same story to two different listeners and adapt the register for each.
- Add reported speech (a preview of L8): *"she said that…"*
- Tell a story in the future tense — describing what will happen.

### E. Acceptance criteria table

| Row | Criterion | Weight | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| Narrative length | 2:30–3:30 | required | `take_final.mp4` |
| Connector range | ≥ 10 distinct | required | `connectors/inventory.md` |
| Tense accuracy | ≥ 95% | required | `tense_audit.md` |
| Comprehension | accurate retelling | required | `listener_retelling.md` |
| Revision evidenced | 3 takes with changes | required | `revision_log.md` |

**Rule:** the listener retelling is the gate. If a native speaker cannot retell your story, the grammar may be correct but the discourse is not.

---

## Level 3 question bank

### Group A — Connectors

1. Give a sentence where -아/어서 is wrong and -(으)니까 is required, and explain why.
2. What are the three functions of -는데?
3. Why does -고 carry no tense?
4. Distinguish -자마자 and -는 동안 in one sentence each.

### Group B — Modifiers

5. Build a noun phrase from: 어제 친구를 만났다 + 사람.
6. Why can -(으)ㄴ be both past and present?
7. What does -던 add that -(으)ㄴ does not?

### Group C — Honorifics

8. Convert 선생님이 밥을 먹고 집에 갔어요 into full honorific form.
9. What is over-honorification, and give an example of the error.
10. Name four humble verbs and their plain equivalents.

### Group D — Nominalisation

11. Convert 공부하다 into three nominalised forms and use each in a sentence.
12. What does -기로 하다 mean?

### Group E — Production

13. Tell a two-minute story with at least eight connectors.
14. Describe a person you know using three noun modifiers.
15. Explain, in Korean, why you are learning Korean — using -아/어서 and -지만.
16. Compare two cities using 보다, 만큼, and 제일.
17. Say what you do before work and after work, using -기 전에 and -(으)ㄴ 후에.

### Readiness standard

| Group | Requirement |
|---|---|
| A | 4/4 with the restriction stated |
| B | 3/3 |
| C | 3/3, no over-honorification |
| D | 2/2 |
| E | 5/5 spoken, ≤ 5 errors total |

---

## Level 3 assessment

**Knowledge** — connector semantics, nominalisation, noun modifiers, the three honorific axes.
**Practical** — 3-minute narrative, 10+ connectors, 95% tense accuracy, listener comprehension.
**Project** — `story-engine` complete.

> **Level 3 completion rule.** A native speaker listens once and can retell your story accurately. That is the only test that matters at this level.

---

# PHASE 1 CAPSTONE — THE FIRST HOUR

> **The brief.** Spend sixty minutes entirely in Korean. No English, no dictionary app, no switching. One conversation, one topic, one hour.

## A. Functional requirements

1. **Duration.** 55–65 minutes, continuous, with a tutor, an exchange partner, or a native speaker.
2. **No English.** Zero English words, zero code-switching. If you cannot say something, you must circumlocute — describe it another way.
3. **Topic coverage.** At least four of: your work or study, your family, your city, a trip you took, a film or book you liked, a plan for next year.
4. **Grammar coverage.** At least one instance each of: past, present, future, negation (both 안 and 못), a connector, a noun modifier, an honorific sentence.
5. **Repair.** At least three instances of asking for clarification *in Korean*, and at least one of correcting yourself mid-sentence.
6. **The recording and the transcript.** Full audio, plus a 15-line transcript of the most and least successful exchanges.
7. **The retrospective.** Written, in Korean if possible, answering: what did I avoid saying, and what structure would have let me say it?

## B. Suggested architecture

```
first-hour/
├── recording.mp3
├── topic_map.md            ← the four topics, in order, with what you wanted to say vs said
├── grammar_checklist.md    ← each required pattern, with the timestamp where you used it
├── repair_log.md           ← three clarifications + one self-correction
├── transcript.md           ← 15 lines: best and worst exchanges
└── retrospective.md        ← what I avoided, and the structure I needed
```

## C. Acceptance targets

| Dimension | Target |
|---|---|
| Duration | 55+ minutes continuous |
| English used | zero |
| Topics | 4+ distinct |
| Grammar coverage | all seven required patterns evidenced |
| Comprehension | you understood ≥ 85% of what the partner said |
| Repair | 3+ clarifications, 1+ self-correction |
| Avoidance | ≤ 3 topics abandoned for lack of vocabulary |

## D. Stretch goals

- Do it on the phone, with no visual cues.
- Do it with two people at once.
- Do it on a topic you have never discussed in any language.

## E. Acceptance criteria table

| Row | Requirement | Pass condition |
|---|---|---|
| Stamina | 55+ min | continuous, single session |
| Purity | no English | verified from the recording |
| Range | 4 topics | `topic_map.md` |
| Grammar | 7 patterns | `grammar_checklist.md` with timestamps |
| Repair | 3 + 1 | `repair_log.md` |
| Reflection | specific | `retrospective.md` names structures, not feelings |

---

# PHASE 1 FINAL ASSESSMENT

**Knowledge (25%)**

- [ ] The seven sound-change rules, named and applied.
- [ ] The conjugation system, including all six irregular families and their exceptions.
- [ ] The particle system: 14 particles with two examples each.
- [ ] The three honorific axes.
- [ ] The 서 vs 니까 restriction, from memory.

**Practical skills (45%)**

- [ ] Read 200 syllables of unseen text at 60+ syl/min at 98% rule accuracy.
- [ ] 95% dictation on a 2-minute everyday-speed clip.
- [ ] Conjugate 60 random verbs × 3 tenses at 85%+ in 20 minutes.
- [ ] Produce 200 words of your own on a familiar topic, written, with ≤ 8 errors.
- [ ] Hold a 10-minute unprepared conversation at ≥ 80% comprehension.

**Project (30%)**

- [ ] All four level capstones complete.
- [ ] *The First Hour* recorded and reviewed.
- [ ] The error ledger has ≥ 100 entries, each classified.

**Pass:** 70% overall, no section below 60%. Below 60% on any section → repeat that section's level.

---

# APPENDIX

## A. Sound-change quick reference

| Rule | Trigger | Result | Example |
|---|---|---|---|
| Liaison | batchim + ㅇ onset | final moves forward | 한국어 → [한구거] |
| Aspiration | ㄱ/ㄷ/ㅂ/ㅈ + ㅎ | ㅋ/ㅌ/ㅍ/ㅊ | 좋다 → [조타] |
| Tensification | stop batchim + ㄱ/ㄷ/ㅂ/ㅅ/ㅈ | doubled | 학교 → [학꾜] |
| Nasalisation | stop batchim + ㄴ/ㅁ | ㅇ/ㄴ/ㅁ | 국물 → [궁물] |
| Liquidisation | ㄴ + ㄹ, ㄹ + ㄴ | ㄹㄹ | 신라 → [실라] |
| Palatalisation | ㄷ/ㅌ + 이 | 지/치 | 같이 → [가치] |
| ㅎ deletion | ㅎ + vowel, ㅎ + ㄴ/ㅁ | ㅎ drops | 괜찮아요 → [괜차나요] |

## B. Conjugation quick reference

| Tense / form | Rule | 먹다 | 가다 | 하다 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Present polite | -아/어요 | 먹어요 | 가요 | 해요 |
| Past polite | -았/었어요 | 먹었어요 | 갔어요 | 했어요 |
| Future | -(으)ㄹ 거예요 | 먹을 거예요 | 갈 거예요 | 할 거예요 |
| Formal present | -ㅂ니다 | 먹습니다 | 갑니다 | 합니다 |
| Formal past | -았/었습니다 | 먹었습니다 | 갔습니다 | 했습니다 |
| Negation (short) | 안 + V | 안 먹어요 | 안 가요 | 안 해요 |
| Negation (long) | -지 않다 | 먹지 않아요 | 가지 않아요 | 하지 않아요 |
| Cannot | 못 + V | 못 먹어요 | 못 가요 | 못 해요 |
| Ability | -(으)ㄹ 수 있다 | 먹을 수 있어요 | 갈 수 있어요 | 할 수 있어요 |
| Must | -아/어야 하다 | 먹어야 해요 | 가야 해요 | 해야 해요 |
| May | -아/어도 되다 | 먹어도 돼요 | 가도 돼요 | 해도 돼요 |
| Progressive | -고 있다 | 먹고 있어요 | 가고 있어요 | 하고 있어요 |
| Honorific | -(으)시- | 드세요 | 가세요 | 하세요 |

## C. The 500-word core

Rather than a list to read, the core is a **deck specification**. Build it yourself, because building it is half the learning.

| Theme | Words | Where introduced |
|---|---|---|
| People and family | 40 | L1, L3 |
| Body and health | 30 | L1 |
| Food and drink | 45 | L1 |
| Home and objects | 40 | L1, L4 |
| Places and transport | 45 | L1 |
| Time and dates | 40 | L1 |
| Numbers and counters | 40 | L1 |
| Actions (core verbs) | 120 | L2 |
| Qualities (adjectives) | 60 | L2 |
| Abstract and function words | 40 | L3 |

**The rule for card design:** every card has a **sentence**, not a word. Front: the English situation. Back: the Korean sentence, read aloud by you. A word in isolation is not learned; a sentence is.

## D. Resources

| Resource | Use |
|---|---|
| *Korean Grammar in Use – Beginning* | the grammar spine for L1–L3 |
| TTMIK (Talk To Me In Korean) Levels 1–3 | audio-first parallel spine |
| Naver Dictionary | every word, with audio |
| Anki + a shared Korean deck (filtered) | spaced repetition |
| iTalki or a language exchange app | the tutor, from L2 |
| TOPIK I past papers | from L3, for format familiarity |
| A drama or vlog you actually enjoy | listening material you will not abandon |

## E. The ten most common ways to fail this phase

| # | Failure | Signal | Fix |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Romanisation dependency | you read *gwaenchanayo*, not 괜찮아요 | ban it from L1 |
| 2 | Silent study | you read a lot, speak never | slot 4 is non-negotiable |
| 3 | Grammar collecting | 40 patterns recognised, 4 produced | ten sentences per pattern |
| 4 | Skipping the irregulars | ㅂ/ㄷ verbs avoided in speech | the 90%-accuracy gate exists for this |
| 5 | 한글 only, no listening | reads well, understands nothing spoken | three-pass dictation daily |
| 6 | Over-honorifying | 저께서, 저는 드세요 | learn the three axes properly |
| 7 | No tutor | errors become habits | one session per week from L2 |
| 8 | Studying vocab as lists | 800 words, cannot form a sentence | sentence cards only |
| 9 | Perfectionism | refuses to speak until "ready" | speak at T1, improve in public |
| 10 | Skipping the capstones | no artefacts, no evidence | the artefact *is* the level |

## What comes next

**Phase 2 — Everyday Fluency · Levels 4–6**

| Level | Title | What it adds |
|---|---|---|
| **L4** | Daily Life Domains | Housing, banking, healthcare, bureaucracy, phone calls — the vocabulary of existing in Korea |
| **L5** | Listening and Pronunciation Mastery | Natural-speed comprehension, contraction rules, native rhythm |
| **L6** | Reading and Writing Foundations | News, short fiction, diary, formal messages, spacing rules |

Phase 2 assumes Phase 1 completely: you will listen to natives who speak at a speed your grammar cannot yet predict, and you will read texts whose vocabulary you do not have. The foundation is what lets you survive that.

*Phase 1 of 7 · Levels 0–3 of 18 · Foundations*
